# Decisions.md — Attest

Every decision the spec left open, or that changed a locked decision, goes here. Format: date, decision, reason, Hard Rule it protects (if any), who/what triggered it. Newest at the bottom of each section. Never delete an entry; supersede it.

## Locked by build_spec.md (2026-10-01)

- Product: local-first Chrome extension that verifies agent claims against the user's own Gmail/Calendar tabs; never acts; nothing leaves the browser. Reason: trust gate (users burned by Muse will not grant server-side Gmail access) and Google restricted-scope gate (CASA assessment) both cleared by the in-browser shape. Protects HR1, HR3.
- Name: **Attest**. Reason: founder's choice from Receipt / Attest / Vouch.
- Platform: Chrome MV3 via WXT; Chromium browsers for free; Firefox/Safari deferred. Reason: single build, store reach.
- Language/typing: TypeScript 5 strict + `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` + `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`; no `any` in `src/core`.
- UI: Preact + Tailwind; no router or state library (the ledger is the state).
- Storage: Dexie over IndexedDB, versioned migrations; `chrome.storage.local` only for flags + install id.
- Schemas: Zod at every boundary. Reason: HR2, HR4 (parse, don't trust).
- Tests: Vitest + fast-check; Playwright against locally served fixture pages; two `@mandatory` tests (network intercept, secret seed) from Sprint 0.
- Lint: ESLint strict + custom `attest/no-act` + `no-restricted-imports` fencing `src/core`. Protects HR3, HR4.
- Package manager: pnpm; Node 22 LTS.
- Adapters: fixture-driven, versioned `selectors.json`, `health()` before `extract()`, read-only. Protects HR3, HR6.
- Grading: pure `grade()`; policy as data in `policy/default.json`; first-match-wins rules; terminal `default_unverifiable`. Protects HR4, HR5.
- Optional model: Chrome built-in on-device first, BYO key second, both off by default, advisory only with capped weight and a monotonicity property test. Protects HR4.
- Telemetry: opt-in, off by default, counts/booleans/enums + random install id only, one host, FastAPI + Pydantic (`extra='forbid'`) + motor on Railway. Protects HR1, HR10.
- Encryption at rest: optional passphrase, AES-GCM, PBKDF2 ≥ 600k iterations, hashes plaintext for indexing. Off by default.
- v1 adapters: Muse (web), ChatGPT (agent mode) with Dots behind `ATTEST_DOTS=1` until a fixture exists, Claude.ai. Instinct and Perplexity Computer deferred to v2.
- v1 observers: Gmail web (Sent + opened receipts), Google Calendar web. No Gmail API, no Plaid, no Outlook (v2).
- Default Grants: permissive (all types, no ceiling, no allowlist) so first run produces receipts; user tightens in options.
- Retention: 90 days default (30/90/180 selectable).
- License: Apache-2.0. Repo: `github.com/Poojan6216/attest`, public from first commit.
- Versioning: SemVer; `0.1.0` = store launch; Conventional Commits.
- Sprints: 8, self-paced, each closed by QA → Security → Tech → Closer, outputs in `reviews/sprint_N/`.
- Out of scope for v1 (do not build): accounts, sync, paid tier, mobile, Firefox/Safari, Gmail API, bank links, analytics SDKs, any acting feature, recovery actions.

### Each §5 row, verbatim (2026-10-01)

- 2026-10-01 Platform: Chrome extension, **Manifest V3**. Chromium-based browsers (Edge, Brave, Arc) get it for free. Firefox/Safari deferred. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Language: **TypeScript 5.x, `strict: true`**, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`. No `any` in `src/core`. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Framework: **WXT** (entrypoints, HMR, manifest generation, cross-browser later). — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 UI: **Preact** + Tailwind for popup, side panel, options, digest. One component per file. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Storage: **IndexedDB via Dexie**, schema versioned with migrations. `chrome.storage.local` only for small flags and the install id. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Schemas: **Zod** for every stored type, every message between contexts, and the telemetry payload. Parse at every boundary. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Tests: **Vitest** (unit + property via `fast-check`), **Playwright** for extension E2E against **fixture pages** served locally. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Lint/format: ESLint (typescript-eslint strict) + Prettier. Custom lint rule: no `dispatchEvent`, `.click()`, `.submit()`, `fetch` to non-allowlisted hosts in `entrypoints/content/**` and `src/adapters/**`. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Package manager: **pnpm**. Node 22 LTS. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Optional matcher model: **Chrome built-in on-device model (Prompt API)** first, if available on the user's Chrome. **BYO key** (Anthropic or OpenAI) second. Both off by default, both advisory only (Hard Rule 4). — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Telemetry backend: **FastAPI + MongoDB** on Railway. One endpoint. Strict Pydantic schema. Rate-limited. Public aggregate stats later. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Encryption at rest: Optional passphrase: WebCrypto AES-GCM, key from PBKDF2 (600k iterations). Off by default; on = ledger unreadable without the passphrase. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Grading policy: `policy/default.json`, versioned. Thresholds and windows live here, never in code. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 License: **Apache-2.0**. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Repo: `github.com/Poojan6216/attest`, public from the first commit. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Versioning: SemVer. `0.1.0` is the store launch. CHANGELOG kept by hand. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Commits: Conventional Commits. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Adapters: **Fixture-driven.** Every adapter and observer is built and tested against sanitised saved DOM snapshots in `tests/fixtures/pages/<vendor>/<yyyy-mm-dd>/`. Live pages are for capture and smoke, never for unit tests. — locked by build_spec §5.
- 2026-10-01 Repo visibility: superseded by the founder on 2026-10-01 (made private); see the Sprint log entry for [0.1].

## Sprint log

(agent appends here: `- YYYY-MM-DD [N.M] decision — reason — rule`)
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] pnpm pinned to 10.34.6 (`packageManager`), not npm `latest` 12.x — mature, known lockfile and install-script behaviour; installed to `~/.local/bin` because the global npm prefix is root-owned — reproducible builds.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] TypeScript pinned to 5.9.3. npm `latest` is 7.0, but §5 locks TS 5.x and typescript-eslint 8.71 supports `<6.1` — §5.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] WXT 0.21.4 ships no Preact template. Used the vanilla template; Preact JSX compiles through Vite 8's built-in oxc transform (`jsx.importSource: 'preact'`). No `@preact/preset-vite` (it pulls `@babel/core` for dev-only refresh) — fewer dependencies, HR9.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] Preact 10.29.8, not 11.0.0 (released days ago): widest compatibility with test tooling for Sprint 4 UI tests.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] WXT auto-imports disabled (`imports: false`); every module imports `defineBackground`, `browser`, etc. explicitly so reviewers and the lint rules see every dependency — HR3, HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] Repo is a pnpm workspace: root `package.json` delegates every script (`pnpm build`, `pnpm test`, …) to `extension/`; `policy/`, `bench/`, `docs/` stay at the root per §6.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] Manifest: `permissions` = `storage` (install id + flags), `alarms` (scheduler), `sidePanel` (added by WXT for the side panel entrypoint, §6 surface); `host_permissions` empty; `minimum_chrome_version` 120 (side panel API + MV3 module service worker) — HR8.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] Popup and side panel exist as static shells in Sprint 0 because task 0.4's scripted session opens them; they read nothing.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] Build emits source maps (readable, unobfuscated code for store review; CWS forbids obfuscation) — HR9.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] Extension version is 0.0.1 until the 0.1.0 store release — SemVer per §5.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] The agent did not create the public GitHub repo. MANUAL.md reserves that for the founder and publishing is not reversible. All commits are local on `main` until the repo exists; then one push publishes the history — HR-neutral, outward-facing action.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] `src/log.ts` is safe by construction: event names must be identifiers; string values print only if they look like plumbing (identifiers, enum values, ULIDs, SHA-256 hex, ISO timestamps, semver); keys naming content (subject, counterparty, body, …) are always redacted; error messages are never printed (name + length only) — HR10.
- 2026-10-01 [0.2] `attest/no-act` goes beyond the five listed patterns: it also forbids synthetic event constructors, `preventDefault`/`stopPropagation`, DOM mutation (`appendChild`, `setAttribute`, `classList.*`, `style.*`, `innerHTML =`, …), navigation (`location`, `history`, `window.open`), programmatic scrolling (Sprint 3: "no scrolling for the user"), `on*` handler assignment, and any reference to network globals (calls, `new`, aliasing, `window.fetch`) — HR1, HR3.
- 2026-10-01 [0.2] Fenced scopes (`entrypoints/content/**`, `src/adapters/**`, `src/observers/**`, `src/core/**`) run with `linterOptions.noInlineConfig`, so an `eslint-disable` comment there has no effect and is reported — the rules cannot be disabled locally — HR3, HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [0.2] `src/core` fence is three rules: `no-restricted-imports` (Dexie, wxt, preact, ulidx, Node built-ins, and any path into adapters/observers/ledger/bus/ui/telemetry/crypto/intel/background/entrypoints/log), `no-restricted-globals` (chrome, browser, DOM, fetch, timers, performance, crypto, globalThis, …), `no-restricted-syntax` (`Date.now()`, argless `new Date()`/`Date()`, `Math.random()`, `getRandomValues`, `randomUUID`, dynamic import) — HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [0.2] Custom rule `attest/top-level-listeners` (background entry, `src/background/**`, `src/bus/**`): `addListener` inside an async function, generator, or then/catch/finally/timer/microtask callback is an error — MV3 listener rule (task 0.7 asks for this).
- 2026-10-01 [0.2] typescript-eslint `strictTypeChecked` + `stylisticTypeChecked`; Prettier `printWidth` 110, single quotes, trailing commas. `no-console` is off only in `src/log.ts` and in `tests/**` (the test runner's own output, not the extension's console).
- 2026-10-01 [0.2] Lint fences are proven by permanent tests (`tests/unit/lint/*.spec.ts`: RuleTester cases for both custom rules, and probes through the real `eslint.config.js`), not by a one-off manual check.
- 2026-10-01 [0.3] Test pages that are not vendor captures (blank pages, attack harnesses) live in `tests/fixtures/pages/_harness/`, outside the `<vendor>/<yyyy-mm-dd>/` scheme reserved for captured fixtures.
- 2026-10-01 [0.3] E2E uses Playwright's bundled Chromium (`channel: 'chromium'`, new headless) with a fresh profile per test; branded Chrome ignores `--load-extension`. `pnpm e2e` always rebuilds first so tests never run a stale build. No retries; `forbidOnly` in CI (qa_tester rule 10).
- 2026-10-01 [0.3] The fixture server binds 127.0.0.1 only, serves GET/HEAD, and rejects any `..` segment, backslash or NUL outright (property-tested, 2,000 runs) instead of clamping.
- 2026-10-01 [0.3] Root-level TypeScript tooling (`bench/`, later `scripts/`) gets its own `tsconfig.json` + `eslint.config.js` at the repo root, using the same pinned lint stack; root `pnpm lint`/`pnpm typecheck` run the extension's checks and then the root's. Root `package.json` is `"type": "module"`.
- 2026-10-01 [0.3] `bench/run_bench.ts` regenerates `RESULTS.md` from the newest `bench/results/<bench>-<date>.json` per bench; with no benches it says so and reports no numbers — HR12.
- 2026-10-01 [0.4] Network-intercept test uses Chromium's NetLog (`--log-net-log`) as ground truth instead of Playwright routing alone: NetLog sees every request from process start, including the extension service worker, which page-level routing does not reliably see. Each request is attributed by its recorded initiator: `chrome-extension://<id>` or any page origin (where content scripts run) counts against the allowlist; `not an origin` is the browser process itself — HR1.
- 2026-10-01 [0.4] Chromium contacts its own services even with Playwright's `--disable-background-networking` (observed: accounts.google.com, android.clients.google.com, clients2.google.com, update.googleapis.com, www.google.com, mtalk.google.com). Browser-initiated requests are ignored only if they go to a fixed list of known Chromium hosts; any other browser-initiated host fails the test (fail closed against misattribution). The list never contains a vendor or agent host (unit-tested) — HR1.
- 2026-10-01 [0.4] The guarded session also routes every page request through Playwright (recorded and aborted unless local or fixture) and sets a dead proxy (`--proxy-server=http://127.0.0.1:9`), so nothing can actually leave the machine even while the test is failing — HR1.
- 2026-10-01 [0.4] Each mandatory test file carries a negative control that runs every time: canary fetches from the service worker, an extension page and a fixture page must be detected; a seed planted in each channel must be caught. A recorder that silently breaks makes the suite red, never green — HR1, HR2.
- 2026-10-01 [0.4] The test's ALLOWLIST is written in the test file and asserted equal to `NETWORK_ALLOWLIST` in `src/config.ts`, so widening it requires changing both (visible in review) — HR1.
- 2026-10-01 [0.5] Seeded secrets carry canary fragments (e.g. "vault code for the Lisbon storage unit") so a partial leak (an 80-char subject hint cut from a body) is caught, not only exact copies. Matching is NFKC + case + whitespace insensitive, over decoded values and serialised JSON — HR2, HR10.
- 2026-10-01 [0.5] Seeds are injected into fixture pages server-side (the page is served already seeded, in text and in the attributes adapters read), so the extension sees them exactly as real content. Channels: ledger export (via the extension's own `ui.query`), raw dump of every IndexedDB database, chrome.storage local/session/sync, console from every context, network URLs and bodies. The test also asserts every channel was actually read — HR2.
- 2026-10-01 [0.4] The scheduler's first real alarm is `attest.purge` (retention, implemented in 2.6); Sprint 0 schedules it and logs when it fires, which is the alarm the 5-minute session triggers. Alarms not ours are cleared on install/update; ours are recreated on startup if Chrome dropped them.
- 2026-10-01 [0.11] CI runs the `@mandatory` E2E tests as their own named step before the rest; validated with actionlint 1.7.12.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] Repo created public at github.com/Poojan6216/attest on the founder's explicit instruction ("create the repo"). Before the first push, the three API-key-shaped seeds were changed to non-provider prefixes (`zk-proj-…`, `AKXQ…`, `sk-anx-api99-…`): the originals matched real AWS/Anthropic key formats and would trip GitHub push protection and provider leak scanners. The leak test needs the shape, not a real vendor format. The unpushed commit was amended so no provider-shaped fake key is in public history — HR10.
- 2026-10-01 [0.1] SUPERSEDES §5 "Repo … public from the first commit": the founder asked to make `Poojan6216/attest` private; done with `gh repo edit --visibility private`. Consequences tracked: (a) Sprint 7.2 serves `PRIVACY.md` via GitHub Pages, which needs a public repo on the free plan — flip public before launch or host the policy elsewhere; (b) store review and the launch copy ("read the source") assume a public repo; (c) private repos get a limited free Actions minutes budget and each CI run includes the 5-minute mandatory network test; (d) the README CI badge only renders for signed-in collaborators. The code and docs keep the public-repo URL; nothing else changes.
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] A Zod 4 schema construction probe (new Function) would trip the extension CSP; handled globally in src/setup.ts (see 0.4 wiring) — HR9
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] src/core/tsconfig.json also sets "jsx": null and "jsxImportSource": null — the inherited react-jsx + preact settings with moduleDetection force made TypeScript import preact/jsx-runtime into every core file, and preact's types load lib.dom, so core was silently type-checked with DOM globals — Hard Rule 4 (src/core pure)
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] Host constants are frozen lists of match patterns per vendor (gmail: ['https://mail.google.com/*'], muse: []) rather than single strings — an empty list cannot leak a '' pattern into host_permissions, and it matches the adapter contract `hosts: readonly string[]` — Hard Rule 8
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] Rule ids must be lowercase snake_case identifiers, stricter than the spec's 'non-empty string' — receipts and logs carry ruleId, and log.ts prints only identifier-shaped strings, so ids stay loggable — Hard Rule 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] allowlistedFields must contain exactly the three sources gmail.sent, gmail.receipt and gcal.event (missing or unknown sources are errors) — a source with no allowlist would leave redaction undefined — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] ObservationFieldName (Zod enum) is defined in src/core/policy.ts because core may not import src/ledger; a unit test pins it to Object.keys(Observation.shape) until Sprint 1 task 1.0 moves the schemas into core — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] RuleOutcome = the 6 Grades + PENDING; a test pins it to the ledger Grade enum — Hard Rules 4/5
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] Policy type is DeepReadonly<z.output<typeof Policy>> and loadPolicy deep-freezes its own parse output (never the caller's input) — no runtime retuning of thresholds — Hard Rule 4
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] loadPolicy parses with { jitless: true } so Zod never uses its new Function fast path for the policy, and parsing behaves the same in Node tests and under the extension CSP — Hard Rule 9
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] Error format: '<path>: <problem>'. A missing key reads 'is required', a non-finite number 'must be a finite number', each unknown key gets its own line with the key in the path, keys containing dots are bracket-quoted (allowlistedFields["gmail.sent"][1]), and a non-object document reads '(root): expected object, got <kind>'. A partial >= verified error is reported on thresholds.partial. — specific errors (task Done-when)
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] Windows are non-negative finite numbers, not forced to integers (as the spec says) — Hard Rule 4
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] The AST test scans entrypoints/ as well as src/ (the spec named src/) because background.ts holds scheduling and alerts, where a hardcoded threshold would be just as wrong. In src/core the rule is stricter than the spec: no numeric literal other than 0 or 1, including fractions (ledger_engineer rule 2 'no magic numbers in src/core'); the exemption set is commented — Hard Rule 4
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] default.json number spelling follows Prettier (0.5, 0.2 instead of 0.50, 0.20); the values are identical, and the root `prettier --check .` requires it — Hard Rule 12 (no hand-edited divergence)
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] Feature flags are a frozen FEATURE_FLAGS tuple, a FeatureFlag union type, and a frozen FEATURE_FLAG_DEFAULTS of type Record<FeatureFlag, boolean>, all false — Hard Rules 1, 4, 11 (opt-in only)
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] Policy rules may not use the id 'default_unverifiable'. It is exported as TERMINAL_RULE_ID from src/core/policy.ts, where the grading contract lives, so grade() can import it without core depending on src/config.ts — a policy rule with that id could grade VERIFIED while its receipt's ruleId says no rule matched — Hard Rule 5 (and ledger_engineer rule 3)
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] The no-threshold scan counts string and template literals that parse as numbers (Number('0.85')) as numeric literals. It covers every TS/JS extension, and in src/core the only JSON import allowed is policy/default.json — this closes the three gaps around 'no threshold exists anywhere in code' — Hard Rule 4
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] Strictness and required keys are tested by walking the shipped default.json, not a hand-written list — any new level or key added to the policy is covered automatically, so the schema cannot loosen silently — Hard Rules 2 and 4
- 2026-10-01 [0.8] I kept the implementer's decisions after checking each one: the snake_case rule-id pattern, frozen lists as host constants, jsx/jsxImportSource null in the core tsconfig, per-parse jitless, Prettier's spelling 0.5 and 0.2, and windows as non-negative non-integers — reasons as in the implementer's report — Hard Rules 2, 4, 8, 9, 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] DEFERRED to Sprint 1.0: Receipt has no agent field, so an exported UNASKED receipt loses its agent (the repo keeps it only as an index field); add agent to the core Receipt schema and drop the putReceipt agent option — HR4 (replay)
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] DEFERRED to Sprint 2.6: coverage keeps only the latest ping per observer+account, so older windows fall back to UNVERIFIABLE (safe side of HR5 but under-reports); decide whether coverage needs history, and add an epoch-ms index for range queries (occurredAt/lastSyncAt are ISO strings with varying offsets) — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] DEFERRED to Sprint 1.3: observationsByCounterpartyToken is an unbounded read; candidate retrieval must bound it by bucket range — tech_reviewer
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] Tables are exposed on the Dexie subclass as typed getters (this.table<Row, Key>(name)) instead of `table!: Table` fields. That avoids non-null/definite-assignment assertions and casts, and Dexie leaves existing accessors alone. — code rules (strict TS) — HR4/maintainability
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] Indexes are exactly as specified; Dexie needed no adjustment. coverage uses a compound primary key [observerId+accountKey] with accountKey = accountHash ?? ''. — spec — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] An upsert whose id already exists with a different contentHash or rawHash throws LedgerError('id_conflict') instead of overwriting. — stored facts are never silently replaced, so replay stays reproducible — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] Adapter health and coverage pings keep the latest report. An incoming report with an older checkedAt/lastSyncAt is ignored and returns {written:false}. Comparison is by epoch ms, not string, because offsets vary; a tie overwrites; a corrupt stored row is overwritten. — a stale OK can't mask a current DEGRADED, and a stale ping can't fake coverage — HR6, HR5
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] Requests, receipts and grants use plain put by primary key (replace). Receipts reuse their id for PENDING→FINAL; whether a state transition is allowed is for the pure core (state.ts), not the repo. — simplest option — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] Counterparty tokens are trimmed and lowercased, with the same function on write and query. The email-domain token is added only when the domain contains a dot. The derivation is marked as a placeholder for Sprint 1's normalize.ts. — tokens come only from allowlisted fields — HR2
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] openLedger is async and opens eagerly. The migration list is checked before IndexedDB is touched (versions are ascending positive integers, and all 8 tables are still declared at the end); IndexedDB/upgrade failures become LedgerError('open_failed') and are logged. — fail loud at open, not on first query — HR6
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] getLedger() caches the opening promise per context and resets the cache on failure so the next call retries. closeLedger() was added (used by tests; the Danger-zone wipe can use it later). — the service worker is ephemeral, so no state that matters lives in memory — extension_engineer rule 3
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] exportLedger reads all tables in one read transaction for a consistent snapshot. Order is IndexedDB primary-key order, and each object's keys follow schema order (Zod output). exportedAt is passed in, so there is no clock in the ledger. Corrupt rows are skipped. — determinism and no smuggled content in the export — HR2, HR10
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] Query arguments are validated: agent against AgentId, date with z.iso.date(), bucket range must be safe integers with from <= to, and the token must not be empty; violations throw invalid_input. — parse at every boundary — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] LedgerError context holds only the table name, codes, counts and Zod issue paths (field names), never values. — HR10
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] Tests inject deliberately ill-typed input with `as never`, use an `omit` helper rather than unused rest-destructuring, and import 'fake-indexeddb/auto' first because Dexie captures indexedDB when the module loads. — test hygiene — n/a
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] A stored row that fails its strict schema (unreadable) and holds the incoming contentHash or rawHash, or the incoming id, is deleted and replaced inside the same rw transaction. It is logged as ledger.row_replaced {table, id}, and the upsert returns {id: new, inserted: true}. A readable row still makes the upsert a no-op (or an id_conflict), as before. — A no-op on a row no read can return hides evidence for good (for example after Sprint 1 tightens a schema), which could turn an email Attest saw into NOT_FOUND. — Hard Rule 5
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] Ledger log lines print the table name in snake_case (adapterHealth becomes adapter_health). — The logger keeps only lowercase identifiers, so the camelCase name was redacted and the failing table could not be identified. — Hard Rule 6
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] A row id is logged only if it passes the table's id schema: Ulid for requests, claims, observations and receipts; AgentId for grants; a non-empty string for adapter, observer and meta keys, which the logger still sanitises. Otherwise it is logged as null. — A corrupt row's id field can hold content that looks like an identifier, such as a hyphenated passphrase. — Hard Rule 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] exportLedger sorts each receipt's signals keys by code unit. — Zod keeps record keys in insertion order; sorting makes equal ledgers export byte-identical. — Hard Rule 4 (replay determinism)
- 2026-10-01 [0.6] Derived index fields are frozen into each row when it is written. Any change to how one is computed (bucket width, normalizeToken, counterpartyTokensOf, dateKey) must ship with a migration that re-derives it; this is documented in index-fields.ts. — Otherwise old rows silently drop out of index queries. — Hard Rule 5
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] The lint fence attest/top-level-listeners now also reports the bus listen() call in async/deferred scopes, and LISTENER_SCOPES run with noInlineConfig (bus verifier finding) — MV3 listener rule
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] Bus log noise (bus.dropped no_handler on other open extension pages, bus.send_failed no_receiver for ledger.changed with no UI open) is warn level by spec; revisit at tech review — HR6
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] send() uses the callback form of runtime.sendMessage and reads runtime.lastError inside the callback. A synchronous throw is also caught. — Chrome reports errors through lastError only in the callback form (promise-returning calls never set it), and reading it there marks it as checked. — HR6 fail loud
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] Transport failures are logged as BusError with a safe code (no_receiver, port_closed, context_invalidated, send_failed), mapped from Chrome's English error strings. The strings themselves are never logged. BusError.message is always 'bus: <code>'; the original error is kept as `cause` for a debugger. — Logs stay useful without printing content. — HR10
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] Every handled message is answered: the listener returns true and calls sendResponse once the handler settles. Fire-and-forget types get an undefined acknowledgement. — Otherwise Chrome reports 'message port closed' to the sender and every successful send would log a false bus.send_failed. — HR6
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] Return values from non-query handlers are awaited (so rejections are logged) but never forwarded. Their handler type returns `unknown`. — Only ui.result has a response schema, so nothing unschema'd crosses a boundary. — HR2
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] ui.query is accepted only from extension pages (sender.url starts with runtime.getURL('')). From a content script it is dropped with reason 'untrusted_context'. — Content scripts run on vendor pages and are the least-trusted context; a compromised one must not be able to pull ledger.export. — HR2/HR10
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] A ui.query handler's answer is validated as a ui.result of the same kind before it is sent; if it fails, the listener logs BusError invalid_result and answers handler_failed. query() checks the kind again on receipt and returns unavailable on a mismatch. — Parse at every boundary. — HR2
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] ui.result `data` is `unknown` but refuses `undefined` (the refine is annotated `: boolean` so TypeScript does not narrow the type). — Chrome's JSON wire drops an undefined key, which would turn a valid answer into 'unavailable' far from the cause. Rejecting it fails loudly at the source. — HR6
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] ledger.changed.tables must name at least one table, and LedgerTable lists the 8 tables from build_spec §6 (they match db.ts). — An empty change notice means nothing. — none
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] adapterId, observerId and version are min(1), like AdapterHealth. Empty requests/claims/observations arrays are allowed. — An adapter that found nothing still reports its batch; health is reported separately. — HR6
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] BusHandlers is a mapped type, so a ui.query handler that does not return UiResult | Promise<UiResult> is a compile error. Dispatch uses a correlated generic lookup with no casts. — Type-checked at compile time, re-validated at runtime. — HR2
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] The bus.listener_failed catch logs only the error, never `raw` or `sender`. — Whatever threw (for example a hostile getter) could throw again inside the catch and escape the listener. — HR6
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] Test harness connectAs(sender) wraps listeners as they register, injecting a Chrome-like sender and JSON round-tripping the message and response. Delivery, the return-true protocol and lastError are still the fake's own. — The fake browser always passes sender {}, which listen() correctly drops as foreign. — none (test-only)
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] listen() fails closed on sender identity: a message is accepted only when sender.id is defined and equals runtime.id — an orphaned context (runtime.id undefined) must not treat undefined===undefined as 'our own extension' — HR10 / security (fail closed)
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] BUS_MESSAGE_TYPES is exported as a frozen copy (readonly BusMessageType[]) instead of Zod's shared mutable .options array — an exported constant must not be mutable by any consumer, and mutating it would also mutate the schema's .options — HR6 (no silent drift)
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] LedgerTable parity with the ledger is enforced by a test (tests/unit/bus/ledger-tables.spec.ts compares it to STORES_V1 and LEDGER_TABLE_NAMES, and to LedgerTableName at the type level) rather than by importing LEDGER_TABLE_NAMES into messages.ts — keeps production bus code off another agent's in-progress module while still making drift a red test; the integrator may later derive the enum from LEDGER_TABLE_NAMES — HR6
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] listener-lint probes must parse (zero fatal messages) before any 'rule did not fire' assertion counts — an unparseable probe proves nothing — sprint 0.7 Done-when integrity
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] Kept the implementer's choice to acknowledge non-query handled messages with undefined instead of forwarding the handler's return value (a deviation from the spec's literal 'deliver it via sendResponse') — only ui.result has a response schema, and content scripts on vendor pages must not receive unschema'd ledger data — HR2/HR10; integrator to confirm and record in Decisions.md
- 2026-10-01 [0.7] Kept the implementer's beyond-spec untrusted_context drop for ui.query from non-extension-page senders (sender.url must start with runtime.getURL('')) — content scripts are the least-trusted context and must not be able to pull ledger.export — HR2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Known blind spots of the sanitiser (documented in docs/adapters.md): a lone first name of a contact not passed via --names, free-text subjects/snippets without --redact-selector, names in CSS content strings, hex or double-encoded emails, unformatted card/phone digit runs. The founder reads every fixture before committing — HR2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Attach with chromium.connectOverCDP(cdp, { noDefaults: true }) — without it, Playwright's focus emulation fired a trusted 'blur' on the captured page (caught by the E2E probe) — Hard Rule 3 (never act on a vendor page)
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Copy the page with inertDoc.importNode(document.documentElement, true) into a document.implementation.createHTMLDocument('') document, not cloneNode — a document with no browsing context runs no custom-element constructors and loads no resources, so making the copy causes no side effects — Hard Rule 3
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Page-side constants go in as a page.evaluate argument, and the in-page function has no inner named functions — tsx's keepNames would inject a __name helper the page lacks; verified by the E2E running the real CLI under tsx — fail loud (Hard Rule 6)
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Inline display always; visibility/font-size/font-weight/color (plus fill/stroke on SVG) only where they differ from the parent; position, background-color and flex/grid basics only when not the default; skip <head> — fixtures render recognisably while staying small — Hard Rule 12 (bench-friendly fixtures)
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Drop every CSS at-rule (not only @import/@font-face) and every non-fragment url()/image-set() — at-rules carry '@' that would break the grep check, and the critical computed styles are already inlined — Hard Rules 1 and 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Turn any '@' that does not start example.com/org/net into U+FF20 '＠' in text, attributes and CSS — so a grep for '@' finds only fake domains — Hard Rules 2 and 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Remove every <link> (a superset of the listed rels), every meta[http-equiv], template, portal, fencedframe, applet and param — zero network, and nothing hidden from the PII walker — Hard Rules 1 and 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Remove all images, including data: URLs and avatars — a picture of a person is personal data and cannot be checked — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Keep href/action/formaction/cite but drop query strings and user:password@ — tokens and ids live there; adapters may anchor on paths and #fragments — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Phone numbers must have separators or a leading '+'; bare digit runs are left alone (documented limitation) — bare runs cannot be told apart from ids and timestamps that adapters need — Hard Rule 5 (don't guess)
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Name scope: --names whole and token by token everywhere in any case; auto-learned names of 2+ tokens everywhere; one-word senders only inside their own element; stopwords never learned. Fakes never equal a real token (Faker.reserve) — removes people's names without rewriting vendor UI words such as 'Google Calendar' — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Seeds: rank by SHA-256(seed, 'seed-pick', id), take the first 3, skip any seed whose value contains '@', and throw too_few_seeds below 3 — deterministic per seed, and keeps the '@' check valid — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] --cdp must be an http(s)/ws(s) URL on 127.0.0.1, localhost or [::1] — the tool never attaches to a remote browser — Hard Rule 1
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] --tab that matches several tabs is an error (ambiguous_tab), not 'pick the first'; with no --tab the default is the first http/https/file page (devtools, extension, chrome:// and about: pages excluded) — never capture a page the founder did not mean — Hard Rule 11
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] happy-dom runs with JS and CSS file loading off, navigation off, and a fetch interceptor answering 451 to every async and sync request; unit tests prove 0 server hits — Hard Rule 1
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Strip <style>/<script> inside svg/math with a tag-level scan before happy-dom parses (and in the page) — happy-dom 20.14.5 silently drops the rest of the document after them — Hard Rule 6 (fail loud, no silent content loss)
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] CLI output uses console (allowed under tests/**), not src/log.ts — the extension logger redacts the paths the founder must see; the CLI prints only origin+path, the output path and counts, never page content (E2E asserts no originals in stdout) — Hard Rule 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] labels.json: other entries parsed as unknown and kept verbatim; the new entry checked by a strict Zod schema ({capturedAt: YYYY-MM-DD, selectorsVersion: null, seededSecrets, expected: [], notes: ''}); written via temp file + rename; corrupt files refused, never clobbered — keeps hand-written labels safe — Hard Rule 12
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Personal-data class tests live in capture-pii.spec.ts (still exercised through sanitizeHtml), not sanitize.spec.ts — keeps each file under 400 lines; sanitize.spec.ts keeps the strip/neutralise/structure/seed/determinism/final-'@' tables — n/a (code-size rule)
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] The capture E2E sets test.use({ trace: 'off' }) — it drives its own browsers, and concurrent Playwright runs clean the shared test-results/ folder, which broke trace copying — qa_tester rule 10 (flaky = failing)
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Neutralise every CSS function whose name contains an escape, whatever it decodes to — Chromium decodes and fetches `\75 rl(…)`, and real style sheets never escape a function name — Hard Rule 1
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Apply url()/image-set()/escaped-function neutralisation to all non-link, non-style attributes on SVG-namespace elements (new 'presentation' verdict), not just a fixed list — Chromium fetches url() from fill, stroke, mask, clip-path, marker-*, cursor and future presentation attributes; neutralising is a no-op elsewhere — Hard Rule 1
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Rewrite percent-encoded and base64/base64url-hidden emails with the same fake as the plain address and re-encode them in the original alphabet and padding — Calendar event ids embed the owner's email and Gmail search fragments embed %40 addresses; the ids stay well-formed for adapters — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] For base64, try '/'-free segments first (keeping their own alphabet), then standard-alphabet tails after each '/' — a url-safe token at the end of a path must not be re-encoded with '+' or '/' — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Learn display names from `Name (email)` as well as `Name <email>`, allowing a newline before the bracket; add account/contact/help/us/our/at to the stopwords — Gmail's account button label leaked the founder's name; the stopwords stop 'Contact Us (help@…)' becoming a name — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Treat 16-digit cards in groups of 4 (+1–3) and Amex 4-6-5 with a consistent separator as a 'card' class with format-preserving fake digits; unformatted digit runs are still left alone — the phone pattern left the last group, and bare runs cannot be told apart from ids — Hard Rules 2 and 5
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Accept Latin-script letters and combining marks in emails (u flag), not all Unicode letters — catches accented addresses without swallowing neighbouring CJK text into a fake address — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Drop nonce attributes, hidden and password input values, and meta tags whose name mentions csrf/xsrf/token/nonce/secret — session secrets have no place in a committed fixture, and adapters anchor on visible data — Hard Rule 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Treat xlink:href as a link attribute (query and userinfo trimmed) and attributionsrc as a resource attribute (removed) — tokens live in query strings; attributionsrc can trigger background requests in Chrome — Hard Rules 1 and 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Wrap labels.json write/rename failures in CaptureError('io_failed') naming both paths — a half-written capture must fail loud with a fix, not a raw stack — Hard Rule 6
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] The E2E records every request from the live browser during capture and asserts the fixture keeps a colour that only inlined computed styles can supply — read-only must include zero network, and the spec's 'inline critical styles' requirement had no test — Hard Rules 1 and 3, qa_tester rule 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.9] Harness link text avoids a lone first name of a learned contact ('From this sender') — that is the documented --names blind spot, not something the tool claims to catch — Hard Rule 12 (tests assert only what the tool measurably does)
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] src/setup.ts (z.config({ jitless: true })) is the first import of every entrypoint, enforced by tests/unit/setup.spec.ts: Zod 4 otherwise probes eval with new Function at schema construction, which the extension CSP blocks and Chrome reports as a console error — HR9
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] Service worker wiring: listen(createHandlers()) answers ui.query (ledger.export, health.list) only; onInstalled ensures the install id then resets alarms; onStartup recreates dropped alarms; onAlarm dispatches by constant name. - 2026-10-01 [0.10] The options page reads and resets the install id directly through src/ledger/install-id.ts (chrome.storage.local), not through the bus. It is storage, not a message between contexts, and storage.onChanged keeps every open tab in sync — HR8/HR10 (smallest surface)
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] Install id is a lowercase v4 UUID, checked with z.uuidv4() plus a lowercase regex; an uppercase or non-v4 value counts as corrupt and is replaced, so telemetry later counts one id per install — HR10
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] ensureInstallId() treats a missing id as expected (log.info install_id.created); getInstallId() treats it as unexpected (log.warn install_id.regenerated reason=missing), because the install handler should already have created it — HR6 (fail loud)
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] After writing a fresh id, the module reads it back and returns what storage holds, so a service worker and an options page creating it at the same moment both return the winner — HR6

- 2026-10-01 [0.10] handleInstalled(details: unknown) checks the event with a non-strict Zod schema (Chrome adds fields); malformed details are logged and ignored; chrome_update and shared_module_update are skipped. Storage errors throw InstallIdError to background.ts, which logs install.failed — HR6
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] The section list (SECTIONS, in entrypoints/options/sections.ts) lives with the UI, not in src/config.ts: it is UI wording and structure, and config.ts says it holds plumbing constants. Nav and headings both read from it so they cannot drift
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] Options page has an in-page nav to sections instead of a skip link: the spec allows either, and the privacy line must be the first text in main
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] No link to docs/privacy-architecture.md yet (ux rule 8): neither that doc nor the public repo exists; add the link when the doc lands, so the page has no dead link
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] The two-step reset moves focus to Cancel (the safe choice) when the question opens and back to the start button afterwards; the result is announced in an always-present role=status paragraph
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] The solid danger button sets its focus-ring colour with Tailwind's important modifier (focus-visible:outline-red-700!), because the unlayered global :focus-visible rule in src/ui/tailwind.css beats every layered Tailwind class. - 2026-10-01 [0.10] PRIVACY.md also discloses the 200-character request text hint (Request.textHint in build_spec §7). The task's list left it out, but the policy has to match what the contracts store — HR2

- 2026-10-01 [0.10] The reset control moves focus in useLayoutEffect, not useEffect: focus must move in the same task as the render that removes the focused button, or a fast keyboard user's next key lands on <body> and Tab restarts from the top — ux_engineer rule 10 (no Hard Rule)
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] When the stored install id changes to a missing or invalid value, the options page shows 'No valid install id is stored' instead of the old id, and the change listener never writes. - 2026-10-01 [0.10] PRIVACY.md describes future telemetry the way Sprint 5.6/5.7 specify it: one report a day of counts and yes/no values plus Attest version, Chrome major version, date and install id. The implementer's wording ('weekly', counts only) understated what the payload carries — HR1, HR12
- 2026-10-01 [0.10] Kept the implementer's choice that ensureInstallId logs a missing id on first install as info 'install_id.created', not as warn 'install_id.regenerated' (the spec text says warn for missing). A first install expects a missing id, so a warning on every install would be noise; getInstallId still warns on a missing id. A unit test now locks this in — HR6

The launcher accepts ATTEST_EXTENSION_PATH so mutant builds can be tested; CI never sets it — HR1
- 2026-10-01 [0.4] The negative control includes a WebSocket canary: Playwright routing never sees WebSockets, so it proves the NetLog layer catches extension traffic on its own (observed via=netlog) — HR1
- 2026-10-01 [0.5] Proof run 2026-10-01: clean build green; a mutant build with console.log(<person_name_1 seed>) in background main() failed with LEAK seed=person_name_1 channel=console (the report names the seed id and location, never the secret) — HR2, HR10
- 2026-10-01 [0.5] The no-leak test asserts every channel was actually read (export ok, attest database present, storage present, service-worker console captured), so an unreadable channel can never pass as no leaks — HR2
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] SUPERSEDES the [0.1] logger line: src/log.ts no longer decides by shape. Event names come from the closed LOG_EVENTS list in src/log-vocabulary.ts (log.* only accepts the LogEvent type); a string value prints only if it is in LOG_VALUES (our enums, codes, reasons, table and alarm names, our adapter/observer ids) or is a ULID (first character 0-7), a lowercase SHA-256, an ISO timestamp with offset, or a semver. Identifier-shaped and UPPER_SNAKE strings outside the list print as [redacted len=N]. A test pins the vocabulary to every enum the code logs — identifier shape cannot tell `bus.dropped` from `ottoline.vasquez` (QA-6, SECURITY-1) — Hard Rules 10 and 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] Log keys print only when they match /^[a-z][A-Za-z]{0,31}$/ (no digits, no punctuation); any other key prints as field<N>. Content-key redaction is decided on the key as the caller wrote it, by substring (address, body, email, hint, name, subject, text, title, token, url, value, … in src/log.ts), so `senderEmail` or `Subject Hint` are redacted too, numbers and vocabulary words included — QA-2, SECURITY-1 — Hard Rule 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] Log numbers print only without a run of 5+ digits, or as a non-negative integer below 10^8 under a count key (count, issues, total, version, bytes, ms, …Count, …Ms, …Bytes, …Total); anything else prints as [number]. The 10^8 cap keeps SSNs, phone and card numbers out under any key. Error names print only from LOG_ERROR_NAMES (built-ins, DOMException, Dexie, ZodError, our errors); others print as Error — SECURITY-1 — Hard Rule 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] New lint rule attest/log-call on all of src/** and entrypoints/**: the event is a string literal and fields are an object literal (or a ternary of object literals) with keys written in the source; no computed keys, no spreads, no variable, no log[level] — a key can then never carry data; the runtime key rule is the second line — QA-2 — Hard Rule 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] The log level is info in every build, with no runtime or user setting; setLogLevel exists for tests only. Debug lines are routine plumbing (bus drops nobody needs to see); a switch that changes what is printed is one more thing to get wrong — TECH-18 — Hard Rule 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] SUPERSEDES [0.5] "exactly 15 seeds": seeded.json has the 15 task-0.5 formats plus three identifier-shaped seeds (token_lowercase `k7pq2vxw9mzr4tnb8hyd`, email_local_part `perpetua.villanueva`, passphrase_hyphenated `saffron-otter-lantern-quarry`) that the old logger would have printed; the mandatory loader requires at least 15 (`.min(15)`), the capture CLI's loader stays lenient. Every seed now has canaries: name tokens of 6+ letters (written capitalised), three-word windows of passphrases, 16-character windows (stride 8) of every key, token and the base64 blob, the SSN digits, the URL slug — QA-6, SECURITY-V3 — Hard Rules 2 and 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] SUPERSEDES the [0.5] matching claim ("whitespace insensitive", which only collapsed whitespace): the no-leak matcher compares a compact form (NFKC, lower case, every whitespace, punctuation and symbol removed) of needle and haystack, so `OttolineVasquezBrannigan` and `912447031` are caught; every string is also searched percent-decoded and with each base64/base64url run decoded. Needles shorter than 6 compact characters are refused at load. The negative control plants a base64-encoded secret in the console, a character-by-character percent-encoded name in a URL, and a compacted passphrase in chrome.storage — SECURITY-V3 — Hard Rules 2 and 10
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] The no-leak test also reads the extension origin's localStorage and sessionStorage (from every open extension page, per tab), its CacheStorage, and every web page's localStorage, sessionStorage, IndexedDB and document.cookie, plus every cookie in the profile; each new channel has a negative-control plant and the main test asserts the channels were read. The mandatory guard pins all nine channel names — SECURITY-V4, TECH-V2 — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] SUPERSEDES the [0.2] no-act description: attest/no-act now reports a forbidden name on any member access (called, referenced, .call/.apply/.bind, read off a prototype), in destructuring, and through reflection (Reflect.set/defineProperty/deleteProperty/apply/construct/setPrototypeOf, Object.assign/defineProperty/defineProperties/setPrototypeOf); any call or write through a computed key not written in the source; DOM insertion (append, prepend, before, after, insertNode, Range edits, CharacterData edits, adoptNode, document.write/writeln/open); actions reset, close, stop, print, animate, show, popovers, fullscreen, pointer lock, postMessage; Worker, SharedWorker, Audio, import() and serviceWorker as network; the vendor origin's localStorage, sessionStorage, indexedDB, caches, cookieStore, openDatabase, navigator.storage, the clipboard and document.cookie (read or write) as storage; and writes to data, className, id, title, name, type, dir, lang, slot, role, open, contentEditable, designMode, aria*, srcset, poster, formAction and more, on any object. Extractors therefore build their results as object literals. select/remove/normalize are allowed only as calls with arguments (`text.normalize('NFC')`) — SECURITY-3, SECURITY-V1, SECURITY-V2 — Hard Rules 3, 1, 2
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] New lint rule attest/no-network on all of src/** and entrypoints/** (scope analysis of fetch, XMLHttpRequest, WebSocket, EventSource, WebTransport, RTCPeerConnection, importScripts, and any member reaching them or sendBeacon). Exactly two files are exempt, declared now so no later task improvises a wider hole: src/telemetry/send.ts (Sprint 5.7) and src/intel/providers/byo.ts (Sprint 5.3); fences-scopes.spec.ts pins the pair. noInlineConfig now applies to all of src/** and entrypoints/**, so no eslint-disable works anywhere in shipped code — SECURITY-5, TECH-3, SECURITY-V5 — Hard Rule 1
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] Storage fences (build_spec §5: Dexie and chrome.storage only): localStorage, sessionStorage, caches, cookieStore and openDatabase are forbidden in all extension code (also via window/globalThis/self); indexedDB only in src/ledger and src/crypto. Content scripts, adapters and observers may not import ledger, background, crypto, telemetry, intel or ui code, or dexie; the extractors (src/adapters/<vendor>/**, src/observers/<source>/**, not base.ts) may not import src/bus or wxt/* either — TECH-2, SECURITY-V4, TECH-V2 — Hard Rules 1 and 2

No WebWorker-lib tsconfig was added: the lint fence covers it without a second DOM-free type world for shared modules — TECH-V3 — Hard Rule 6
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] console is forbidden everywhere except src/log.ts as a global (catches `const c = console` and destructuring) and as window/globalThis/self.console, on top of no-console. - 2026-10-01 [0.close] TypeScript projects split: extension/tsconfig.json is browser code only (src, entrypoints; types []), tests/tsconfig.json adds Node types, tsconfig.node.json covers *.config.ts and eslint-rules/*.d.ts; pnpm typecheck runs all four. TypeScript alone cannot keep Node out: wxt/utils types import vite's, which reference @types/node. So Node globals (Buffer, process, require, …), Node built-in imports and NodeJS.* types are also lint-fenced in extension code — TECH-6 — §5 TypeScript strict, Hard Rule 6
- 2026-10-01 [0.close] New lint rule attest/core-determinism for src/core: Date only as `new Date(value)`, `Date.UTC(…)` or `instanceof Date` (so no Date.now, Date.parse, `const { now } = Date`, aliasing or passing Date); Math only as a named member other than random; local-time getters and setters, getTimezoneOffset, toDateString/toTimeString, toLocale* and localeCompare are forbidden on any object; Intl is a restricted global. Date#toString cannot be told apart without types, so CI also runs the core and property suites under TZ=Pacific/Kiritimati and TZ=America/St_Johns (`pnpm --dir extension run test:tz`) — TECH-V1 — Hard Rule 4

- 2026-10-01 [0.close] Playwright runs 1 worker in CI and 2 locally. The hang QA saw is Chromium, not load: Chromium for Testing on the founder's Mac (Intel, native) sometimes never finishes starting when an extension service worker runs code. Measured with launchPersistentContext alone: Attest builds hung 2/30, 2/40, 1/40, 3/15 and 3/100 launches (the committed Sprint 0 build 2/20; a classic non-module worker build too); an empty extension 0/40, an empty module worker 0/30, Attest without its worker 0/30; no hung launch ever finished within 120 s, healthy ones take about 1 s. So tests/e2e/support/extension.ts gives each start 15 s and replaces a hung start with a new browser, at most 3 attempts, each logged as "[e2e] Chromium start-up hung"; only browser start-up is repeated, never a test step or an assertion, and the two @mandatory tests give every attempt its own NetLog (netlog-<n>.json) and analyse all of them, failing closed if any is missing or unreadable (a hung start's NetLog parses: checked on two). The service-worker wait is 30 s with its own message, and a launch that fails after the browser started closes it and removes its profile. Evidence: options + worker-lifecycle + smoke with --repeat-each=6 on 1 worker, 7 of 96 failed before (all 30 s launch timeouts) and 96 of 96 passed after, with 6 hung starts replaced; on 2 workers 96 of 96 passed. Before that, with 2 workers and six back-to-back `vitest run`s saturating the CPU, 74 of 75 passed (one 30 s launch timeout) — QA-4, QA-V2 — qa_tester rule 10 (the flake was in Chromium start-up, so that is what is replaced; the tests stay unretried), Hard Rules 1 and 2 (no NetLog is dropped)

- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] docs/grading.md v1 is the Sprint 1 contract, written before the code and revised after an adversarial review of v0 (9 blockers, 18 majors, 9 minors; all blockers fixed). Every module, golden scenario and test is written against it — HR4, HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] grade() takes a required sixth argument `context` {priorSpendCents, attribution?, scope?}, hashed with the other inputs. build_spec §7's five-argument signature cannot make cumulative ceilings or UNASKED pure — HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Receipt ids derive from the receipt's subject (claim id, orphan observation id, or agent+scope), not from inputsHash (which is stored). With inputsHash ids every coverage ping and grace alarm minted a new receipt, PENDING→FINAL could not update one row, and orphans were double-counted. Idempotency (ledger_engineer rule 8) is kept: same subject → same row — HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] No grade is decided in floating point. The loader turns weights, thresholds and unasked weights into integer basis points (rejecting values that are not multiples of 0.0001); feature values are exact rationals; threshold and tie comparisons cross-multiply in BigInt. The review showed one mandatory scenario scoring 0.5 or 0.49999999999999994 depending on summation order — HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Rule order changes from §7: over-scope rules (type, allowlist, ceiling, cumulative, observed scopes) come before no_coverage, because they read only the claim, the grant and assigned evidence; HR5 forbids VERIFIED/NOT_FOUND without sight, not OVER_SCOPE on the agent's own claim. Without this, a $200 claimed purchase against a $50 ceiling with no receipt email was UNVERIFIABLE (red-team strategy 11) — HR5, HR6.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] NOT_FOUND requires coverage 'full' explicitly, and graceElapsed requires every relevant observer to have synced after the whole claim window could have happened (≥ claimedAt + graceMin, and for non-calendar types ≥ window end). `pending` sits before `partial_coverage`. Coverage-based UNVERIFIABLE stays PENDING until finalizeUnverifiableHours (chosen 24) so a late Gmail visit still yields a real answer — HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] New rules: unasked, over_scope_observed, over_allowlist, over_ceiling_cumulative, no_target, underspecified, partial_coverage. `underspecified` closes a CRITICAL hole: a claim with no counterparty and no subject ("Done! I've sent the email") was VERIFIED by any email minutes later on time proximity alone — HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] New feature counterpartyName (chosen weight 0.25): agents name people ("sent email to Priya"), observers see addresses; it matches name tokens against the address local part only, never the attacker-controlled display name. Without it the §2 demo email could not reach VERIFIED. A display-name spoof `"Priya" <attacker@evil.tld>` stays below verified — HR5, red-team strategy 3.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Confusables are flagged from the raw address (any code point > U+007F, or an xn-- label) before any normalisation; addresses are never NFKC-normalised and only ASCII A–Z are lowercased (NFKC maps 𝐩 and fullwidth ｐ to p; toLowerCase maps the Kelvin sign to k). Plus-tags are reported (reason cp.plus_tag), not erased, so priya+x@ never VERIFIES priya@ (red-team strategy 3) — HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Claim gains targetAt (claimed event start, required for calendar.*; without it rule no_target) and claimedAtPrecision (minute | hour | day, so "Yesterday" widens the window instead of producing a false NOT_FOUND). Observation gains rowKeyHash (hash of the vendor row id) so a created and a deleted event, or two identical sends in one minute, stay distinct. Grant gains spendCurrency and accounts (which Gmail/Calendar account hashes belong to the agent) — HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Receipt gains agent, policyHash, attribution ('likely', on UNASKED), override, and structured reasons {code: ReasonCode, params}. Reasons never carry an address, a name, a subject or a formatted local time: receipts stay identical across time zones and locales, and shared receipts cannot leak PII — HR2, HR4, HR7, HR10.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Coverage eligibility: an entry counts only if its source is relevant, lastSyncAt ≤ evaluatedAt, and its account is the agent's (grant.accounts) — with no binding, an entry without an account hash or a source seen on ≥ 2 accounts counts at most as partial; a calendar view without all calendars counts at most as partial (red-team strategy 7). Observers emit coverage only for passes whose health() was OK — HR5, HR6.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Purchase coverage: a purchase-like claim whose counterparty is not in policy.receiptMerchants has coverage 'none'. The list starts empty and Sprint 2.3 adds each merchant as its receipt parser lands, so no purchase is ever NOT_FOUND because a parser was missing — HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Assignment is one-to-one and batched: a batch is every PENDING claim plus FINAL claims as fixed pre-assignments; gradeBatch is the only path from the background to receipts (Sprint 3.6 re-grading uses it, never grade() per claim) — HR4, red-team strategy 1.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Attribution scores each agent separately, needs positive idle evidence (missing data is never idle), defines "explained" as a same-type claim by that agent within ±adjacentMin, and keeps UNASKED PENDING until that agent's adapter has synced past occurredAt + beforeClaimMin + graceMin — HR7.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Replay rebuilds candidates and assignment as of each receipt's computedAt and takes coverage, grant and context from a receiptInputs side table keyed by inputsHash (derived data only). The ledger must store it (Sprint 2.6/3.6), append coverage history and keep grant versions — HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] USER_OVERRIDDEN can be reverted to its previous state (Sprint 4.3 says overrides are reversible), a deviation from "terminal" in task 1.6 and ledger_engineer rule 5; an overridden receipt is never regraded.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] src/core gains units.ts, hash.ts, sha256.ts, base32.ts, punycode.ts, conditions.ts, receipt.ts, batch.ts beside the §6 list, to keep modules acyclic and the algorithm constants in files exempted (with a reason each) from the no-literal test; every other core file stays {0, 1}-only and imports named units — HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Chosen policy values: toleranceMin 1 (Gmail times are minute-truncated), spendWindowDays 30, finalizeUnverifiableHours 24, counterpartyName 0.25, sessionMin 10, idleMin 2, commonDomains (11 free-mail domains), a 21-word English stop-word list, unasked.signatures empty for every agent until captured fixtures show real ones (never invented).
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] v1.1 (second adversarial review, 7 blockers): counterpartyDomain counts a free-mail domain when the addresses are exact or differ only by a +tag (otherwise an exact gmail.com match could never be VERIFIED); address equality is code-point identity after ASCII-only lowercasing, with no NFC (NFC folds the Kelvin, Ohm and Angstrom signs); feature applicability is decided by the claim alone (an observation that omits a field scores 0, never "not applicable", so it cannot make matching easier) — HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Only assignable claims take part in assignment: underspecified claims, calendar claims without targetAt, and types with no sources are never scored against observations, so a vague claim can neither steal evidence from a specific claim nor hide an orphan from UNASKED (red-team strategy 10) — HR5, HR7.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] grade() sets state and finalAt; finality lives in conditions.ts (isFinalizable); state.ts only applies events (regrade, override, revert, supersede). VERIFIED finalises once every relevant source synced at end(p,t) + graceMin; PARTIAL/NOT_FOUND need the whole window; OVER_SCOPE from claim-only facts is FINAL at once, OVER_SCOPE that depended on assigned evidence waits like PARTIAL; pending/no_coverage/partial_coverage finalise as UNVERIFIABLE after finalizeUnverifiableHours (reason coverage.never_completed) so no receipt stays "checking" forever or drops out of counts — HR4, HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] amountAboveCeiling/cumulativeAboveCeiling apply only to policy.spendTypes with a ceiling and a known amount; Grant.spendCurrency is required when a ceiling is set. A literal reading of v1 made every email claim OVER_SCOPE once a currency was set — HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Claim.rawHash = sha256(canonicalJson({agent, sourceKind, rowKeyHash or null, normalised text, claimedAt truncated to its precision unit})): identical claim lines at different times stay distinct (red-team strategy 1, claim inflation) while one line re-rendered on every page load stays one claim (strategy 9) — HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] UNASKED receipts can be superseded (event supersede) when a claim is later assigned their observation; a PENDING UNASKED that re-evaluates to anything else is deleted; superseded receipts leave counts and alerts. FINAL and overridden receipts are fixed pre-assignments in every batch — HR7.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Coverage without an account binding: NOT_FOUND carries reason coverage.account_unbound; a user who only ever opens the wrong Gmail account still gets NOT_FOUND. Accepted and listed for docs/attacks-that-work.md (red-team strategy 7); Sprint 3 settings adapters record the agent's account hash so grant.accounts can be filled — HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Sprint 3.5 amended: adapter content scripts also record user activity so the background can record idle intervals; userIdle needs positive evidence, and the observer tabs may be closed when an agent acts (otherwise the §2 demo's UNASKED could not fire live) — HR7.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] Reasons are a closed ReasonCode enum with id/timestamp/number/enum params (docs/grading.md §15); golden scenarios are JSON files with short refs, a policyPatch, exact "p/q" scores and reason codes, built into schema-valid objects by a loader so no id or hash is typed by hand — HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] The core no-literal test exempts sha256.ts, base32.ts, punycode.ts, units.ts and schema.ts by name, each with a reason; the policy-fraction check still covers every file with no exemption.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] v1.1 final review: READY after three fixes — golden loader defaults (observation observedAt = first covering sync, claim text = ref), FINAL UNASKED observations stay assignable (only overrides fix them), and an explicit reason-code emission table so golden reasonCodes are decidable. Also: counterpartyDomain = 1 whenever counterpartyExact = 1 (identical non-ASCII addresses can verify), precision units are closed intervals [start, start + unit], types with no sources finalise UNVERIFIABLE immediately.
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Repos take the schemas' input types and return branded output types. Fixtures build input objects; tests parse when they need branded values (row builders). Reason: the ledger parses at every write, and a plain string should not need a cast to be offered for validation — HR2
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Cross-field checks live in a leaf module, src/core/schema-checks.ts, that imports only zod types. Reason: schema.ts must hold every numeric literal (the no-literal exemption names schema.ts only), and a split that imports SCHEMA_LIMITS back from schema.ts would create a runtime ESM cycle — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Ulid accepts only the uppercase Crockford form (z.ulid() plus an uppercase regex). Reason: idFromHash emits uppercase, and a lowercase twin would be a different IndexedDB key and a different canonical-JSON hash for the same id — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Currency is /^[A-Z]{3}$/, tightened from §7's length(3). Reason: amountExact compares currencies for equality, and 'usd' and 'USD' must not silently differ — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] IsoTs is capped at 35 characters (nanosecond precision) as SCHEMA_LIMITS.ISO_TS. Reason: Zod's datetime regex allows unbounded fractional digits, which would be an uncapped string in storage and exports — HR2 (SECURITY-4)
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] New caps: TEXT_HINT 200 and SUBJECT_HINT 80 (moved from literals), ISO_TS 35, AMOUNT_CENTS 10^12 (also used for spendCeilingCents), ACCOUNTS 32 per source, REASON_PARAMS 8, GRADING_INPUT 10,000 (Coverage.observers and each Activity list). Reason: every array and string capped — HR2 (SECURITY-4)
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Reason params: keys must be identifiers (/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*$/, at most 64 characters); values are finite numbers or token strings (/^[A-Za-z0-9_.:+-]+$/, at most 64 characters). The token rule rejects '@' and spaces, so an address, a full name or a formatted local time cannot ride in a reason. Signal keys use the same identifier rule — HR2, HR10
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] The Receipt invariants go beyond the two the task lists. `attribution` is allowed only on UNASKED, matching §2/§13 ('likely', on UNASKED), which is stricter than the literal iff. `supersededBy` is allowed only on UNASKED (§8). `finalAt` is present iff the state before any override is FINAL (§3: finalAt is the evaluatedAt of the first FINAL grading). Each violation is reported at the offending field's path — HR4, HR7
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] ruleId on a receipt is a non-empty snake_case RuleId (the same pattern as policy rule ids) — HR10 (rule ids are printed in logs)
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] ReceiptOverride.reason must contain a non-space character (/\S/), not merely min(1). Reason: §8 says the override reason is required and non-empty, and a blank reason is empty in substance — HR4 (replay shows a real reason)
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] ObserverCoverage rejects windowEnd before windowStart (compared as instants via new Date(value).getTime()) and allCalendars on a non-gcal source. I did not require windowEnd ≤ lastSyncAt, because a calendar view can show future events — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Attribution requires 1 to 4 distinct signals, at least one agent-specific (userIdle alone never attributes, §7), and scoreBp is an integer in [0, BASIS_POINTS] — HR7
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] GradeContext.priorSpendCents is an integer ≥ 0 with no 10^12 cap: it is a sum of capped amounts, not an amount. z.number().int() already rejects unsafe integers — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Policy is not defined in schema.ts. The sprint_1 1.0 line lists Policy, but the contract §13 places Policy in policy.ts (loadPolicy), and the §13 schema.ts row omits it; per the brief, the contract wins
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] There is no Dexie migration for the receipt shape change: nothing has shipped, and any pre-1.0 receipt or claim row fails the strict schema and is skipped by readRow and logged by id (fail loud). The [agent+date] index spec is unchanged because agent is now a domain field — HR6
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] The ledger export sorts the keys of every record field (receipt signals, reason params, grant accounts). Reason: Zod keeps record keys in input order, and the export must stay byte-deterministic — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] The ledger schema-limits spec now covers only the ledger-only shapes, the bus batches and the re-export identity. The core caps moved to tests/unit/core/schema-limits.spec.ts, so each cap is tested once
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Each table-driven core spec has its own copy of a small runner (issuePaths plus describe.each, about 15 lines, in schema.spec.ts and schema-grading.spec.ts). Reason: the only files I may create in tests/unit/core are schema*.spec.ts, and importing one spec from another would re-register its tests
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] The override reason must contain a visible character: not whitespace and not a Unicode format mark (\p{Cf}: zero-width space, bidi marks, BOM). A reason made only of invisible marks is blank in substance, and replay must show a real reason (§8). Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Kept the implementer's TOKEN rule for reason param values instead of a closed union (Ulid | Sha256 | IsoTs | RuleId | core enum values). The union would reject plausible params (currency codes, feature names) before grade.ts exists, which would block the next wave. The gap is logged as a contract issue proposing closed params per code. Protects HR2 and HR10 without breaking HR4 producers.
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Corrected the ISO_TS comment instead of capping fraction digits at 9. The cap bounds storage, and no consumer depends on timestamp precision. A second regex would also raise two issues at the same path for one bad value. Protects HR2.
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Strictness is proven twice: structurally, by walking every exported schema and failing on unknown container kinds, and behaviourally, with unknown-key rows. A table can miss a nested object; the walker cannot. Protects HR2.
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] The ledger-only shapes (CoveragePing, MetaEntry) get their own 5 valid / 5 invalid table in tests/unit/ledger/storage-shapes.spec.ts, so 'every schema' holds for every schema src/ledger/types.ts exports. Protects HR2.
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Accepted the implementer's stricter-than-literal Receipt invariants: attribution and supersededBy only on UNASKED, and finalAt iff the state before any override is FINAL. They match §2, §3 and §8 and the [1.contract] log ('attribution ('likely', on UNASKED)'). Protects HR4 and HR7.
- 2026-10-01 [1.0] Did not split the pre-existing >60-line describe callbacks in Sprint 0 ledger and bus specs. They are test containers that already exceeded 60 lines at HEAD, and splitting them was not needed for task 1.0. Reported as a residual issue (tech_reviewer rule). Hard rule: none.
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] Split the module into normalize-text.ts, normalize-amount.ts, normalize-time.ts and normalize-relative.ts, with normalize.ts as the barrel carrying the §13 exports — keeps every file under the 400-line cap with no change to purity — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] sameAddress(a, b) takes parsed EmailAddress objects (callers run email() first) — a non-address can never silently compare as 'false' — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] plusTagOnly is computed independently of confusable (both can be true); features.ts decides which reason wins — the comparison reports structural facts only — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] nonAscii follows the contract literally (exact and has a non-ASCII code point), so identical xn-- addresses are exact with no flag (see contractIssues) — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] email() rejects quoted local parts, domain literals, comments, several addresses, whitespace or control characters, empty or doubled dots, and single-label domains. It accepts non-ASCII (RFC 6531) but flags it confusable — reject rather than guess — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] The display name is the text before the final <...> when the input ends with '>'; it is discarded — it is attacker-controlled (red-team strategy 3) — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] counterparty(): returns null when the NFKC text contains @, < or > but is not a valid address; returns {kind:'name', tokens:[]} for text with no tokens — a broken address is never matched as a name — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] name(): strip Cf and Default_Ignorable characters, NFKC, toLowerCase, NFD, remove only \p{Mn}, NFC, collapse whitespace. Spacing and enclosing marks (Mc/Me, e.g. Devanagari vowel signs) stay inside tokens, which split on [^\p{L}\p{N}\p{M}]. A final NFC instead of NFKC keeps name() idempotent (property-tested) — folding fewer marks means fewer false name matches — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] nameTokens() returns distinct tokens in order of first appearance; subjectTokens() returns a set sorted by UTF-16 code unit, and normalises stop words with NFKC and lowercase before comparing — deterministic output — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] Amount separators: with both '.' and ',' present, the last one is the decimal point and appears once. A lone '.' or ',' is decimal if 1..exponent digits follow, grouping if it splits a well-formed group of three, and null if both readings fit. Spaces and apostrophes only group. Grouped integers may be Western or Indian and never start with 0. NFKC and an invisible-character strip run first. BigInt arithmetic; null above Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER — never guess an amount — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] Currency tokens: ISO codes in any ASCII case, plus us$ ca$ a$ au$ nz$ mx$ r$ hk$ s$ sg$ € £ ¥(JPY) cn¥ ₹ ₩. C$ (CAD or NIO) and kr (SEK, NOK, DKK, ISK) are deliberately unmapped. '$10 USD' takes the suffix when it is a dollar-sign currency; any other prefix/suffix disagreement is null; a bare $ without defaultDollar is null — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] CURRENCY_MINOR_UNITS in units.ts: the required list plus CNY, HKD and SGD (all 2), needed by the symbol table, frozen. Also added DAYS_PER_WEEK, HOURS_PER_HALF_DAY and DECIMAL_RADIX so the other core files stay literal-free — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] instantMs accepts exactly the IsoTs (zod z.iso.datetime({offset:true})) grammar: seconds required; Z or ±hh:mm with hh ≤ 23. Dates are validated by a round trip through setUTCFullYear, because Date.UTC misreads years 0–99. Sub-millisecond digits are truncated with BigInt. Property-tested against zod and Date — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] isoWithOffset always writes ±hh:mm (never Z) and always writes seconds, adding .sss only when the milliseconds are non-zero. It throws NormalizeError for a non-integer instant, an offset of 24 h or more, or a local year outside 0000–9999 — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] DST: wallToInstant uses the RFC 5545 / Temporal 'compatible' rule with the injected offsetAt (a repeated hour gives the earlier instant; a skipped hour is shifted forward by the gap). resolveRelative then widens a clock time that is not exact to day precision at the start of that local day — a DST hour can never produce a false NOT_FOUND — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] A clock-only time ('2:03 PM') resolves to today unless that instant is more than one displayed minute after the reference (clock-skew slack = MS_PER_MINUTE, the display precision, not a policy value); otherwise yesterday. This handles a stale page read after midnight — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] Weekday names resolve to the most recent such day strictly before today (1–7 days back), so 'Thu' read on a Thursday means last week; returning null when the weekday is today was the alternative considered — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] 'N min/hours ago' uses floor semantics: the range is [ref − (N+1)·unit, ref − N·unit]. The result's iso is the range start, at the finest aligned precision (in that instant's offset) that contains the whole range, or null if no single local day does. Vendors that round are still covered because the claim window adds afterClaimMin. 'N days ago' means calendar days, like 'Yesterday' — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] Month-day without a year means the latest such date not after the reference date (today included). With an explicit year the date may be in the future (event starts). A weekday plus an explicit date must agree, else null — reject rather than guess — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] resolveRelative also accepts 'Today', 'at', a.m./p.m., and Chrome's narrow NBSP before AM/PM; it is English only and rejects seconds. A non-integer or ≥ 24 h value from offsetAt throws NormalizeError('invalid_offset'); a bad referenceIso throws invalid_iso; text that would land outside years 0000–9999 returns null — fail loud — HR6
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] punycode.ts: RFC 3492 §6.2 decoder with overflow checks at 2^31−1; it rejects results in the surrogate range or above U+10FFFF. toUnicodeDomain shows an invalid ACE label exactly as read; decoding is never used for comparison — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] Typed errors: NormalizeError (invalid_iso | invalid_instant | invalid_offset) and PunycodeError (invalid_input | overflow) — typed error rule — HR6
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] TimePrecision ('minute'|'hour'|'day') is declared in normalize-relative.ts, structurally identical to schema ClaimPrecision, rather than importing schema.ts, which another agent is still editing — avoids coupling to in-progress work — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] All non-ASCII test inputs and code literals are written as \u escapes (comments keep visible glyphs); no raw bidi or zero-width characters exist in source — Trojan-Source safety, no hidden content in code — HR9/HR10
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] Amounts, vendor-relative times and name counterparties containing a bidi override (U+202D/U+202E) are rejected (null), not stripped: an override makes the displayed order differ from the stored order ('‮01$' shows '$10'). Marks (LRM/RLM/ALM) and isolates are still stripped, because they do not reorder letters or digits and number formatting emits them around amounts — HR5
