- 2026-10-01 [1.1] Amounts and vendor-relative times containing any \p{No} character (superscript, subscript, circled, fraction digits) are rejected before NFKC: NFKC turns '$9⁹⁹' into '$999'. Nd digits (fullwidth, mathematical) are still folded, because they display as the digit they mean — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] email(): a display name that still contains '<' or '>' after its quoted strings are removed makes the input null (lists such as '<a>, <b>' and nested forms such as '"P" <evil<p>'). An unquoted '@' in a display name is still allowed ('priya@company.com <attacker@evil.tld>' -> attacker@evil.tld, display name dropped) because DOM text often shows the address as the name — HR5, red-team strategy 3
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] email(): the +tag separator is the first '+' after a non-empty base; a leading '+' belongs to the base, so '+a@x' vs '+b@x' is not a plus-tag pair (no base mailbox to tag) — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] counterparty(): an address that contains an override is still returned (it is already confusable, so it never matches); only a NAME with an override is null, so a vague or spoofed claim falls to underspecified/UNVERIFIABLE instead of matching a different mailbox — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] name(): whitespace is JS \s plus Unicode White_Space (NEL), collapsed to one space — HR4 (deterministic, consistent with nameTokens)
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] readsDifferently() lives in normalize-text.ts and is not re-exported from the normalize.ts barrel: it is an internal guard shared by amount and relative, not part of the §13 contract — HR4 (smallest surface)
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] Kept the implementer's reviewed choices (strictly-past weekday, floor-range 'N min/hours ago', calendar-day 'N days ago', 'compatible' DST with day widening, lone-separator ambiguity rejection, ¥ = JPY) and raised the open ones as contractIssues rather than deviating — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.1] Test tables are module-level constants so every function stays <= 60 lines; every non-ASCII test input is a \u escape (no raw bidi or zero-width characters in source) — tech_reviewer checklist, HR9/HR10 (Trojan-Source safety)
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] All seven windows and the three unasked.windows values must be integers >= 0 (Zod int, so also safe integers). Reason: windows become integer ms and timeProximity is an exact BigInt rational (§5.1, §5.3); a fractional minute like 0.1 gives 6000.000000000001 ms. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Conversion to basis points: bp = Math.round(x*10000), accepted only if bp/10000 === x. This accepts exactly the nearest doubles to 4-place decimals and rejects 0.1+0.2 and 0.00005. bp values are numbers branded `BasisPoints`, not bigint, so the loaded policy can be frozen and serialised; ratio.ts converts them to BigInt. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] The `bp` section has the same layout as the document (bp.features, bp.thresholds, bp.unasked.signals, bp.unasked.threshold). policyHash is computed from the parsed document only, never from bp, and is branded with schema Sha256. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] `version` stays an integer >= 1 instead of being pinned to 2. Pinning needs the literal 2 in core, which the no-literal rule forbids outside exempt files. The v2 shape (strict, every key required) already rejects v1 documents. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Checks that compare fields run in a top-level superRefine. Under Zod's default rule they run only when the document is well typed, and they compare integers (bp, ms). The partial < verified check moved there and is now done in bp. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Every list in the policy rejects duplicates ("duplicate \"x\" (first at [i])"), keeps its written order, and is hashed in that order, so reordering gives a new policyHash. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Vocabularies come from core ./schema, not hand-written lists. ObservationFieldName = Observation.keyof() (includes rowKeyHash). RuleOutcome = Grade.options + PENDING. Rule ids use schema RuleId, so every policy rule id is a valid Receipt.ruleId (at most 64 chars). Signal keys use UnaskedSignal. Per-type maps are z.record(ActionType, …), signatures z.record(AgentId, …), allowlistedFields z.record(ObservationSource, …), so a missing key reports "is required" and an extra key reports "unknown key". Hard Rules 2 and 4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] commonDomains entries must be lowercase ASCII hostnames with at least 2 labels; each label uses only letters, digits and hyphens and does not start or end with a hyphen. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] receiptMerchants entries must be lowercase, trimmed, single-spaced and free of format characters. That is the shape of a §11 normalised name that can be checked without importing normalize.ts. Hard Rule 5 (a non-normalised entry could never match, which is the safe direction, but it fails loud instead).
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] stopWords entries must be a single token of letters and digits that is unchanged by NFKC and by lowercasing, so subjectTokens can actually produce it. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Each unasked.signatures entry must contain a letter or digit. A signature that is empty or only punctuation/whitespace would match almost any subject and inflate UNASKED. Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Module split: policy.ts is the public API (loadPolicy, DEFAULT_POLICY, the PolicyDocument schema, BasisPoints, the Policy and PolicyBasisPoints types, and re-exports). The internals are policy-schema.ts (per-field schemas), policy-checks.ts (checks across fields) and policy-errors.ts (PolicyError and the path/message formatting). Other core modules should import only from './policy'.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] The Zod schema export `Policy` was renamed to `PolicyDocument` (schema and document type). `Policy` is now only the loaded type (document plus bp plus policyHash). Nothing outside the policy tests imported the old value.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Old test files were split to respect the 400-line limit: policy.spec.ts, policy-hash.spec.ts, policy-errors.spec.ts, policy-errors-lists.spec.ts, policy-shape.spec.ts. core-tsconfig.spec.ts needed no change (it still passes).
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Every window (windows.* and unasked.windows.*) is capped so that its length in milliseconds is a safe integer, floor((2^53 − 1) / ms per unit) in its own unit. Reason: §5.1/§5.3 exact rationals need integer ms, and a double past 2^53 is rounded. This extends the implementer's whole-units decision. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Basis points are normalised with Math.abs so that a document weight written -0 becomes bp 0. The document part keeps -0 as written, and the hash is unchanged because canonicalJson writes -0 as 0. Reason: bp values must be plain non-negative integers. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] The top-level label of a commonDomains entry must start with a letter (RFC 3696 §2), so IPv4 literals are rejected while 163.com and xn-- TLDs still load. DNS label and name lengths are not enforced, because a bogus entry only makes matching stricter (the safe direction). Hard Rule 6 (fail loud).
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Kept the implementer's unpinned `version` (int >= 1) and listed it as residual, not a defect. Pinning to 2 needs a literal or a constant in an exempt file owned by someone else. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Kept the implementer's other decisions after re-verifying them: only multiples of 0.0001 are accepted, with an exhaustive 10,001-value check; partial < verified is compared in bp; lists must be unique and are hashed in order; vocabularies come from ./schema; policyHash is computed from the document only, never from bp. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.policy] Split test describe blocks rather than raising any limit, to meet the 60-line function rule. Added a near-valid mutation property test because the existing fc.jsonValue totality test never gets far enough to run the cross-field checks.
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] observationIds = the assigned candidate only (best candidate with score ≥ thresholds.partial), else []; a NOT_FOUND whose best candidate is below 1/2 still carries match.best, its score and its cp.* reasons (18–23, 30) — simplest reading consistent with 'override to NOT_FOUND has empty observationIds' and fixed pre-assignments — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] gradeBatch keeps claim-less receipts only when they are UNASKED or an undeclared observed scope (§1); orphans below the threshold or with no agent signal yield no receipt, and batch `expect` is the exact receipt set — HR7
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] message.sent / other are graded by no_coverage, FINAL at once (§8 row), not underspecified, despite empty identity lists (45) — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] allowlist.unchecked sits in the rule-reason group (after the rule's own code, before coverage and match codes) (37) — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] ceiling.currency_mismatch replaces ceiling.exceeded when only the currency differs (34) — HR5
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] override.user and superseded are appended after all graded reasons; override/supersede are expressed as scenario `events` applied with transition() so these codes are covered (56, 57) — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] An observation fixed as taken by an overridden UNASKED receipt is excluded from every claim's candidates, like one fixed to a claim (32) — HR7
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] Claims named in `fixed` are FINAL/overridden: not passed to gradeBatch, no expectation; Activity.claims defaults to every scenario claim — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] rowKey labels become rowKeyHash = SHA-256(label); contentHash includes rowKeyHash whenever present, even if a policy's allowlist omitted it, so ids do not depend on the policy version — HR2, HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] rawHash truncates claimedAt to an ISO string in the claim's own offset ('YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm:ss±hh:mm', ms only when non-zero) — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] Grade mode: a claim-less call names `agent` for the grant, and context.attribution = attribute(candidates[0], activity, policy) when activity is given (57) — HR4, HR7
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] Where an expectation depends on hash-derived ids (claim-id and observation-id tie-breaks, contentHash dedupe), the file states it in assertIds and the spec verifies it; labels were chosen so ids sort in ref order (31, 58, 11) — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] All ids are pinned in __snapshots__/ids.json via vitest toMatchFileSnapshot (written locally, verified under CI=true) — HR4
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] Signature-based attribution uses a scenario-only policyPatch; the shipped signatures stay empty (never invented) (49) — HR7
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] Added scenarios only where a plausible broken core would otherwise pass, and kept them off readings the contract leaves open: lowercase local parts, receiptMerchants entries already normalised, the UNASKED 24 h check not on the exact boundary, no candidate in another currency for an amount-less claim. Reason: the golden files are the spec, and a new file must not bake in an unreviewed reading — HR4, HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] 65 reuses the first author's reading 5 (ceiling.currency_mismatch replaces ceiling.exceeded) so the suite stays consistent; it is still listed as a contract issue — HR5.
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] Did not write the golden runner (out of scope for this wave) and did not edit Decisions.md, docs/grading.md, build_spec.md or sprint files. New contract questions are reported in contractIssues only — HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.golden] Accepted the first author's loader choices (observerId defaults to source; rowKeyHash always enters contentHash when present; claimedAt truncated in the claim's own offset). They match §10/§15 where those sections are specific and are pinned by loader.spec.ts and the ids snapshot — HR4.
- 2026-10-01 [1.contract] v1.2: about 30 clarifications raised while implementing wave A folded into docs/grading.md (claimedAtEnd for DST-safe day precision; closed params per ReasonCode enforced by the schema; uppercase-only ULIDs and currencies; Coverage/Activity capped at SCHEMA_LIMITS.GRADING_INPUT; receipt invariants; observationIds definition; gradeBatch's claim-less outputs; identity features must have weight > 0; PENDING only on rule pending; ¥ = JPY and ambiguous separators rejected; relative-time rules; subjects keep combining marks and strip zero-width/bidi marks; text with bidi overrides or NFKC-changing digits is not read; allowlist entry casing; candidate-currency ceiling hole closed; receiptMerchants matched by normalised name). Sprint 1 task lines 1.0 and 1.1 now point at the contract where their v0 wording disagreed — HR4, HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] Ratio is a plain {num, den} BigInt pair, not a branded type, matching §13's literal shape. Every function rejects a hand-built value whose den is not positive with RatioError('invalid_denominator'). ratio() takes bigint or safe-integer numbers and rejects anything else with 'not_an_integer' rather than rounding. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] RatioError codes: invalid_denominator, not_an_integer, invalid_weight (negative or non-integer weight or bp), zero_weight_sum. weightedScore throws zero_weight_sum for an empty list or all-zero weights instead of returning 0. Protects HR5 (a missing score is never made up).
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] weightedScore keeps its running sum reduced, so the result is the same reduced Ratio in any term order. The property test checks it against an independent copy of the §5.3 formula. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] toDisplay rounds half away from zero to 4 decimals (one basis point), computed in BigInt; it never returns -0. Display only, never compared. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] Also exported RATIO_ZERO and RATIO_ONE (frozen) and the types WeightedTerm and RatioErrorCode beyond the §13 list. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] FeatureClaim, FeatureObservation and FeaturePolicy are structural types that parsed Claim/Observation and a loaded Policy satisfy. Their optional fields accept an explicit undefined, because Zod types optional output fields as T | undefined. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] features.ts builds a WindowClaim view with absent fields left out before calling claimWindow. window.ts's WindowClaim optionals reject undefined, so a parsed Claim would not type-check against it directly. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] timeProximity uses claimWindow's `anchor` and edge lengths, so calendar (targetAt) and non-calendar claims share one code path. The value is 1 while Δ ≤ toleranceMin and 0 at or past the edge, so the divisor is always positive. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] When a pair is both confusable and a +tag-only difference, the only reason is cp.confusable: 'same mailbox, different tag' is an identity claim a confusable address cannot support. Raised as a contract issue. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] cp.non_ascii is emitted for an identical pair whenever the address is confusable (any non-ASCII code point or an xn-- label), following §5.1 and the task text, not normalize's narrower `nonAscii` flag. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] cp.* reasons come only from the address features (counterpartyExact/Domain). A name claim never gets cp.* reasons, matching golden 54's note. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] amountExact applies when the claim has amountCents; a claim currency is not required to apply it, but without one the feature always scores 0. This matches §5.2's 'lacks its field' and golden 63. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] merchantMatch applies when counterparty(raw) is not null and nameTokens(raw) is non-empty. The observation merchant goes through the same check, so a broken address or bidi-override text never matches as a name (Decisions [1.1]). Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] isAssignable = the type has sources AND claimWindow is not null (window.ts returns null exactly for a calendar claim without targetAt) AND not underspecified; the claim facts are computed once. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] signals contain only the applicable features plus 'score', in the policy's featuresByType order. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] Golden cross-check level 2 goes beyond the task's single-claim check: it derives each claim's candidates from the expectations themselves (other claims' observationRefs and fixed pairs excluded, observedAt ≤ evaluatedAt, the calendar.created first-seen rule). It also checks the best candidate's cp.* codes, taking the first by (occurredAt, id) among equal scores. Protects HR4 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] Each spec defines its own small builders, because I can create only *.spec.ts files and a spec must not import another spec (same reason as Decisions [1.0]). Every non-ASCII test input is a \u escape; § and · appear only in test titles.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] All mutation testing ran in a scratch mirror (copies of policy/, src/ and tests/, with node_modules symlinked), never in the shared tree, so concurrent agents' test runs could not see a mutant.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] Left features() throwing RatioError('zero_weight_sum') when no applicable feature has weight, rather than widening isAssignable or isUnderspecified. Making the claim non-assignable while not underspecified could let it reach NOT_FOUND with no candidates (breaks HR5), and calling it underspecified departs from §5.2's definition. Failing loud (HR6) plus a loader fix (contract issue) is the simplest option that keeps the Hard Rules.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] Wrote the new fail-loud test to accept either outcome (loader rejects the policy, or features() throws). It pins 'never a made-up score' without breaking when the policy owner adds the check.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] Kept visible math and section glyphs (§ · ≥ Δ Σ) in test titles, as the existing ratio and features specs do. Only inputs (confusable, invisible, bidi and combining code points) must be \u escapes under Decisions [1.1].
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] Accepted the implementer's choices as consistent with the contract and golden files: cp.confusable replaces cp.plus_tag for a confusable +tag pair; merchant text that counterparty() rejects has no tokens; amountExact applies on claim amountCents alone; cp.* reasons come only from the address features. Carried them forward as contract issues for the contract owner rather than changing behaviour.
- 2026-10-02 [1.4] Did not edit golden files, Decisions.md, sprint files, docs/grading.md or any file outside the 1.4 ownership patterns. Every probe and tool lived in scratch, and I moved my tools into a private scratch subdirectory after another agent overwrote shared scratch filenames (fnlen.cjs, mutate.mjs). The mutation run had already loaded its script, so its results are unaffected.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] subjectHint is cut to SCHEMA_LIMITS.SUBJECT_HINT (80) code points by iterating the string. A surrogate pair counts once and is never split; a lone surrogate counts as one. This is exactly how the pinned zod 4.6.5 string .max counts (checked in node_modules/zod/v4/core/checks.js), so the cut value always parses. contentHash covers the stored (cut) text, so two rows that store the same text are one observation. Protects HR2.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] Overlong counterparty (over 320) and merchant (over 120) are dropped, not truncated: a cut address or merchant would name someone else. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] An allowlisted value is validated with the Observation schema's own field schema (Observation.shape[field]) and stored exactly as given. Lowercase currency, numeric strings, fractional or negative amounts, wrong types and containers are dropped, never coerced. -0 passes the schema's int().min(0) and is kept; canonical JSON writes it as 0, so its hash and id equal those of 0. Protects HR2 and HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] rowKeyHash is computed only from ctx.rowKey and never read from raw. It is kept and hashed whenever a row key is given, even if a policy's allowlist omits rowKeyHash. This follows the existing golden decision ('so ids do not depend on the policy version') and §10 ('when the observer has one'), so two identical sends never merge. Protects HR4 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] id, source, type, observedAt and contentHash are always computed; an allowlist entry naming them never makes them readable from raw. Content fields = Observation keys minus those six plus rowKeyHash. The list is derived from the schema so a new Observation field cannot be silently ignored, and a unit test pins it to the six names. Protects HR2.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] Only own data properties of raw are read, via Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor: inherited (prototype) properties and getters never become content. A non-object raw (null, string, number) has no fields, so it ends in missing_occurred_at. Protects HR2.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] Two typed error codes only: invalid_argument (bad source, type, observedAt, a non-string rowKey, or an unknown ctx key, because ctx is a strict object so a misspelt rowKey fails loud) and missing_occurred_at (absent, invalid, or not allowlisted). Messages are 'redact: <code>', never a value. Protects HR6 and HR10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] Object schemas are parsed with { jitless: true }, as loadPolicy does, so parsing behaves the same in Node and under the extension's CSP. Protects HR9.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] The policy parameter is typed Pick<Policy, 'allowlistedFields'>: a full Policy fits, and the dependency is explicit. The import is type-only, keeping the §13 order (redact sits above policy) with no runtime dependency.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] hashText returns the branded Sha256 (Sha256.parse(sha256Hex(text))), so callers can store it without a cast.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] project does not check that type belongs to source per policy.sourcesByType, and does not cap rowKey length (it is hashed, never stored). Neither is in the contract; I chose the simplest option.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] Tests pass ill-typed values with z.custom<T>().parse(value) instead of casts (no 'as unknown as'). They are not tagged @mandatory, because tests/unit/harness/mandatory-guard.spec.ts pins that tag to exactly the two Playwright tests.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] The mutation check ran out-of-tree: a scratchpad vitest config aliased src/core/redact to mutated copies, so no shared file was changed while other agents were working.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] redact.ts left unchanged. Adversarial review found no smuggling path: only own data properties of the fixed CONTENT_FIELDS are read, through getOwnPropertyDescriptor, so prototype keys, getters, proxy get traps, symbols and array elements never become content. Every kept value is validated by its own Observation field schema and never coerced. Identity fields are computed from the arguments only. Errors carry a code only. The final Observation.parse cannot fail on validated parts. HR2, HR4, HR10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] Kept the implementer's -0 behaviour (kept as given, hashes and ids like 0) instead of normalising it, because the spec says 'dropped, never coerced' and -0 passes the schema. Pinned it with a test so it cannot change silently. HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] Kept hashText = sha256Hex(text) despite the lone-surrogate/U+FFFD collision, because task 1.2 and §13 pin that definition; reported it as a contract issue. Row keys from real vendors are ASCII ids, so the collision is theoretical. HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] Added 'Z' to the property test's safe alphabet, not a broader set, so the guard that no generated valid string can spell a seed still holds with one obvious extra character. HR2.
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] Rewrote test inputs with invisible or confusable characters as \u escapes, following Decision [1.1], and left visible §/→ glyphs in titles and comments as other core specs do. HR9, HR10 (Trojan-Source).
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] Ran every mutation out of tree through a scratchpad vitest alias, reusing my interrupted run's 39 mutants and adding 11, so no shared file changed while four other agents work in the tree. I stopped only my own stray vitest process (matched by its scratch spec path).
- 2026-10-02 [1.2] Classified the full-suite failures as non-owned and load-induced, not as redact defects: each failing test passes alone, and every one is a 5-second default timeout under load average about 100.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] The calendar.created first-seen bound is measured from start(p,t) − beforeClaimMin, not from the raw claimedAt instant. The two are identical for unit-aligned claims, which is what adapters (resolveRelative) and the golden loader produce. For a day-precision claim the raw instant would wrongly reject an event first seen that morning: a false NOT_FOUND. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] First-seen uses the earliest observedAt among every observation sharing the contentHash, not just the kept one. A later re-sighting of an event that was already on the calendar can never pass the rule. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Dedupe representative: lowest id, then earliest observedAt instant, then the smaller observedAt text. This is a total order, so the result never depends on the index's return order; candidates feed inputsHash. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Rows are filtered by type and window before grouping by contentHash. Equal contentHash implies equal occurredAt (it is hashed), so a row and its re-sightings are in or out of the window together. Protects HR4 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] claimedAtEnd must be strictly after claimedAt, else WindowError('end_not_after_claimed_at'). This matches the Claim schema's cross-field check, which another agent added during this run. Protects HR6 (fail loud).
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] WindowClaim is a structural type (type, claimedAt, claimedAtPrecision, claimedAtEnd?, targetAt?), with optional fields typed `string | undefined`. A parsed Claim is assignable to it, and so is a bare timing object in tests and the bench. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Every window bound must be a safe integer, else WindowError('unsafe_window'). A double past 2^53 would be rounded silently. Protects HR4 and HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] isCalendarType(type) is type.startsWith('calendar.') and is exported from window.ts, so features.ts and conditions.ts can share it. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] window.ts imports instantMs from normalize.ts, which sits on the same §13 tier. There is no cycle: normalize imports nothing of mine.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] The memory index widens every read outward to whole buckets of bucketMinutes. That is the over-return a bucketed Dexie store will have, so candidates() is always tested against it. Reads use per-type and per-token arrays sorted by (occurredAt ms, id) with binary search. bucketMinutes must be a positive whole number; bounds must be safe integers (typed CandidatesError). Protects HR4 and HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] A bad read range rejects the returned promise (a throw inside the Promise executor), as an async store's read would, rather than throwing synchronously. Protects HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] counterpartyTokens: the counterparty is read only through email(), so the display name is never indexed; it is attacker-controlled. Tokens are the full address, its domain and its local-part tokens split on [._-] without the +tag, all ASCII-lowercased, plus the merchant's name tokens. Distinct and sorted; looked up exactly as given. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] `occurredAt` is memoised per observation object in a module-level WeakMap (candidates-time.ts), warmed by index build. Parsing dominated lookup: 1.6 s down to about 0.44 s. The memo is keyed by object identity and weakly held: it never changes a result, the core purity lint does not flag it, and observations are immutable in core. observedAt is parsed only for calendar.created claims. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] assign takes an optional 4th parameter `taken: ReadonlySet<Ulid>` for observations fixed as taken by an overridden UNASKED receipt. The greedy pass never assigns them; a fixed pair is still kept as given. Protects HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Fixed pairs that are not one-to-one (one observation with two claims, or one claim with two observations) throw CandidatesError('fixed_conflict'). An identical repeated pair is harmless. A claimedAtMs that is not a safe integer throws 'invalid_pair'. Protects HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] assign returns its Map ordered by observation id, so iteration order is deterministic whatever the input order. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] exclusiveCandidates excludes a taken observation even from the claim it is fixed to: the literal §4.3 'minus … fixed as taken'. Protects HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] The bench workload lives in bench/core.ts (seeded mulberry32, no Math.random) and the gate test imports it, so the gate and the committed number measure exactly the same thing. bucketMinutes is 15 there: an index tuning parameter, not policy.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] The bench rebuilds the deterministic workload as fresh objects every run, so no run reuses parses from an earlier run. It reports lookup (the gate), index build, and build + lookup separately. Protects HR12.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] The bench result file is dated with the machine's local calendar date (2026-10-01 EDT; UTC was already 10-02), matching the Progress Log dates. The bench is not core and may read the clock.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] The perf gate test is not tagged @mandatory. That tag is pinned by tests/unit/harness/mandatory-guard.spec.ts to the two E2E tests. The gate fails the suite like any unit test (qa_tester rule 7).
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Mutation testing ran on an isolated scratchpad copy, never on the shared tree, so concurrent agents never saw mutated files.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Accepted the implementer's calendar.created first-seen bound of start(p,t) − beforeClaimMin, with the earliest observedAt across a contentHash group. It coincides with the literal §4.1 text for every unit-aligned claim, including every golden scenario, and avoids a false NOT_FOUND (HR5). It is carried as a contract issue rather than changed.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Ordering fix: contentHash is the final sort key in candidates(). This is the simplest way to make the order total without changing (occurredAt, id) for any honest ledger (HR4).
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Accepted the module-level WeakMap memo in candidates-time.ts. It is keyed by object identity, and each entry is used only while the field still holds the text it was parsed from, so it can never change a result. Lint purity rules pass, and it cuts the parse cost that dominates lookup.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Accepted the perf gate's CPU clock (process.cpuUsage over the lookup). The lookup is synchronous, single-threaded work with no I/O, and CPU time counts every thread of the process, so it never under-reports. Wall time is unusable as a gate on this machine: a parallel suite measured 470 ms of CPU against 1,577 ms of wall. The bench commits the wall-clock median and the CPU median side by side.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Deleted the untracked bench/results/core-2026-10-01.json instead of keeping it. The current bench code did not produce it, its metrics have a different shape, and RESULTS.md already renders only the newest file, so its content is unchanged (HR12).
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Kept the bench number measured under concurrent-agent load rather than waiting for an idle machine. It is a real measurement from the command named in the file, it passes the gate, and the CPU median shows the uncontended cost.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] Ran all mutation testing on an isolated scratchpad copy (outside the repository), so the shared tree was never mutated.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Reason params are enforced by a per-code strict schema run in a superRefine (ReasonParams: ReadonlyMap<ReasonCode, ZodType>), not a z.discriminatedUnion. The static type stays Record<string, string|number>, so src/ledger/export.ts (sortedRecord over params, not my file) keeps typechecking — HR2, HR10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] REASON_CODES and the §15 REASON_PARAM_KINDS table live in schema-checks.ts (a leaf, no import from ./schema). ReasonCode = z.enum(REASON_CODES), and the table is typed Record<code, …> so a missing or extra code fails compilation. Reason: schema.ts must stay ≤ 400 lines and I own only schema.ts and schema-checks.ts — HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Removed SCHEMA_LIMITS.REASON_PARAMS (8) and the TOKEN value regex: the closed per-code table subsumes both. The three schema-limits rows for them were replaced by closed-table tests — HR2.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Unknown params are reported one per key at ['params', key] ('not a param of <code>'); a missing or bad param at ['params', name] — HR6 (fail loud, exact paths).
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] The `source` param is optional on coverage.none/partial/account_unbound/calendars_hidden/multiple_accounts/no_account and grace.waiting (§15: omitted for a type with no sources); every other listed param is required — HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Param kinds: amountCents/ceilingCents = AmountCents (≤ 10^12); priorSpendCents = int ≥ 0, uncapped like GradeContext.priorSpendCents; currency/spendCurrency = Currency; override.user grade = Grade, as §15 says — HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] claimedAtEnd: only the specified refine (strictly after claimedAt, in ms; equal ms rejected). No upper bound, since the contract gives none (raised as a contract issue) — HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Identity weight-0 check compares rounded basis points, so a weight written -0 counts as 0; reported at identityFeatures[type][index] beside the existing 'not listed' check — HR4, HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] PENDING_RULE_ID = 'pending', exported from policy.ts. The check is one-directional as written: the 'pending' rule may conclude something else — HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Stop words may contain combining marks (\p{M}) and must not contain Default_Ignorable characters, matching the v1.2 subject tokenizer; the error message changed accordingly — HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] readsDifferently narrowed to the contract wording: a \p{No} character is unreadable only if NFKC changes it (Node reports 749 NFKC-stable \p{No} code points). This keeps name() idempotent. Amounts and times are unaffected because their parsers accept only ASCII digits after NFKC — HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] name() returns '' (so nameTokens returns []) for text that reads differently, and counterparty() returns null for such a name. This extends the [1.1] bidi-override rule to NFKC-changing digits ('Priya²' was 'priya2') — HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] subjectTokens strips Cf + Default_Ignorable once, before NFKC, as the contract orders. I checked over all code points that no visible character NFKC-maps to an invisible one. Subjects still NFKC-fold \p{No} digits, because §11 lists only the bidi override for subjects (raised as a contract issue) — HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] resolveRelative now returns {start, end, precision}, replacing {iso, precision}; no code outside my tests consumed it. For 'N min/hours ago', start is now the aligned unit start rather than the range start — HR4, HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Each returned instant is written in the offset in which it begins its unit: the alignment offset for minute/hour, and the pre-gap offset for a day whose midnight a DST gap swallowed. So §4.1/§10 truncation of claimedAt in its own offset gives claimedAt back. end is written in its own offset (a day's end as the next local 00:00) — HR4, HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] When an 'ago' range escalates to day precision it now uses the DST-correct local day instead of a fixed-offset 24 h, so ranges in the extra hour of a 25-hour day resolve instead of returning null — HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] A unit whose end cannot be written as a four-digit-year ISO timestamp (the last day of year 9999) returns null instead of claiming a unit with no end — HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Test files split to keep each under 400 lines: Reason cases moved to tests/unit/core/schema-reasons.spec.ts, resolveRelative cases to tests/unit/core/normalize-relative.spec.ts (each with its own copy of the zone helpers, since only *.spec.ts files are mine) — tech_reviewer limits.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Added an explicit 30 s timeout (PROPERTY_TIMEOUT_MS) to the policy-shape whole-domain property and the three resolveRelative properties. They run in 0.3–0.9 s alone but exceeded Vitest's 5 s default when the full suite ran in parallel with another agent's CPU-heavy perf test. Run counts unchanged — HR4 (tests must be reliable).
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] schema-checks.ts instantMs returns NaN for any string not ending in Z or ±hh:mm, and claimProblems skips the comparison on NaN. This is the simplest change that makes the cross-field checks independent of the host zone and reports a bad timestamp once, at its own path; for every valid IsoTs the result is unchanged, as the property test pins down (Hard Rules 4, 6).
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Kept Date-based parsing in schema-checks instead of importing normalize-time's strict parser: the existing property shows the two agree on every IsoTs-accepted string, V8 truncates sub-millisecond digits as normalize does, and keeping the module a leaf avoids a new dependency (Hard Rule 4).
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Did not make refineReasonParams fail closed on a missing per-code schema: ReasonParams is built from ReasonCode.options, the table is typed Record<ReasonCode, …>, and a test asserts the map covers the vocabulary. A fail-closed branch would only duplicate the enum's own issue (Hard Rule 6 is already met).
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Did not restrict Receipt.signals keys. It is outside this task and would touch features/receipt code owned by others, so I raised it as a contract issue instead (Hard Rule 2).
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Placed the new cross-field timestamp tests in schema-grading.spec.ts, which already holds the refinement properties, to keep schema.spec.ts under 400 lines.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Ran all mutation testing in a private scratch copy, not the shared tree, so concurrent agents never see a mutated file, and judged each mutation against a clean baseline. The first pass in the shared `mut/` copy was discarded because of baseline noise (missing docs, another agent's mutations).
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.2-impl] Accepted the implementer's choices as consistent with the v1.2 contract: per-code strict params via superRefine (static params type left as a record so src/ledger/export.ts compiles), `source` optional on the coverage.* and grace.waiting codes, readsDifferently narrowed to \p{No} characters NFKC changes, subjects stripping invisible characters before NFKC, and resolveRelative writing each boundary in the offset in which it reads 00:00/hh:00/hh:mm. That last choice matches window.ts truncating in claimedAt's own offset.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] No existing expectation changed: v1.2 settled all eight v1.1 readings (observationIds, claim-less outputs, no-source types, allowlist.unchecked position, currency_mismatch replacing exceeded, override/superseded last, taken observations, UNASKED reasons) exactly as the files already read them. Only notes and the README changed. Protects HR4 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Reason params are asserted through an optional `reasonParams` map keyed by code, with ids written as refs (o1/c1) that the loader resolves and parses with core Reason. When present it must name every code in reasonCodes, so the runner compares {code, params} exactly. Simplest form that keeps ids from being typed by hand. Protects HR2 and HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Params are not asserted for scope.observed_not_declared, because §15 does not say what scopeHash hashes. Every other code that carries params is asserted at least once (a spec check enforces this). Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Readings taken as the natural reading of the closed table: override.user.grade is the override's grade; ceiling.exceeded.amountCents is the §6.1 Amount (the larger in-currency amount); ceiling.currency_mismatch.currency is the claim's currency, else the assigned candidate's; unasked.*.agent is the attributed agent. Listed as 'taken' in the README. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] checks.ts order groups now state v1.2 explicitly: the rule's own code (including unasked.* signals and coverage.never_completed), then allowlist.unchecked, then coverage reasons, then match/cp, then override.user/superseded. Earlier this matched only by accident of table order. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] over_ceiling may carry [ceiling.exceeded, ceiling.currency_mismatch] together, per §6.1 v1.2 ('unless the amount also exceeds the ceiling'). No file exercises that combination, because what counts as 'exceeding in spendCurrency' is an open reading. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] The pending-rule code check uses the pre-override state (previousState), so an overridden pending receipt keeps grace.waiting / coverage.never_completed consistent. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Scenarios stay off open readings, and two of these are enforced by checks: allowlist.unchecked never appears together with coverage.never_completed, and no file has an allowlist-type claim with no counterparty under a non-empty allowlist. Protects HR4 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] The ids snapshot is written by an explicit script (`pnpm exec tsx tests/golden/write-ids.ts`), as §15 asks ('written by a script and reviewed'). The spec compares the file byte for byte and never rewrites it, replacing toMatchFileSnapshot. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] The loader passes claimedAtEnd through to the core Claim as written and keeps it out of rawHash (§10 lists rawHash's inputs, and claimedAtEnd is not among them). Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Invisible characters in scenario files are written as JSON \u escapes, and a scan confirmed no raw Cf characters remain in tests/golden. This follows the Trojan-Source rule from Decisions [1.1]. Protects HR9 and HR10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Each v1.2 rule gets a `contract:<tag>` and a predicate in v12-rules.ts proving the tagged file exercises it (for example, the DST proof needs evidence later than start + 24 h + afterClaimMin). Mutation tests show each predicate is false on a plain exact match. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Split scenarios.spec.ts: the mutation tests moved to checks.spec.ts so every file stays under 400 lines and every describe/function under 60 lines (tech_reviewer rule).
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] I accepted all of 68–74 and every asserted param unchanged after recomputing them from v1.2 alone. My values for 68 (1/1, 647/792), 69 (9/14 OVER_SCOPE, FINAL and PENDING), 70 (1/1, 4/9, 1/1), 71 (1/1, 1/1, 8/13), 72 (1/1, 5/6, 1/1), 73 (4/9 pending, no orphan receipt) and 74 (both reason orders, override params) match the files. Protects HR4 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Added 75–77 for v1.2 rules the spec list did not name but the contract changed. Each is built so the v1.1-era mistake gives a different grade or score, and each pins only text that v1.2 settles (75 c4 and 76 c2 follow §11/§5.2 and §6.1 literally). Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] 76 pins the counterparty-less purchase with no receipt in its window: coverage none instead of a false NOT_FOUND. It does not pin the odd case of an assigned exact receipt, which I raised as a contract question instead. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] The ceiling Amount check skips claims whose own currency differs from spendCurrency, so it does not encode open reading 5. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Attribution scores are checked as integer bp sums against the loaded policy's bp.unasked.signals, never the decimal fields, compared with BigInt cross-multiplication (§5.3). Protects HR4 and HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] New checks live in a new checks-claims.ts rather than in checks.ts or checks-reasons.ts, so every file stays under 400 lines and every function under 60 (tech_reviewer rule).
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Proofs for 76 and 77 run with each scenario's patched policy (receiptMerchants), via a proves() helper in checks.spec.ts, so the mutation tests check the real proof inputs. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract] v1.3: ~40 clarifications from wave B folded into docs/grading.md. One is a behaviour change: a purchase whose merchant has no receipt parser (or a claim with no merchant) now caps coverage at partial (reason coverage.no_parser) instead of 'none', so positive evidence still verifies while Attest can still never say NOT_FOUND for a merchant it cannot read. Previously a counterparty-less purchase claim matched to an exact receipt was graded UNVERIFIABLE while consuming that receipt — HR5. Others: claimedAtEnd only for day precision within 25 h; first-seen compares the earliest observedAt per contentHash; dedupe and ordering ties; counterparty-token vocabulary; assign(pairs, policy, fixed, taken); cp.confusable replaces cp.plus_tag and cp.* only for address claims; amountExact applicability; merchant text read via counterparty(); identity lists required for typed sources; allowlist and ceiling edge cases; best-candidate tie-break; claim-less default; UNASKED observationIds; signals keys closed; rowKeyHash via canonicalJson (lone surrogates); subjectHint counted in code points; contentHash over content fields only; bidi embeddings refused; subjects fold compatibility digits on purpose; allowlistedFields rules.
- 2026-10-02 [1.3] The committed core bench number (289.6 ms median for 5,000 claims over 50,000 observations) was re-measured by the integrator on an idle machine (load average about 4) with `pnpm bench`; the verifier's run under ~100 concurrent agent processes measured 1,163.5 ms wall and was not committed — HR12.
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] coverageLevel(claim, grant, coverage, policy) and graceElapsed(claim, grant, coverage, policy) take the grant, because §6.1 account binding reads grant.accounts. The task text's 3-argument form cannot apply it. (HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Coverage is decided per relevant source, then combined: full only if every source is full, none only if every source is none, otherwise partial. For one source (every default type) this matches §6.1. With several sources it stops one source's full coverage from allowing NOT_FOUND while another source was never seen. (HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] v1.3 no_parser cap: gmail.receipt entries are capped (coverage.no_parser) when name(claim.counterparty) is not in policy.receiptMerchants, or the claim has no counterparty. This is the same as §6.1's 'purchase-like type' for the default policy, and applies to any type that reads receipts. (HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Capped entries are still eligible, so they count for graceElapsed and VERIFIED finality: they prove the observer synced (golden 74 c2). Account hashes are counted over every entry of the source with lastSyncAt ≤ evaluatedAt, not only entries overlapping the window. Intervals are closed; a 1 ms gap makes coverage partial. (HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] counterpartyNotAllowed is not applicable (allowlist.unchecked) whenever no address is known at all. This covers a name or no counterparty with no assigned candidate (§6.1), and also an assigned candidate whose counterparty email() cannot read. A confusable address never matches; entries compare after asciiLower; a domain entry matches the domain or a '.entry' suffix. (HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] amountAboveCeiling: when the guard fails (spend type, ceiling, a known claim or candidate amount) the condition is not applicable; inside the guard it is true or false. The candidate's amount counts only in spendCurrency; a candidate with no currency neither counts nor mismatches. cumulativeAboveCeiling sums in BigInt and needs claim.currency === spendCurrency. (HR4, HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] ceiling.currency_mismatch names the currency that actually differs: the claim's if it differs, otherwise the assigned candidate's (golden 81 c2 expects CAD). The literal 'claim's when present' would name spendCurrency itself when only the candidate mismatches (golden 81 c1, which does not assert params). Raised as a contract issue. (HR2, HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Reason codes are derived from the conditions the matched rule names, not from rule ids. This gives exactly §15 for the default policy and still works if a policy reorders or renames rules. unasked.* appear only on UNASKED. grace.waiting appears only while a PENDING outcome is pending, and becomes coverage.never_completed once it finalises. Cap codes appear only when the rule names coverage:'partial'. coverage.account_unbound appears on NOT_FOUND, once per relevant source with no grant.accounts. coverage.none/partial/grace.waiting carry one reason per source, or {} for a type with no sources. coverage.no_parser has params {}: §15 lists none, and the v1.3 goldens expect {}. (HR2, HR4, HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] OVER_SCOPE counts as candidate-dependent, and waits like PARTIAL, when the matched rule names bestScoreGte/Lt, or counterpartyNotAllowed held only because of the candidate's address, or amountAboveCeiling held without a claim-only cause (the claim's own amount over the ceiling, or its own currency). Otherwise it is FINAL at once. (HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Finality: UNVERIFIABLE is FINAL at once when the matched rule names targetMissing or underspecified (facts of the claim alone) or the type has no sources. no_coverage, partial_coverage, default_unverifiable and pending finalise at end(p,t) + finalizeUnverifiableHours. A claim-less outcome other than UNASKED is FINAL at once. adapterLastSyncAt is used as given (it is hashed in context). finalAt is computedAt. (HR4, HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Claim-less grades need exactly one subject: one visible orphan and no scope, or no candidate and a scope. Anything else throws GradeError('claimless_subject'). A grant that is not the claim's or the attributed agent's throws 'agent_mismatch'. A policy rule concluding UNASKED for a claim throws 'unasked_with_claim'. reasonCode() throws 'unknown_reason_code' rather than dropping a reason the schema does not know. GradeError lives in a leaf module, grade-error.ts, shared by conditions, receipt and grade. (HR6)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] isFinalizable takes one FinalityInput object (outcome, rule, claim, orphan, grant, coverage, context, policy, candidateDependent, optional precomputed eligible). The §8 five-argument list has no grant (account binding) and no candidate dependence. (HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] What gets graded and hashed: the inputs after the §3 filters (candidates observed after evaluatedAt and coverage synced after it are dropped). Candidates are then de-duplicated by id: the row whose canonical JSON sorts first is kept, and that same row is both graded and hashed. Every set-like array is sorted and de-duplicated before hashing: observers (compared field by field, canonical JSON only on a full tie), allowedTypes, counterpartyAllowlist, declaredScopes, observedScopes, each accounts[source] list, attribution.signals, and candidates by id. (HR4)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] The best candidate is the highest exact score, with ties going to the lower observation id (the same tie-break assign uses). observationIds is the assigned candidate (score ≥ partial) or []; an UNASKED receipt's is [its orphan]; other claim-less receipts get []. signals are the best candidate's feature signals, or {} when nothing was scored. Confidence follows §6.4 via toDisplay; UNASKED uses scoreBp/10000. All of these are display values only. (HR4, HR5)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Speed-ups that change no results: a module-level WeakMap caches parsed coverage-entry times, re-used only while the entry's text is unchanged (the pattern candidates-time.ts uses); grade-best imports observedMs/occurredMs from candidates-time.ts, as batch-orphans.ts does; finality reuses the condition evaluator's eligible coverage. (HR4)
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Tests use the golden builders (tests/golden/build.ts) for schema-valid inputs, because a spec may not import another spec. Long describe blocks were split to stay at or under 60 lines. Each property has a 300 s timeout so a busy machine cannot cause a false failure; run counts were not reduced. Mutation testing ran only in an isolated scratch copy.
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Claim-less outcomes are restricted by OUTCOME (UNASKED or OVER_SCOPE), not by rule id. A policy that renames or reorders rules keeps working, and this matches batch.ts claimlessOutcome, which stores claim-less receipts only for these two outcomes. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] -0 is normalised once, at the single reason constructor in grade-reasons.ts, rather than at each condition. confidence and signals cannot be -0 (toDisplay returns a non-negative quotient), so reason params are the only path. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Accepted the implementer's readings, verified against §6.1/§8/§15 and every golden: coverage per source then combined; capped entries count for grace and VERIFIED finality; account hashes counted over every visible entry of the source; counterpartyNotAllowed not applicable with no readable address; amountAboveCeiling true or false only inside its guard; cumulativeAboveCeiling needs claim.currency === spendCurrency (the §6.1 parenthetical, read literally); OVER_SCOPE counts as candidate-dependent when the rule names a score condition, or the allowlist or ceiling held only because of the candidate; reasons derived from the conditions the matched rule names; best candidate = highest exact score, ties to the lower id. Protects HR4 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Accepted fail-closed behaviour for malformed allowlist entries: an entry like 'company.com.' matches nothing, so the claim is OVER_SCOPE rather than silently allowed. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Accepted GradeError('claimless_subject') for a claim-less grade whose only orphan was observed after evaluatedAt. gradeBatch never passes one, and refusing beats inventing a subject. Protects HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Did not try to speed up inputsHash inside receipt-hash.ts, for example by hand-building the canonical JSON string around a cached coverage substring. The profile caps the gain at about 7% of batch CPU against a 19x gap, and a hand-built string risks drifting from hash.ts canonical JSON, which would break replay (HR4). The gate needs the outside changes listed in residualIssues.
- 2026-10-02 [1.5] Mutation testing ran only in an isolated scratch copy, so the shared tree was never mutated while five other agents were running tests. New tests went into a new owned file, tests/unit/core/grade-adversarial.spec.ts, which matches the grade*.spec.ts ownership pattern.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] The event shape uses the discriminant `event` ({event:'regrade', next} | {event:'override', grade, reason, at} | {event:'revert'} | {event:'supersede', claimId}). This matches the golden loader's BuiltEvent, so the runner can pass loaded.events straight in; the extra receiptId field is ignored. Payload strings (reason, at, claimId) are checked against the core schemas (ReceiptOverride, Ulid) at run time. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] There is one typed error, IllegalTransitionError, with a code and `from` (the state of the receipt it was applied to). The codes are regrade_final, regrade_overridden, already_overridden, not_overridden and not_unasked for the illegal pairs, plus subject_mismatch, invalid_receipt, invalid_event and invalid_result. It lives in the leaf module state-error.ts, like candidates-error.ts, and state.ts re-exports it with RECEIPT_EVENTS. The message holds only the code and the state, never the override reason or other user text. Protects HR6 and HR10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] transition parses the stored receipt with the Receipt schema first (invalid_receipt) and parses every result (invalid_result, for example when an override would push reasons past SCHEMA_LIMITS.REASONS). So nothing schema-invalid can come out. Protects HR4 and HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] Lifecycle reasons are derived, never just appended. A receipt's reasons must be its graded reasons, then override.user {grade} if overridden, then superseded {claimId} if superseded, in §15 table order whatever order the events came in. A stored receipt that breaks this is refused with invalid_receipt. That makes revert restore the exact prior receipt. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] regrade requires next to be valid, PENDING or FINAL (never USER_OVERRIDDEN), with consistent lifecycle reasons (invalid_event otherwise), and to have the same id as the stored receipt (subject_mismatch otherwise). It returns next exactly and carries nothing over, including supersededBy: an UNASKED receipt that gets regraded is an orphan again, so its supersession is stale. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] supersede with the same claimId changes nothing; with a different claimId it replaces supersededBy and its single superseded reason. That is the literal 'sets supersededBy'; see contractIssues. Protects HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] override is allowed on UNASKED receipts (PENDING or FINAL), because §4.3 already talks about 'an overridden UNASKED receipt'. override does not compare `at` with computedAt, and regrade does not compare next.computedAt with the stored receipt's computedAt; the contract is silent on both and they are raised as contract issues. Simplest option.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] An unknown event kind at run time throws invalid_event. A non-object event (null) is not handled, because the type forbids it and the bus parses at its boundary. The fuzz property therefore uses fc.object() for arbitrary events.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] Each spec file has its own copy of the receipt fixtures. Only state*.spec.ts files are mine, and importing one spec from another would register its tests twice (precedent Decisions [1.0]). The grace integration test builds its claim, grant and coverage with the golden builders (tests/golden/load.ts, time.ts), so the inputs follow §15's documented defaults.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] Mutation testing ran only on a scratchpad copy (scratchpad/mut-state), never on the shared tree, following the [1.3] and [1.contract-v1.2-impl] precedent.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] I added a new spec file, state-grace-full.spec.ts (inside my state*.spec.ts ownership), instead of growing state-grace.spec.ts, so each grace file stays focused and under 400 lines. It builds its inputs with the golden builders and loadPolicy(mergePatch(...)), the same way as conditions.spec.ts, and checks coverageLevel to show that each case really separates grace from coverage.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] The minute-walk integration tests got an explicit 30 s timeout, the same as the properties, because this machine reached load average 100 while other agents ran. numRuns stays at 1,000.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] I accepted the implementer's design choices after checking them against §8 and §15 and the mutation results. They are: the `event` discriminant matching the golden BuiltEvent; one IllegalTransitionError with codes and `from`, and no user text in the message (HR10); stored receipts and results parsed with the Receipt schema; lifecycle reasons derived from the override and supersededBy fields, and checked on input; regrade returning next exactly and refusing a USER_OVERRIDDEN next or one with another id; supersede replacing an earlier claim and doing nothing for the same claim; override allowed on UNASKED receipts.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] I did not add a guard against regrading an UNASKED receipt to a non-UNASKED one, or against regrading to an older computedAt. §8 and the task text are silent, gradeBatch never produces either case, and the simplest option is to raise them as contract issues, not invent rules (HR4).
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] I did not handle a non-object event (null). The ReceiptEvent type forbids it and the bus parses at its boundary; this follows the implementer's decision.
- 2026-10-02 [1.6] All mutation testing ran in a scratchpad mirror (outside the repository) with node_modules symlinked, never in the shared tree, so other agents' runs could never see a mutant.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] attribute(orphan, activity, policy, evaluatedAt?) takes an optional 4th argument. evaluatedAt filters only adapterSyncs (a sync after evaluatedAt is ignored), as the task says. The latest sync is chosen by instant; equal instants written differently resolve to the smallest string, so input order never matters. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Added attributor(activity, policy, evaluatedAt?), which indexes the activity once (per-agent sorted intervals with a running max end, so each check is one binary search). attribute() delegates to it, so a batch does not rescan per orphan. batch.ts already calls attributor. Protects HR4 and ledger_engineer rule 9.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] userIdle needs ONE recorded idle interval covering [occurredAt − idleMin, occurredAt], inclusive. Touching intervals are not merged and missing data is never idle. Protects HR7 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Every signal window is inclusive (distance ≤ window) and exact to the millisecond. A bound outside the safe-integer range throws AttributionError('unsafe_window'), never a rounded comparison. Protects HR4 and HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Adjacency suppression measures a claim's time as its precision unit [start(p,t), end(p,t)] (window.ts precisionBounds), not the bare claimedAt instant. A day-precision claim of the same type therefore suppresses activityLogAdjacent for that agent all day. This is the conservative reading. Protects HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Activity entries' optional `type` is accepted and not read: §7 fires activityLogAdjacent on any entry of the agent. Protects HR7 by following the contract literally.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Signature matching uses ASCII-only case folding (normalize.asciiLower, the §11 rule), with no NFKC and no stripping of invisible characters. Fullwidth or Kelvin look-alikes cannot fire a signature. Only subjectHint is read. Protects HR7 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Only agents with at least one agent-specific signal are candidates. userIdle is added to each candidate. The likely agent has the highest score, then the most signals (userIdle included), then the lowest agent id by UTF-16 order. The attribution is returned whatever its score; grade() applies the threshold. Signals come out in UnaskedSignal order, which is also the §15 reason order. Protects HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] priorSpendCents matches currencies by equality: a claim without a currency counts only earlier claims without one. A claim id listed more than once counts once, at its largest qualifying amount, so batch claims plus ledger claims cannot double-count. The sum is in BigInt and saturates at Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER (already above any allowed ceiling). A negative or non-integer amount throws ScopeError('invalid_amount'). Days are fixed 24 h (MS_PER_DAY), as golden 65 requires. Protects HR4 and HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] priorSpendCents returns 0 when the claim's own type is not in spendTypes (cumulativeAboveCeiling never applies to it). An email receipt's hashed context then does not change whenever the agent buys something, and the batch work is cut by about 75%. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] priorSpendCents memoises each claim's claimedAt instant (WeakMap, used only while the text is unchanged, the same pattern as candidates-time.ts). Cost on the bench workload fell from about 1.2 s to about 0.1 s. A memo never changes a result. Protects HR4 and ledger_engineer rule 9.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] scopeSubjects(grant) returns string[]: observed minus declared scopes, compared exactly as stored, de-duplicated and sorted by UTF-16 code unit. gradeBatch builds the {k:'scope', agent, scope} subject. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] reasonText accepts any reason-shaped value (code: string, params: record). It checks the code with ReasonCode and the whole params object with schema.ts ReasonParams (strict), throwing ReasonTextError('unknown_code' | 'invalid_params'); nothing is rendered as a guess. Text uses fixed labels: AGENT_LABEL (exported), source, account service, action type and grade. Amounts are plain digits '(minor units)' with no currency guessed and no locale. Ids and the scope hash appear verbatim. Protects HR2, HR4, HR6 and HR10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Every unasked.* sentence uses the template 'This was likely {Agent}: <evidence>.'. The banned-phrase test also rejects 'this was' not followed by 'likely'. Protects HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] The attribution unit tests are split into attribution.spec.ts (Done-when, per-agent, idle, windows, text) and attribution-rules.spec.ts (adjacency, signatures, tie-breaks, sync, result, errors) to stay under 400 lines. Helpers are duplicated because spec files do not import each other (Decisions [1.0] precedent). Long describe blocks were split to stay at or under 60 lines. Hard rule: none.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] The grade()-dependent Done-when tests are in attribution-grade.spec.ts and scope-grade.spec.ts. They import the real grade.ts, which landed during this task, and pass now. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] No source change in src/core: every attack on attribution, spend and reason text behaved per §7, §6.3 and §15, and every implementer decision I re-checked holds (precision-unit adjacency, ONE covering idle interval, ASCII signature folding, lowest-agent-id tie, evaluatedAt filter on syncs only, currency equality, fixed 24 h days, dedupe by id, 0 for non-spend claims).
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Kept the literal §7 reading that ANY same-type claim of the agent (an underspecified one included) suppresses activityLogAdjacent, and raised the §4.3 conflict as a contract issue. This is the conservative direction for Hard Rule 7 (fewer UNASKED), and changing it is a contract decision.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Kept the module-level WeakMap memo in scope.ts (same pattern as candidates-time.ts). It never changes a result, and a new test now proves an edited claim is re-read. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Treated the batch-perf failure as task 1.5's residual, not this task's. The profile shows the owned modules use under 1% of gradeBatch CPU. The verdict is PASS because every 1.7/1.8 Done-when item is met with evidence and every owned-module check passes; the full-suite fail is reported as is.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Done-when 1.7: each signal alone stays below the threshold (attribution.spec.ts, and attribution-grade.spec.ts through grade()). Combinations reach it (9 unit combinations, 7 through grade()). The receipt text never states that the agent acted: reason-text.spec.ts renders every ReasonCode with every param combination, requires 'likely' in each unasked text, and checks banned phrases; the same check runs on real UNASKED receipts.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Done-when 1.8: scope-grade.spec.ts covers type-not-allowed (over_scope), scope-not-declared (over_scope_observed with scopeHash), a single $200 purchase against a $50 ceiling (over_ceiling, FINAL) and cumulative two $30 against $50 (the second is over_ceiling_cumulative with params 3000/3000/5000). It now also has gradeBatch tests for same-instant purchases and currency mixing.
- 2026-10-02 [1.7] Added tests only where a mutant survived or an attack had no direct test, and kept each file under 400 lines and each function under 60 lines.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] gradeBatch(claims, index, grants, policy, coverage, activity, fixed = []) takes fixed as BatchFixed[] = {observationId, claimId: Ulid | null}. claimId null means the observation is taken by an overridden UNASKED receipt. This is exactly the golden loader's BuiltFixed shape; gradeBatch splits it into assign()'s FixedPair[] and a taken set — HR4/HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Inputs from after coverage.evaluatedAt are ignored (§3) at the index boundary: visibleAt() wraps the index and drops rows with observedAt > evaluatedAt, so they are neither scored, assigned nor orphans. Coverage passes synced after evaluatedAt define no orphan window, and grants with updatedAt > evaluatedAt are not in force. Claims are graded exactly as given; the caller passes the PENDING claims and replay filters them — HR4, HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] The grant in force for an agent is the one with the latest updatedAt not after evaluatedAt. No grant in force (for a claim's agent or an orphan's attributed agent) throws BatchError('missing_grant'); two grants of one agent at the same instant throw BatchError('ambiguous_grant'). Core never invents a default grant; Sprint 3.6 supplies permissive defaults — HR5, HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] An orphan is an observation of a type with sources whose occurredAt lies in the union of windows of coverage entries of its own source, synced by evaluatedAt. It is counted once per contentHash using candidates()' representative rule (lowest id, then earliest observedAt), and must be assigned to no claim (fixed pairs included) and not taken. Windows are merged per source and read once per type — HR5, HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Orphans with no attribution are skipped: a claim-less grade with neither attribution nor scope can match no rule. For attributed orphans and scope subjects, gradeBatch asks grade's own evaluators (evaluateConditions + firstMatch from conditions.ts) which rule matches, and calls grade() (which hashes) only when the outcome is UNASKED or OVER_SCOPE. Every policy rule names a condition, so this is exact; it removed about 46 s of 88 s on the bench workload — HR4, HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Claim-less receipts are kept by the grade their rule concludes (UNASKED for orphans, OVER_SCOPE for scope subjects), not by rule id, so a renamed rule in a patched policy is not silently dropped. With the shipped policy this equals rules `unasked` and `over_scope_observed` — HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Non-assignable claims (underspecified, calendar without targetAt, a type with no sources) get no candidate read and are graded with an empty candidate list (§4.3). Fixed and taken observations are not scored (assign never moves them; grade never sees them). Candidate reads run sequentially (simplest, deterministic).
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] priorSpendCents reads activity.claims grouped by agent. priorSpendCents already filters by agent, so the grouping only narrows the scan — §6.3.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Output is ordered by receipt id. Two receipts with one id throw BatchError('duplicate_receipt'), which only a corrupt ledger can reach now that the schema pins id = idFromHash(contentHash). A claim given twice throws 'duplicate_claim'; a claim both graded and fixed throws 'claim_fixed'; non-one-to-one fixed pairs throw CandidatesError('fixed_conflict') from assign — HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Golden runner: follows the README runner contract. It passes bucketMinutes 15 to createMemoryIndex (the README call omits this required argument). It passes evaluatedAt to attribute() in both modes, matching gradeBatch, which ignores adapter syncs after evaluatedAt. Score comes from features() of the match.best candidate; attributionScore is recomputed from attribute(). Generic checks also parse every receipt with the core Receipt schema. Runner self-tests prove each comparison reports a broken expectation or receipt.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Unit tests build their inputs with the reviewed golden loader (buildScenario from tests/golden/load.ts) instead of new builders, so every object is schema-valid with hash-derived ids. I may create only batch*.spec.ts files, so each spec carries its own ~20-line load/run helper (same precedent as Decisions [1.0] and [1.4]).
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] The determinism property is a fast-check test with 1,000 runs in tests/unit/core/batch-determinism.spec.ts (my ownership pattern) rather than tests/property. Each run picks a golden batch scenario, shuffles every input list with seeded mulberry32 from bench/core.ts, and uses one of three index behaviours (memory, reversed reads, everything over-returned).
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] The perf gate uses the CPU-time clock (as in Decisions [1.3]) with a 600 s timeout. Correctness is asserted before the timing so a fast wrong answer cannot pass. The gate test is not tagged @mandatory (Decisions [1.3]).
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Bench grading workload: the same 50,000 observations and 5,000 claim timings as task 1.3. Identity comes from a second seeded stream (seed + 1): 40% restate a real observation, 10% are vague, the rest are random. Coverage is one pass per source per day (93 entries; the last day's pass is after evaluatedAt). Four grants are bound to one account (muse with a 500,000-cent USD ceiling, dots with one undeclared scope). Activity is 960 session pings, 60 idle spans and 480 log entries, with the shipped DEFAULT_POLICY. The result is the median of 5 cold runs, wall and CPU, plus receipt counts by grade, merged into core-<date>.json.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Bench ids: the Observation schema (changed concurrently by another agent) now requires id = idFromHash(contentHash). Counter hashes now put the counter in the leading 128 bits, so ids stay distinct and ascend as before; claims use id = idFromHash(rawHash). The 1.3 workload is otherwise unchanged (candidatesReturned is still 383,354). measure() was refactored onto a shared timed() helper to keep the file under 400 lines; 1.3 metric names are unchanged.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] The gate number is the one measured under concurrent-agent load (load average about 10), as Decisions [1.3] did; HR12 is satisfied because it is a real measurement from `pnpm bench`. The gate was not weakened and the workload was not re-tuned to pass it ('fix the index, not the gate').
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Did not change gradeBatch: every attack (vague claims, FINAL UNASKED staying assignable, overridden fixed pairs, taken observations, several agents and grant versions, inputs after evaluatedAt) behaved per §3/§4.3/§7. The work went into tests that pin that behaviour. HR4, HR5, HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Kept the silent handling of an observation fixed both to a claim and as taken, rather than throwing. state.ts allows overriding a superseded FINAL UNASKED receipt, so this is a legitimate ledger state; throwing would fail every later batch. Pinned by a test and raised as a contract clarification. HR6 is not at stake (nothing is graded approximately).
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Kept BatchError('missing_grant') for an orphan attributed to an agent with no grant in force, and 'ambiguous_grant' for two grant versions at one instant: core never invents a grant (HR5). Sprint 3.6 supplies defaults.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Runner: added claimId and policyHash as generic checks. Both are fixed by §3 (receipt subject; policyHash on every receipt), so they cannot disagree with any honest scenario, and all 84 pass. Did not compare inputsHash or confidence, because the README runner contract does not name them. HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] New tests went into tests/unit/core/batch-attacks.spec.ts (my batch*.spec.ts pattern) with their own small load/run helper built on the reviewed golden loader, following the precedent of Decisions [1.0]/[1.4]. The determinism property stays in tests/unit/core, consistent with eight other unit/core specs that use fast-check.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Did not weaken, skip or re-tune the perf gate or its workload ('fix the index, not the gate'). The verdict is FAIL because Done-when item 3 is not met.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] Ran `pnpm bench` twice and committed the second run. Run 1, at load 33–65, inflated the 1.3 lookup wall median to 1,964 ms, near its gate. Run 2 at load about 16 is real and representative (lookup 253.7 ms). Both runs reproduce the implementer's receipt counts exactly, which confirms the earlier committed figure was a real measurement. HR12.
- 2026-10-02 [1.batch] All mutation work ran in a private scratch mirror (scratchpad/verify/mirror); probes and profiles stayed in scratchpad/verify. No file outside my ownership was edited.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] coverage.no_parser takes an OPTIONAL `source` (the same 'source?' kind as the other coverage-capping codes). This satisfies the task's '(params {source})' and still accepts the contract's literal closed table, which does not list the code (raised as a contract issue). HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] override.user's grade param and ReceiptOverride.grade share one new exported `OverrideGrade = z.enum(['VERIFIED','NOT_FOUND'])` (§8). HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] Receipt.signals keeps its static type Record<string, number>. Keys are validated by a superRefine against RECEIPT_SIGNALS (MATCH_FEATURES + 'score', defined in schema-checks.ts), so src/ledger/export.ts compiles unchanged. The old count cap was removed: the closed set has 9 keys, within L.REASONS. HR2, HR10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] New exports MatchFeature, ReceiptSignal and OverrideGrade (schema.ts), ALLOWLISTABLE_FIELDS (policy.ts) and hashRowKey (redact.ts). A test pins MatchFeature to the policy's FeatureName, and ALLOWLISTABLE_FIELDS to redact's CONTENT_FIELDS plus rowKeyHash and contentHash, so the duplicated lists cannot drift. HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] The Observation id cross-check runs only when contentHash is valid lowercase hex, so a bad hash is reported once, at its own path, and the check never throws on malformed rows. Cost is about 60 ms per 50,000 parses. HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] claimedAtEnd checks run in order: precision first ('allowed only with day precision'), then strictly after claimedAt, then the 25 h upper bound (inclusive), measured from the local midnight of claimedAt's own written offset. The schema does not check that claimedAt is unit-aligned: the contract puts that on adapters, and window.ts truncates anyway. HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] Policy: kept the export name ObservationFieldName and narrowed it to the 8 allowed fields (only owned tests consumed it). The occurredAt requirement replaces `.min(1)` as a list-level refine. Like the existing duplicate check, it runs only once every item is valid, so a load reports the bad item first. The empty-identity issue is emitted after the featuresByType weight issue, keeping document order. HR2, HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] assign's `taken` parameter is now required (no default), matching §13's `assign(pairs, policy, fixed, taken)`, so a caller cannot forget overridden UNASKED observations. A fixed pair whose observation is also in `taken` is still kept as given (Decisions [1.3]). A test pins this, and it is raised as a contract issue. HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] The candidates and features code already implemented the v1.3 rules from wave B, so they got doc updates only. The features-attack test now pins two things separately: the loader rejects the policy behind the old hole, and features() throws RatioError('zero_weight_sum'). HR5, HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] redact: rowKeyHash goes through a new hashRowKey(rowKey) = Sha256(sha256Hex(canonicalJson(rowKey))) helper, so adapters can compute a claim-side rowKeyHash the same way. hashText is unchanged because §13 still lists it. HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] Tests: rows whose id does not match their contentHash (twins, one id with two contents) are built as typed objects without a schema parse, as a bad index could return them. Twin ids start at 100 so they never equal an honest idFromHash(k…k) id. This fixed a rare property failure.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] Tests: the ledger fixture sha(n) now writes n in both 128-bit halves, and makeObservation derives its id from its contentHash. idFromHash reads only the first 128 bits, so padded-at-the-end hashes all mapped to the same id.
