- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] Ledger tests: the old 'existing id with a different contentHash → id_conflict' case now expects invalid_input, because the v1.3 schema rejects such input before the repo sees it. Two other tests were rewritten as same-id re-sightings: the duplicate and concurrent upserts, and the unreadable-row replacement. The unique contentHash index is now checked by a raw Dexie add in its own describe.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] Moved the ReceiptOverride table from schema.spec.ts into the new schema-cross-checks.spec.ts, and hoisted the policy test tables to module level. This keeps every test file under 400 lines and every changed function under 60.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] Mutation testing ran in a private copy of the extension in my scratchpad (node_modules symlinked), never in the shared tree, so no temporary breakage could reach the five concurrent agents.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] Kept the implementer's v1.3 implementation unchanged after re-deriving each rule from the 02a89c2 diff and killing a mutation of each. No v1.3 logic defect was found.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] Added the features() memo, although it is outside the v1.3 rule list. features.ts is in this task's ownership, the task 1.5 agent cannot edit it, and it was about half of gradeBatch CPU. It follows the WeakMap memo precedent in candidates-time.ts, conditions-coverage.ts and scope.ts. Each entry is used only while every input it read is still Object.is-equal, so an object edited in place is never scored from old fields (Hard Rule 4). Correctness is pinned by byte-identical receipts, unit and property tests, and 10 killed memo mutations.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] The memo keys claim facts on policy.windows and policy.normalize.stopWords (the exact parts read), not on the policy object. A hand-built policy that changes only featuresByType therefore reuses the facts correctly, and one that changes windows (even with shared stop words) recomputes. A test pins this.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] Property-test timeouts are a numeric 60_000 third argument rather than an identifier. Prettier keeps its compact test-call layout only for a numeric literal; an identifier pushed two files over 400 lines and two functions over 60. The literal is allowed in tests (the no-literal rule covers src/core), and 60 s is only an upper bound.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract-v1.3-impl] Split the pre-existing long describe callbacks in owned ledger and bus tests rather than leave them. Every hook was file-level, so the split is mechanical, and vitest list shows the same 83 tests before and after.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] coverage.no_parser has no params ({}), taking §15's closed table literally ('every other code has no params'). It is asserted in 06 and 76. The core schema accepts an optional source, and the core currently emits {}, so the runner passes. Protects HR4 and HR10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] A scopeHash param is written in a scenario as the observed scope; the loader stores sha256Hex(scope). This keeps hashes from being typed by hand, as §15 requires. A check ties the param to the receipt's own scope.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] In the loader, row keys hash as sha256Hex(canonicalJson(label)) for both claims and observations (§10 v1.3). Account labels stay sha256Hex(label), as §15 says for accountHash.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] 76 gained a c3: an 'ACME' claim with no receipt that must be NOT_FOUND. Under v1.3 a raw-name comparison on c1 would still give VERIFIED, so without c3 the normalised-name rule would no longer be tested. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] 81 c1 (USD claim, EUR receipt) asserts reason codes only. Read literally, §15 would set the mismatch's currency param to the claim's USD, which is also spendCurrency. Raised as a contract issue.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] 34 and 65 c2 keep OVER_SCOPE with ceiling.currency_mismatch for a claim amount in another currency. This reads 'an amount is known' as any amountCents, in any currency. The other reading lets a claim dodge the ceiling by naming a different currency. Protects HR5; raised as a contract issue.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] 81 c2 asserts [ceiling.exceeded, ceiling.currency_mismatch]. v1.3 counts a claim amount with no currency in the Amount, so 8000 exceeding a 5000 ceiling keeps ceiling.exceeded. The claim is past grace, so both readings of finality give FINAL.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Equal best scores break by observation id, lowest first. This matches §4.1's dedupe ('lowest id kept') and the ascending order in §4.3. 82 is built so that earliest-occurredAt or candidate order would pick the other observation.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] The no-parser check approximates name() as lowercase plus collapsed spaces. Files keep merchant names to plain ASCII words, so the check never relies on NFKC.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Removed the old guard against combining coverage.never_completed and allowlist.unchecked (v1.3 settles it as the pending rule's own code). The order check now enforces never_completed first, and 80 c2 pins it.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] allowlistUncheckedProblems now treats a claim with no counterparty like a name claim (v1.3 §6.1). The old open-reading flag is gone.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] To stay within the 400-line and 60-line limits, the v1.3 proofs are split across v13-rules.ts, v13-rules-evidence.ts and proof-helpers.ts. Their mutation tests are in a new v13-rules.spec.ts, and v12-rules.ts imports the shared helpers. The two purchase proofs moved to v1.3 with updated meanings, and the tag purchase_no_counterparty_none became purchase_no_counterparty_capped.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Kept new scenarios away from readings v1.3 still leaves open: an interval gap combined with the no-parser cap, an assigned candidate with no address under an allowlist, and a claim-less default_unverifiable. The README lists each one.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Added scenario 84, although the task's list did not name these rules. They are v1.3 behaviour changes with no golden coverage, and each sub-case is built so the v1.2 reading gives a different grade or score. This protects HR5: a string that displays differently from how it is stored is never read as a match.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] Kept scenario 27's mid-day claimedAt with day precision rather than moving it to the unit start. It is the only file proving that the core computes start(p,t) itself instead of trusting claimedAt. The note now says so; ids are unchanged.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] The contract-tag registry lists four descriptive v1.0/v1.1 tags (demo, demo_unverifiable, counterparty_domain_plus_tag, grade_mode_claimless) instead of writing proofs for them. The demo pair is already asserted in scenarios.spec.ts, and the other two only describe their files. Any new contract tag must now have a proof.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] I left the implementer's no_parser params {} and the codes-only 81 c1 as they are. Both follow the literal contract and are raised as contract issues.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden] I kept the hasNoParser approximation unchanged. The runner, not this check, is the oracle for a non-ASCII merchant, and noted under residual issues.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract] v1.4: ~90 clarifications from wave C. Substantive: inputsHash names candidates by {id, contentHash, observedAt} and hashes grant and coverage once (grantHash, coverageHash), still covering every input — the v1.3 definition hashed ~260 MB per 5,000-claim batch and made the grading perf gate unreachable (HR4 kept: every input is in the hash); gradeBatch returns {receipt, inputs} so receiptInputs can be stored for replay; the grant in force is the latest updatedAt <= evaluatedAt (else BatchError); orphans are defined; only assignable claims suppress activityLogAdjacent (a vague claim could otherwise hide an UNASKED — the evasion §4.3 closes); calendar attribution measures from first observedAt, not the event start; coverage levels are per source; regrade requires the same subject, monotonic computedAt, and UNASKED to stay UNASKED; supersede replaces and is applied only once the superseding claim is FINAL; override.at >= computedAt; lifecycle reason invariants; Claim.id = idFromHash(rawHash) enforced — HR4, HR5, HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract] v1.5: inputsHash names candidates through a Merkle-style candidatesHash (SHA-256 over the 32-byte refHash of each candidate, refHash = SHA-256 of canonicalJson({id, contentHash, observedAt}) computed once per observation). The v1.4 form re-encoded ~383,000 candidate references as text (~50 MB) per 5,000-claim batch, the largest remaining cost after a first optimisation pass (23.3 s → ~3 s warm CPU). Every input stays bound to the hash, so replay proof is unchanged — HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.perf] Grading optimisation, two passes, behaviour-preserving: gradeBatch over the 5,000-claim x 50,000-observation bench workload went from 23.3 s to a 1,006.5 ms median (1,257.9 ms CPU) in `pnpm bench`. Proven by a differential verifier that ran the pre-optimisation code (commit 314d8b6) from a git worktree: receipts byte-identical (inputsHash aside, whose definition changed in v1.4/v1.5) on the bench workload, all 84 golden scenarios, 8 workload/policy/grant variants, 12,000 random scenarios and 96 targeted boundary cases; idFromHash equal on 1.2M inputs. The verifier found and fixed one defect introduced by the optimisation (the spend prefix index summed a repeated claim id twice when its copies were not adjacent) and pinned the fast path to priorSpendCents with property tests — HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.perf] Perf gates run in their own serial pass (tests/perf, vitest.perf.config.ts: one fork, no file parallelism; `pnpm test` runs unit then perf) and gate on the median CPU time of 3 runs, each on a freshly built workload so no memo can carry a result between runs; every run's CPU and wall time is logged, the cold first run included. Measured inside the parallel unit run, the same code's CPU time was inflated ~25% by seven workers on four physical cores and failed or passed depending on load — a flaky gate is a failing gate (qa_tester rule 10). The 2,000 ms threshold and the dense workload are unchanged; CPU (stricter than wall) stays the metric. Measured 3x: gradeBatch median 1,385-1,582 ms CPU (cold first run ~1.9 s CPU, ~1.3 s wall), candidate lookup ~330 ms.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] gradeBatch's result type: BatchReceipt {receipt, inputs: BatchInputs {grant, context, policyHash}} is defined in batch-grade.ts and re-exported from batch.ts. inputs.grant is the grant-in-force object as given (by identity) and inputs.context is the exact GradeContext passed to grade. Coverage is omitted because it is the batch's own (§3). This protects HR4: replay can rebuild every receipt from its stored inputs.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Claims with observedAt > evaluatedAt are filtered once in gradeBatch (claimsSeenBy). The filter applies to both the graded claims and activity.claims, so they are neither graded, nor assigned evidence, nor counted in priorSpendCents, nor read by attribution. Protects HR4 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Duplicate-claim and claim_fixed checks still run over every claim given, including claims ignored for observedAt > evaluatedAt. A corrupt batch fails loud whatever it holds (HR6).
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Sessions, entries and idle intervals after evaluatedAt are cut by attribution.ts attributor(activity, policy, evaluatedAt), which gradeBatch already calls. That is the attribution agent's in-progress change: it drops later sessions, entries and claims and clips idle at evaluatedAt. I did not add a second batch pre-filter: it could diverge (dropping vs clipping idle) from direct attribute() calls used by replay and the golden runner. Protects HR4 and HR7. batch-visibility.spec.ts pins the behaviour end to end.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] coverage.no_parser now carries {source: the capped source}, like the other cap codes. I left the emission conditions unchanged. For a purchase-like type (sources exactly ['gmail.receipt'], §6.1), a partial_coverage receipt of a claim with no parser always has an overlapping capped receipt entry, so no_parser is always emitted. Protects HR2 (params are enum values only).
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Rules already in the code (orphans §3, per-source coverage levels, counterpartyNotAllowed, the amountAboveCeiling guard, the claim-less terminal rule, the claimless_subject throw, currency_mismatch param precedence, plainZero) were checked against v1.4 and left as they were; I only added tests.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] grade-golden.spec.ts now works out each expectation's grading instant from the last applied regrade event of its receipt, as scenarios 93 and 94 require. It also uses the grant in force at that instant, counts spend only from claims seen by then, and uses core attribute() for a non-UNASKED orphan expectation (scenario 91). It cross-checks the expectation's stated inputs: grant, priorSpendCents and scope.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] batch-determinism.spec.ts now builds its gradeBatch inputs explicitly. It used to spread the whole LoadedScenario, and the golden loader now holds a Map (grantRefs) that canonicalJson rejects.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] The perf gate checks stored inputs for sampled claims (grant identity, context, policyHash). It also regrades every 50th claim-less receipt with grade() from its stored inputs, using each observation's first sighting, the representative the batch grades.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Housekeeping: I first extracted the HEAD baseline over a pre-existing scratch directory (scratchpad/head) before switching to my own scratchpad/v14core-base, now removed. No project file was affected.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Orphans follow §3 literally: an observation of a type with sources is an orphan candidate when its occurredAt lies in a seen window of its own source, whatever sources the policy lists for its type. This matches §4.1, where candidates are matched by type from any source. It is implemented as one index read per type over the merged union of all seen windows, then an exact own-source check, so reads do not triple. Protects HR7 and HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] The §8 counterfactual for OVER_SCOPE finality is computed in grade.ts by grading again with no candidate at all (bestScore null, assigned null). If the first matching rule still concludes OVER_SCOPE, the receipt is FINAL at once; otherwise it waits like PARTIAL. This is the simplest exact reading of 'would not be OVER_SCOPE without that candidate's amount, currency or address'. It runs only for candidate-dependent OVER_SCOPE outcomes, and replay and gradeBatch share it. Protects HR4 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] A claim-less grade with a scope subject and any observation, visible or not, throws claimless_subject (fail loud, HR6). Replay and gradeBatch always pass [] for scope receipts, so no caller changes.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Kept Decisions [1.5]: the no_parser cap applies to gmail.receipt entries of every type that reads receipts, not only to purchase-like types. This keeps HR5, never NOT_FOUND for a merchant Attest cannot parse. A grade-params test pins it, and the gap is listed as a contract issue rather than changing the code to the less safe literal reading.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Mutation testing and perf comparisons ran in private copies in the scratchpad (src, tests and bench copied, node_modules symlinked), never in the shared tree. Another agent removed a shared-name scratch directory mid-run, so I moved to a unique v14verify/ directory and later deleted my copies.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Idle intervals and evaluatedAt (§3): an interval counts only up to evaluatedAt. One that starts after it is dropped; one that straddles it ends at it. Sessions and entries after evaluatedAt are dropped, and claims with observedAt > evaluatedAt do not suppress. These filters apply only when attribute/attributor get evaluatedAt (gradeBatch and the golden runner always pass it). Of the readings, this is the only one where no evidence recorded after evaluatedAt changes a result, so replay sees what grading saw — HR4, HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] The calendar anchor is the orphan's own observedAt. attribute() takes one observation, so passing its earliest sighting is the caller's job; gradeBatch's coveredObservations already keeps the earliest sighting per contentHash. The Attributor's occurredMs argument stays (batch.ts unchanged) and is ignored for calendar.* types — HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] For suppression, a calendar claim's time is still the precision unit of its claimedAt, not targetAt, as §7 literally says ([start(p,t), end(p,t)]) — HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] AttributionClaim is now FeatureClaim + agent + observedAt, and AttributionPolicy is FeaturePolicy & Pick<Policy,'unasked'>. attribution.ts imports features.isAssignable, which §13's layer order allows. A parsed Claim and Policy still satisfy both types — HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Exact pre-filter: a claim is checked with isAssignable only if its precision unit lies within 2×adjacentMin of one of its agent's entries. The entry and the claim must each be within adjacentMin of the anchor, so no other claim can ever suppress. This keeps the attributor's index build at about 15 ms on the 5,000-claim bench. A boundary test and a mutant pin it — ledger_engineer rule 9.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] State: two new IllegalTransitionCodes, regrade_stale (next computed before the stored receipt, compared as instants; equal is allowed) and regrade_not_unasked. An override whose at is before computedAt is invalid_event, checked before the result is built so it never comes out as invalid_result. Regrade checks run in this order: state, then next's schema, then subject (id), then stale, then UNASKED — HR4, HR6, HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] state.ts no longer runs its own lifecycleConsistent check, because the Receipt schema now enforces the lifecycle reasons. A stored receipt that breaks them is still invalid_receipt, and a next that breaks them is still invalid_event — HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] The Receipt invariants moved to a new src/core/schema-receipt.ts (my schema*.ts ownership), because schema-checks.ts would otherwise reach 426 lines. Problem now allows a nested path, so override.at errors are reported at ['override','at']. Lifecycle errors are reported at ['reasons']; the order check runs only when presence and params are right, so each case reports one problem — HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] The Claim id check reuses the Observation id helper (derivedIdProblems) and runs only when rawHash is valid lowercase hex, so a bad hash is reported once at its own path — HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Numeric reason params: -0 is accepted as zero, with no schema transform. Canonical JSON (and so every hash) writes it as 0, as §15 requires — HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Test layout: the v1.4 attribution rules are in a new attribution-v14.spec.ts, and the Receipt table moved from schema.spec.ts to a new schema-receipt.spec.ts. This keeps every file ≤ 400 lines and every function or describe ≤ 60 lines.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Ledger fixtures: makeClaim derives its id from its final rawHash (mirroring makeObservation), and a new claimId(n) helper is added. The duplicate-rawHash and unreadable-row tests became same-id re-sightings, and 'existing id with another rawHash' now expects invalid_input, following the [1.contract-v1.3-impl] observation precedent.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Bus harness: CLAIM gets its own rawHash ('cd'×32) with ULID_CLAIM = idFromHash of it, so the claim and observation fixtures do not share one hash.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Mutation testing ran only in a scratchpad mirror (node_modules and policy symlinked), never in the shared tree.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] regrade refuses a superseded `next` with invalid_event, the same code as a USER_OVERRIDDEN next. §8 requires next to be grade()'s receipt, and grade() never supersedes, so a supersession can only come from the supersede event. Simplest option: one predicate, no new error code — HR4, HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] regrade still drops a stored PENDING receipt's supersededBy, as §8's literal 'PENDING → next' says. I did not invent a carry-over rule and raised it as a contract issue instead; the new test does not pin either behaviour.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Not added: a claimId-equality check in regrade's subject test. §8 defines 'same subject' as 'same id', and grade() derives the id from the subject; noted as a residual.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] Perf was judged by an interleaved A/B in private mirrors rather than one gate run, because the shared machine's load average was 50 to 106 on 8 cores. The gate itself passed four times in a row once load fell.
- 2026-10-02 [1.v14-core] All probes, mirrors and mutation runs live in scratchpad/v14attr-adv/ (cur, attrhead, headcore and mut mirrors, with node_modules and policy symlinked); nothing outside the owned files was written in the shared tree.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract] UNASKED finality is measured from the same anchor as attribution: the earliest observedAt for a calendar.* orphan, else occurredAt (conditions-finality.ts, contract §7). Measuring from the event start left an event created today for next month PENDING for a month while every attribution signal was read at the sighting. Protects HR5 and HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract] A superseded receipt is never regraded: regrade on a receipt with supersededBy throws IllegalTransitionError('regrade_superseded'). Its observation is fixed to the superseding FINAL claim, so the caller deletes a PENDING one; the literal 'PENDING → next' would silently drop supersededBy and put it back in counts. Refusing beats carrying the marker over, which would also need the lifecycle reason rebuilt. Protects HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract] The §8 preconditions are now stated as refusals in the contract (regrade_superseded, subject_mismatch, regrade_stale, regrade_not_unasked, invalid_event for a non-grade() next and for an override dated before computedAt), matching state.ts and golden 93. Protects HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract] §3 now says a claim with observedAt > evaluatedAt gets no receipt, and the §3 and §13 gradeBatch signatures match batch.ts ({receipt, inputs}[]; fixed as {observationId, claimId | null}[], null = taken). Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.contract] coverage.no_parser is stated per entry: for any claim whose type reads gmail.receipt and that has no parser, every gmail.receipt entry is capped, and no_parser is emitted on partial_coverage when a capped gmail.receipt entry overlaps the window. The shipped policy has no mixed-source receipt type, so both earlier readings agree today; the general rule is the safer one. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] The runner checks every input gradeBatch returns, on every receipt of every batch grading (the first grading and each regrade): the policy hash, the grant in force (latest updatedAt ≤ evaluatedAt, compared by grantHash), the context shape, and a replay. The replay must give back the identical receipt: grade(subject, candidates, inputs.grant, policy, coverage, inputs.context). Candidates are the claim's candidates() over observations seen by that instant, minus observations of other claim receipts and every fixed or taken observation. A claim that is not assignable gets []; UNASKED gets [earliest sighting of the orphan]; a scope gets []. This is the strongest form of 'assert the inputs' and needs nothing typed by hand. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] In grade mode the inputs are the runner's own call, so they get no replay check. The attributionScore of an UNASKED receipt is now read from the inputs it was graded with (batch), or from the runner's own attribute() call (grade mode).
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Stated inputs (`expect.inputs`) are optional and allowed in batch mode only: grant (a grant ref), priorSpendCents and scope (context.scope.observed). A grant is compared by grantHash and also by agent and updatedAt, so a failure message is readable.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] A claim whose observedAt is after evaluatedAt is passed to gradeBatch and must get no receipt. This takes §3's filter sentence over 'grades exactly the PENDING claims it is given', and matches the in-progress batch.ts (claimsSeenBy). checks.ts expects no receipt for such a claim. I dropped a planned batchClaims input field as unnecessary. Protects HR4 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Events: `regrade` (batch mode) re-runs gradeBatch on the same built objects with only coverage.evaluatedAt replaced, then applies transition(regrade, next) with next = the new receipt of the same subject. `deleted` models the caller's deletion of a PENDING UNASKED receipt that the new grading no longer returns. `rejected` means transition must throw IllegalTransitionError and nothing changes. The suite never asserts state.ts error codes, so it does not depend on their names. Protects HR4 and HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] computedAt and finalAt are expected from the grading that produced the receipt's current version: the scenario's evaluatedAt, or the instant of its last applied regrade.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Scenario 90 patches out partial_coverage, so its partial two-source claim falls to default_unverifiable, which adds no reason code. This keeps the file off an open question: how many coverage.partial/none/grace.waiting reasons a multi-source claim carries. Protects HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Scenario 86 keeps its calendar UNASKED PENDING both when finality is measured from occurredAt and when it is measured from the first sighting, so the file stays off the open finality-anchor reading. Protects HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Scenario 93 was restructured so the evidence is visible at the first grading (VERIFIED, PENDING) and only VERIFIED finality waits for the later sync. Another owner's spec, tests/unit/core/features-golden.spec.ts, cross-checks every claim score at the scenario's own evaluatedAt, and my first version broke it.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] No priorSpendCents is stated for claim-less receipts, because §3 defines the field for claims only.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Added grants.ts (grantInForce and grantProblems). Every check and proof that looked up 'the agent's grant' (checks.ts, checks-reasons.ts, v12/v13 proofs) now uses the grant in force. 'Two grants for one agent' became 'two grants of one agent at one instant'.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Added `adjacentMin` to PolicyView (build.ts) for the adjacency proofs.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] To stay within 400 lines per file and 60 per function, new code went into separate files: runner-inputs.ts, runner-diff.ts (diffScenario moved here; golden.spec imports it from here), checks-events.ts, grants.ts, v14-rules.ts, v14-rules-events.ts, v14-rules.spec.ts and runner-v14.spec.ts. runner.ts re-exports RUNNER_BUCKET_MINUTES.

- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Kept the implementer's reading that a claim with observedAt > evaluatedAt gets no receipt (88, 94). It matches §3's filter sentence and the code, and stays listed as a contract issue. Protects HR4 and HR5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Kept 93's assertion that an override dated before computedAt throws IllegalTransitionError, not some other error. The §8 diagram text says every illegal pair throws it, and state.ts uses invalid_event. The contract issue asks §8 to say so. Protects HR6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Fixed 89 inside the file instead of adding a new scenario: one file now pins list-order independence and the ≤ boundary. The boundary instant is written in +01:00 so it also catches text comparison. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Added the claim-first currency case to 81, the existing currency-param scenario, instead of a new file. That keeps both branches of the §15 v1.4 rule together. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Ran mutation testing on a disposable copy of src/core in a uniquely named scratch directory (the shared scratchpad had removed my first one), so the real core was never touched. Removed the copy afterwards.
- 2026-10-02 [1.golden-v14] Treated the redundant-filter mutants (attribution's own late-claim filter, batch's scope-outcome filter, state.ts's override.at check behind the schema) as equivalent. The suite catches each one once both layers are removed.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] LedgerSnapshot keeps receiptInputs and policies as ReadonlyMaps keyed by inputsHash and policyHash, and holds loaded Policy objects. The JSON export uses objects with the same keys and stores raw policy documents; the CLI loads each one and rejects it unless it hashes to its key (exit 2, [policy_hash]). Protects HR4: no ambiguous keys, and the policy is the one that graded.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] Which claims were in the batch at T is inferred from the stored rows alone: claims first seen by T whose latest receipt was computed at or after T. A claim the snapshot lacks is in no batch. If one claim has two rows, the later (computedAt, then id) decides. HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] Fixed pairs start strictly after the receipt's own computedAt (never in the batch that graded it). FINAL fixes its observationIds. An override fixes its stored observationIds from `at` on (none for NOT_FOUND, my reading of §4.3). Before `at`, the overridden state applies: FINAL as FINAL, PENDING fixes nothing. An overridden UNASKED receipt marks its orphan taken from `at` on; a FINAL UNASKED fixes nothing. HR4, HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] The assignment is rebuilt with the §13 reference path (candidates + features + assign + exclusiveCandidates), not by rerunning gradeBatch. gradeBatch needs grants, contexts and activity for claims not replayed at that instant, which are not stored. The reference path also cross-checks the batch's fast path: any disagreement shows up as a mismatch. HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] Beyond the four contract fields, replay also compares `id` (the subject of the replayed receipt). It also checks that an UNASKED receipt's observation is still an orphan in the rebuilt batch (field `orphan`), because gradeBatch would never emit UNASKED for an assigned or taken observation. HR4, HR7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] Failures are reported, never thrown: receiptInputs, policy, subject, orphan, error. Errors from grade() or assign() become 'error' mismatches labelled `name:code`, never the message. One bad receipt cannot hide the rest of the report. HR6, HR10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] Only grade and ruleId (and inputsHash with its row moved) are asserted to give exactly one mismatch. A stored state legitimately decides later batches' fixed pairs: marking a FINAL receipt that holds evidence as PENDING releases it, and other receipts change. The state test uses a receipt that holds nothing.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] The replay memory index uses a 1-minute bucket, since core allows no literal other than 0/1. The bucket only affects how much a read over-returns, never the result.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] The CLI imports LedgerExport from src/ledger/export.ts and extends it, so the format is literally the Sprint 0 shape plus two keys. Importing Dexie under tsx works (verified).
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] CLI exit codes: 0 all reproduced, 1 any mismatch, 2 bad arguments or unusable export. The summary lists the first 20 mismatches and counts the rest. Relative paths resolve from pnpm's INIT_CWD, because the script itself runs in extension/.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] No fixture export is committed. The tests write exports to a temp dir from the hand-built scenario and from a synthetic ledger. A committed export would need a path outside my ownership, and would break on every intended grading change.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] The synthetic world (seeded mulberry32) is shared by the property, unit and CLI tests. It mixes honest, contested, random, vague and no-source claims; calendar and purchase claims; an unbound agent; coverage gaps; an undeclared scope; an allowlist; a disallowed type; a mid-ledger grant change; and a mid-ledger policy change. The last batch leaves some receipts PENDING.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] Property run counts: Done-when 5 runs at full size (each builds and replays a 1,000 x 10,000 ledger twice) with a 600 s timeout. Small ledgers: 60, 40 and 30 runs.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] Replay compares the whole computed receipt, not just the four fields from §9. Under §9's own reading, 'a receipt that grading its stored inputs again does not reproduce' must be reported. The lifecycle part (override, supersededBy and their reasons) records events, not grading, so it is set aside, as state.ts says only regrade writes computed fields. Protects HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] computedAt is compared as text. A rewritten offset at the same instant is reported as computedAt alone; grouping stays by instant. That is the simplest exact rule. HR4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] signals and reasons appear in the report as SHA-256 hashes, and arrays and numbers as canonical JSON. The report keeps ids, hashes, enums, numbers and timestamps only. HR2, HR10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.9] Observation integrity is recomputed from the stored observation, as SHA-256 of canonicalJson minus id, observedAt and contentHash. A test pins it to redact.project so the two cannot drift. It is checked only for observations a receipt is graded with, each once per replay (a per-call WeakMap). It is reported per receipt as 'contentHash'. HR4, HR2.

- 2026-10-02 [1.11] Root `pnpm check-docs` added and chained into `pnpm lint`, so the pre-commit hook and CI enforce docs_writer rule 4 on every commit, not only through the check-docs.spec test. Protects HR12 (no doc claims a command or rule that does not exist).
- 2026-10-02 [1.11] check-docs counts a policy rule id as documented only when it appears as an inline code span outside fenced blocks. This is stricter than 'appears': the prose word 'partial' and the §12 JSON listing would otherwise pass for every id. It found that `unasked` was never named that way.
- 2026-10-02 [1.11] A pnpm command counts as quoted only inside an inline code span or a fenced block; prose mentions are not checked. pnpm's own commands (install, exec, dlx, add, …) need no script. `run` and `run-script` take the next word as the script. `test` and `start` are treated as scripts. Global options --dir/-C/--filter/-F/--reporter/--loglevel skip their value.
- 2026-10-02 [1.11] Links: anything with a URL scheme or starting with // is external and skipped. Query strings are stripped. Percent-encoding is decoded. A leading / resolves from the repo root. A link that resolves outside the repo root is reported even if the file exists. #anchors are checked only when the target is a .md file. docs/*.md is scanned non-recursively, as specified.
- 2026-10-02 [1.11] Heading anchors reproduce github-slugger: lowercase, keep \p{L}\p{M}\p{Nd}\p{Nl}\p{Pc}, space and '-', each space becomes '-', duplicates get -1, -2. Inline code text is kept and link/emphasis markup dropped, and HTML id/name attributes count as anchors. No reference slugger is installed and installs are forbidden, so test cases pin the behaviour (em dash gives '--', § is dropped, non-Latin letters kept, emoji dropped).
- 2026-10-02 [1.11] Markdown scanning is a deliberate subset of CommonMark: code spans are matched within one line, fences are recognised at any indentation (fences nested in list items count), and indented four-space code blocks are not recognised. The repo docs use fences.
- 2026-10-02 [1.11] check-docs takes an optional --root <dir> for testability. It exits 2 on bad arguments, 1 on problems and 0 when clean, and problems are sorted by file, line and message (code-unit order).
- 2026-10-02 [1.11] The SVG has no prefers-color-scheme media query: in an <img> it follows the OS, not the page, and Playwright showed the primary text vanishing when the GitHub theme differs from the OS theme. It uses one mid-tone palette instead: #6e7781 gray, #a06c00 amber, #238636 green, #8957e5 purple, with a transparent background. Contrast on #ffffff and #0d1117 is 3.89–4.87:1, so every colour clears the 3.8:1 floor and four of five clear 4:1. The best a single colour can reach on both is about 4.35:1. A unit test enforces the 3.8:1 floor. (The SVG's own comment and the test's description still say 4:1, which the gray falls just short of on #0d1117; the code and these numbers are correct.)

- 2026-10-02 [1.11] A link is broken when its letter case differs from the file on disk, even where the local filesystem resolves it. This is the simplest rule that matches how GitHub and Linux CI resolve links. The test computes its expected message from the filesystem's case sensitivity, so it passes and bites on both macOS and Linux.
- 2026-10-02 [1.11] A trailing '/' on a link to a file is reported as broken, because the filesystem itself says such a path does not exist (existsSync('file.md/') is false). A trailing '/' on a directory is fine.

This reuses the scanner's existing NOT_PARAGRAPH rule instead of adding a CommonMark parser, and the repo docs contain no wrapped links today (checked).

- 2026-10-02 [1.close] SUPERSEDES the [1.5] reading 'cumulativeAboveCeiling needs claim.currency === spendCurrency (the §6.1 parenthetical, read literally)' (TECH-C3): a claim's amount is counted in spendCurrency when its currency is absent or equals the grant's spendCurrency. Leaving the currency out never lets spend escape the cumulative ceiling. — Hard Rule 5

- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] Baseline is Unicode 15.0.0. Chrome 120 is the oldest supported Chrome (wxt.config.ts minimum_chrome_version), and it ships ICU 73.1 / Unicode 15.0. I verified this from Chromium's own files: the DEPS at tags 120.0.6099.0 and 120.0.6099.234 both pin icu.git a622de35, whose uvernum.h says ICU 73.1 and whose uchar.h says Unicode 15.0. I also checked Chromium 123.0.6312.4: it still reads 15.0, with U+2EBF0 not a letter and U+1CCF0 not a digit. So every supported engine has at least 15.0. Protects Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] How it works. Character properties come only from tables generated from the UCD 15.0.0 files. Normalisation and lowercasing still use the engine, but only on text in which every code point 15.0 does not assign has been swapped for a permanent noncharacter of the same length (U+FDD0, or U+1FFFE above the BMP). A noncharacter is unassigned in every Unicode version, so the engine treats it exactly as a 15.0 engine treats a newer character. Readers that keep text in their output (name(), stop-word keys) put the original code points back. So nothing stored, hashed or compared ever carries the substitute character. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] Code points 15.0 leaves unassigned but already marks Default_Ignorable (U+2065, U+FFF0–U+FFF8, and unassigned parts of E0000–E0FFF) are not swapped. They are stripped as invisible, which is what a 15.0 engine does. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] Final sigma is decided by our code, not the engine. U+1171E moved from Mn to Mc in Unicode 16.0, so it stopped being case-ignorable, and 'A\u{1171E}Σ' lowercases to ς on Chromium 123 but σ on Node 22 and Chrome 153. I found this while finishing the fix; the reviewer's probe did not cover it. lowerBaseline decides final sigma from the 15.0 Cased and Case_Ignorable tables using ICU 73's algorithm: a character that is both cased and case-ignorable is skipped. I read that algorithm in ICU 73's ucase.cpp, isFollowedByCasedLetter. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] lowerBaseline also keeps a 15.0 character as it is when the engine would lowercase it into a character 15.0 does not assign. This has happened before: Unicode 8.0 gave existing Cherokee letters newly encoded lowercase partners. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] Committed source data: DerivedAge, DerivedGeneralCategory, PropList and WordBreakProperty from UCD 15.0.0, about 640 KB, under scripts/ucd/15.0.0/ with SHA-256 pinned in the generator. I did not commit DerivedCoreProperties.txt (1 MB). Instead the generator derives Default_Ignorable, Cased and Case_Ignorable, and I checked the derivations against that file once: 0 differences. The test pins the totals that file states (4,526 Cased, 2,707 Case_Ignorable). The files were fetched at dev time; nothing ships a network call, and the generator reads only the committed files. Hard Rules 4 and 9.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] The generated tables are regex class strings joined at module load, not numbers, so the core numeric-literal ban holds. The reader files compile each class once. ASCII text keeps its existing fast path, and every bench workload is ASCII. Non-ASCII text gets one extra table regex pass. I could only time it on a heavily loaded machine, where the difference was within noise; the perf gates measured it with the rest of the close (the [QA-R3] entries). Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] I added a static fence test, unicode-fence.spec.ts, so engine Unicode cannot creep back into src/core. Two files, policy-schema.ts and schema-text.ts, are listed as pending; the list can only shrink. Each needs the same change: read BASELINE_CLASS. (Superseded: both now read BASELINE_CLASS; the pending list is gone and no exemption may be added.) Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] I added a 'simulated later engine' property. It patches String.prototype normalize, toLowerCase and trim so that unassigned code points get compatibility, case and whitespace mappings, and a 15.0 letter gains a new lowercase partner. It proves the readers never let the engine see a newer code point, whatever Unicode version Node or Chrome moves to. Before this property existed, five mutants survived because Node 22 has no new Cf, whitespace, No or '@'-producing characters to tell them apart; now every one is caught. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] The baseline never moves for receipts graded under it. Moving it changes grading. It would first need the baseline version recorded in each receipt's inputs, and the 15.0 tables kept so old receipts can still be replayed. The generator refuses a DerivedAge newer than its pinned version, and a test pins UNICODE_BASELINE_VERSION to '15.0.0'. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] What is not covered: (a) how the engine normalises characters 15.0 already has; Unicode's normalisation stability policy keeps this fixed. (b) Lowercase mappings between two characters that both exist in 15.0; Unicode keeps existing case pairs, and so far has only paired existing characters with newly encoded ones, which lowerBaseline already refuses. unicode-tables.spec.ts fails on the engine running CI if either ever changes; on Node 22, toLowerCase matched UCD 15.0 UnicodeData and SpecialCasing for every 15.0 character, with 0 differences. (c) policy-schema.ts and schema-text.ts until they read the baseline classes too. (Superseded: both now read BASELINE_CLASS.) A disagreement there makes a policy or an observation fail validation loudly; it never silently changes a grade. Hard Rules 4 and 6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] Checked on real engines: 6,300 mixed strings through 12 readers, plus the SEC-1 grading probe. Node 22.14, Chromium 123.0.6312.4 and Chrome for Testing 153 give byte-identical results, equal to the old code running on Chromium 123, a true 15.0 engine. So no receipt already graded on Chrome 120–123 changes. Before the fix the three engines gave three different results. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] QA-R9: I deleted merchantMatchOf (no caller) rather than keeping dead code. The doc comment's claim that a property test pins the batch-path Jaccard to merchantMatchName is now true. The property sits in tests/unit/core/features-content-merchant.spec.ts (fast-check, 2,000 runs) beside the other tests of the batch path's content features; several unit specs already use fast-check. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] I removed the unused UNICODE_BASELINE_VERSION re-export from normalize.ts. The constant is still exported from unicode-baseline.ts.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-1] Test convention kept: every non-ASCII input in the tests is written as a \u escape (Decisions [1.1]). Some escapes had become raw characters while the tests were written, so they were re-escaped, then checked every test file the SEC-1 fix touched: no raw non-ASCII remains outside comments.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-2/SEC-4] The guard throws PolicyError with the new closed code 'policy_not_loaded', not GradeError or BatchError. There is one cause, so there is one typed error at every entry, and it lives in the policy module. The grade and batch error enums stay unchanged. No production caller catches BatchError or GradeError by type today. Callers detect it with `instanceof PolicyError && code === 'policy_not_loaded'`. Protects Hard Rules 4 and 6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-2/SEC-4] Membership is by object identity, in a module-private WeakSet that only loadPolicy fills. This refuses even a copy whose values are all valid, because a copy keeps the original's policyHash and its receipts would name a document they were not graded with. The set is weak and deterministic and holds no data. Protects Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-2/SEC-4] The guard goes where the defect is: every entry that stamps policyHash (grade, gradeOrphan, gradeClaim, gradeBatch), plus the only memo keyed on policy-part identity (features.ts claimPlan, and isAssignable before its early return). The attributor (attribute, attributor) and replay carry the same guard. Narrow helpers typed on a Pick of Policy are not guarded: candidates, assign, priorSpendCents/spendIndex, claimWindow, redact project and the coverage helpers. They read values on every call, keep no memo and stamp no hash, so they cannot give a stale result. Their tests pass out-of-domain values on purpose (window.spec afterClaimMin 0, redact-fields allowlist without occurredAt), and guarding them would weaken those tests. Protects Hard Rules 4 and 6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-2/SEC-4] The SEC-4 rule set, from §2's definitions. VERIFIED needs bestScoreGte 'verified'. PARTIAL needs bestScoreGte at either threshold: the loader already enforces partial < verified, so 'verified' implies at least partial, and claiming less than the evidence shows is not a guess. NOT_FOUND needs graceElapsed true, coverage 'full' and bestScoreLt 'partial', matching the shipped not_found rule exactly. bestScoreLt 'verified' is refused, because 'nothing matched' means below the partial threshold. This goes beyond the three outcomes the review named. UNASKED must name unaskedLikely true (Hard Rule 7 and ledger_engineer rule 6: UNASKED fires only above the threshold). OVER_SCOPE must name one out-of-grant fact set to true, because an unevidenced OVER_SCOPE is a false accusation (Hard Rule 5). UNVERIFIABLE and PENDING need nothing. The check is per rule, and rule order stays policy data (§6). Protects Hard Rules 5 and 7.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-2/SEC-4] I did not add the second SEC-4 guard inside grade(). After SEC-2, grade() only ever receives policies loadPolicy validated, so a rule re-check there would be dead code; the loader is the single gate. grade.ts claimlessRule and the unasked_with_claim guards in receipt.ts and conditions-finality.ts stay as defence in depth. Protects Hard Rules 4 and 5.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-2/SEC-4] Test migration. features-memo.spec's 'widerSameWords' policy is now a second load of the same document; a loaded policy cannot share parts with another by identity. features-memo.property's hand-built fourth policy and candidates-calendar's {windows} literal are now loaded too. Left as they are on purpose: window.spec withWindows (a WindowPolicy, not a Policy) and redact-fields handBuilt (a Pick of allowlistedFields, which the loader rejects by design). golden/build.ts PolicyView is also left: it is a loose read of the document for the scenario checks and redact, never passed to a grading entry. The golden runner, the tools (attest-world, attest-snapshot) and bench/core.ts already use loadPolicy or DEFAULT_POLICY.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-2/SEC-4] The gradeBatch refusal test counts index reads, because the attributor's own guard made the old test pass without the gradeBatch guard. It now pins 'refused before reading anything' (Hard Rule 6: fail loud before doing work).
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] SEC-5: grade() refuses a foreign candidate by throwing, not by silently filtering it out. A refusal keeps inputsHash and candidatesHash meaning exactly the candidates given, and a caller's bug can never become a permanent FINAL receipt (Hard Rules 4, 5, 6).
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] SEC-5: a candidate first seen after evaluatedAt is ignored, not refused, although the SEC-5 finding listed observedAt > evaluatedAt as foreign. docs/grading.md §3 defines such candidates as dropped inputs. Replay builds candidates with candidates() over the whole ledger snapshot, which can hold later sightings; refusing them would make old receipts unreplayable as soon as a newer sighting landed in their window (Hard Rule 4). grade() never reads them, so they cannot cause a false grade (Hard Rule 5). A test pins this behaviour.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] SEC-5: the check runs only in grade(), the path replay and tests use. It does not run in gradeBatch's gradeClaim path, whose evidence comes from batch retrieval built on the same order.ts and window.ts predicates. The oracle tests and a 1,000-run property pin the two together, and the 2,000 ms perf gate pays nothing (Hard Rule 4). Measured cost in grade(): about 1.1–2.4 µs per candidate, 5–12% of grade() time on the bench workload.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] SEC-5: for calendar.created, the first-seen check reads the candidate row's own observedAt, because grade() sees one representative per event, not the whole group of sightings. This never refuses a real candidate: the group's first sighting is no later than the representative's. A row that fails the check could never have been a candidate.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] SEC-5: a candidate's source must be one the policy lists for the claim's type. This matches candidates() and batch retrieval, which now filter by relevant source (the TECH-C1/C4 change through order.ts). Evidence the claim's coverage never reads cannot verify it (Hard Rule 5).
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] TECH-C13: gradeBatch takes the union of activity.claims and the graded claims it omits, rather than throwing BatchError('claim_not_in_activity'). An omitted claim is still the user's claim and has one obviously right reading. Ids are deduplicated (the activity.claims copy wins), so nothing counts twice. The union also feeds adjacency suppression (§7). Hard Rules 5 and 6: the cumulative ceiling is never skipped silently.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] TECH-C3: 'counted in spendCurrency' now means the claim's currency is absent or equals the grant's spendCurrency. That one definition is used for the §6.1 Amount (unchanged), the cumulativeAboveCeiling guard (which now admits currency-less claims) and priorSpendCents/spendIndex. Those read a missing currency on the claim or on a prior as spendCurrency; with no spendCurrency, currency-less counts only currency-less. This supersedes the literal reading logged at Decisions.md:526 (Hard Rule 5: leaving the currency out never lets spend escape the ceiling). spendCurrency is an optional 4th argument, so existing callers that do not pass it keep the old reading.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] TECH-C3 consequence: in golden 69, c1 and c2 are both currency-less 3000 claims, so c2 now breaks the cumulative ceiling (3000 + 3000 > 5000). That is a claim-only OVER_SCOPE fact, so under the existing grade.ts waitsForCandidate rule c2 is FINAL at once instead of PENDING. This follows from the contract; the scenario needs a smaller c2 amount to keep testing 'waits like PARTIAL' (done: the [TECH-C3] golden 69 and 98 entry).
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] TECH-C5: attributionReasons is kept as the single signal-to-reason map, and SIGNAL_REASON is made private. Receipts are byte-identical because receipt.ts sorts reasons by code within a group. I deleted scoreGteBasisPoints (ratio.ts) and CENTS_PER_UNIT (units.ts), as TECH-C5 asked.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] TECH-C7: a new leaf module, invariant-error.ts (imports nothing), rather than reusing HashError or BatchError. sha256.ts sits below hash.ts in the §13 dependency order and must not import upward (no cycles). Codes are closed and messages carry the code only (Hard Rules 6, 10).
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] SEC-6: an unknown reason code throws ReasonTextError('unknown_code') with no detail. The detail type is narrowed to the closed vocabulary (a validated ReasonCode, or code.param from the render table), so the type system enforces this (Hard Rule 10).
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] QA-R10: the unreachable fallback becomes a typed throw, WindowError('no_offset'), rather than a test of dead code. writtenOffsetMinutes is exported so the throw is tested directly (Hard Rule 6: never guess an offset).
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] attributor() asserts a loaded policy, like every other entry that takes a Policy (Hard Rule 6). No existing test passed a hand-built policy to it: 58 caller and owned test files, 1,232 tests, all pass.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [grade-guards] 60-line rule: the grade-guard changes split over-long describes only in the test files they touched. The other over-long describes were split by the TECH-R8, QA-G13 and TECH-R6 changes later in this close: batch-inputs.spec.ts:95 (131 lines), batch-visibility.spec.ts:87 (78), conditions-finality.spec.ts:140 (84), conditions.spec.ts:131 (78), receipt.spec.ts:157 (61) plus the file at 462 lines, state-grace-full.spec.ts:134 (66) and state-grace.spec.ts:79 (63).
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] The one-line rule rejects exactly \p{Cc} (which includes tab, CR, LF, VT, FF, NUL, ESC, DEL and U+0085), U+2028 and U+2029. Together these are every Unicode mandatory line break plus the other control characters. Invisible format marks (\p{Cf}: zero-width, bidi) stay allowed, because §11 strips them when reading names and subjects and real vendor rows contain them. Protects Hard Rule 2 without turning honest rows into refused ones (Hard Rule 5).
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] No shape limit beyond one line, such as 'must be an address' or 'a few tokens'. §5.1 says a display-name form may slip into an observation and is reduced through email(), and claims legitimately carry names and merchants, so the contract does not support a stricter rule. The review's suggestion to drop a counterparty that is not an address is rejected: dropping would turn a row into evidence of absence (Hard Rule 5). Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] project() refuses the whole row with RedactError('invalid_field') whenever an allowlisted own value other than occurredAt fails its field schema: multi-line text, a wrong type, an over-long value, a boxed or coercible object, or null. It never drops, cuts or coerces the value; only subjectHint is first cut to 80 code points, per §10. A refused row makes the observer DEGRADED instead of storing a row that silently lacks a fact. Hard Rules 5 and 6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] Only undefined means a field is absent; null is refused. An invalid or absent occurredAt keeps its documented code, missing_occurred_at. When another allowlisted field is also invalid, invalid_field is reported first. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] observationIntegrityProblems does not check rowKeyHash against the allowlist. This mirrors project(), which keeps rowKeyHash whenever the observer has a row id, so every project() output passes the check, which both property tests assert. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] upsertObservation takes a required policy argument (no hidden default) and, new in this close, refuses any policy object that loadPolicy did not return, throwing PolicyError('policy_not_loaded') the way grade, gradeBatch and features do. The allowlist that gates the ledger therefore always comes from a validated document; an in-memory copy with a widened allowlist cannot widen it. It throws PolicyError rather than LedgerError because a bad policy is a caller bug, not bad row data. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] project() and observationIntegrityProblems still take Pick<Policy, 'allowlistedFields'> and do not check that the policy was loaded. The redaction property test deliberately passes allowlists the loader rejects, to prove project()'s own defences hold without the loader. The loaded-policy check sits at the ledger write, the single gate. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] The bus schema (src/bus/messages.ts) does not run the integrity check. It has no policy in hand, and giving it one would create a second source of truth. The ledger write is the single gate; the messages.ts header documents this, and repo-integrity.spec shows the bus accepting the forged row while the ledger refuses it. 2.7 carry-forward: the background handler stores observations only through upsertObservation with the loaded policy. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] A write whose contentHash and id already exist but whose content was edited is refused (invalid_input) rather than silently treated as a duplicate, so a stored row cannot be shadowed. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] contentHashOf lives in redact.ts and returns the branded Sha256. replay-compare.ts keeps its exported contentHashOf, which now just calls redact's, so there is one definition and replay.ts is unchanged (TECH-C4, redact part). The golden loader's own contentHashOf (tests/golden/build.ts) stays independent on purpose: redact-golden.spec proves project() matches it. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] Literal invisible characters in test sources (U+2028, U+2029, zero-width, bidi override, no-break space) are written as \u escapes, so what a reviewer sees is what the test checks. Hard Rule 2 (reviewability of the redaction tests).
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] Sprint 2 carry-forward for observers (Hard Rules 5 and 6): an observer must not report a window as seen when it refused a row inside it. If project() throws RedactError, or the ledger throws LedgerError('invalid_input'), during a pass, that pass is DEGRADED and writes no coverage entry for its window. Extractors collapse whitespace in names and subjects before calling project(). This is already in sprints/sprint_2.md.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] Sprint 2.7 carry-forward (Hard Rules 2 and 6): the background handler for observer.observations stores each observation only through upsertObservation(db, obs, DEFAULT_POLICY or another loadPolicy result), never with db.observations.put or add. On LedgerError('invalid_input') it logs the code and observerId only, marks that observer DEGRADED and drops that batch's coverage. A PolicyError there is a bug and must fail loud.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [SEC-3/SEC-V1] Sprint 3 carry-forward for adapters (Hard Rule 6): Claim.counterparty and Claim.subjectHint are one line. An adapter collapses whitespace before building a Claim, and a Claim the schema refuses marks the adapter DEGRADED rather than vanishing silently. This is already in sprints/sprint_3.md.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [TECH-C1/TECH-C4] candidates(), and therefore the batch's retrieval, returns only observations from a source the policy lists for the claim's type (§6.1 relevant sources). The SEC-5 change makes grade() refuse any other candidate with foreign_candidate. Without this filter, gradeBatch would throw on a row the schema allows (the replay property hit it), and evidence the claim's coverage never reads could verify the claim. Such a row is graded as an orphan instead. Protects Hard Rules 4, 5 and 6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [TECH-C1/TECH-C4] The one orphan rule follows §3 and Decisions [1.v14-core] literally: a row is an orphan candidate when its type has sources and its own source's observer saw its occurredAt, whatever sources the policy lists for its type. The batch's existing reading is kept and replay's divergent extra check is deleted, since the stored receipts were issued under the batch's reading. Protects Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [TECH-C1/TECH-C4] replay() throws PolicyError('policy_not_loaded') before replaying anything when the snapshot holds an unloaded policy, even one no receipt names. That is a caller's bug, not ledger tampering, so failing once beats one error mismatch per receipt. The attest CLI loads every policy through loadPolicy, so it never hits this. Protects Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [TECH-C1/TECH-C4] The shared helpers live in a new internal module, src/core/order.ts. It imports only candidates-time, units, window and types, so it creates no cycle. The memory index's storage order also uses its comparators. hasFirstSeenRule is the single place that says the first-seen rule applies to calendar.created only; firstSeenFromMs and batch retrieval both read it. Protects Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [TECH-C1/TECH-C4] attest-world now draws one extra random number per observation, for the 5% stray-source rows. Every seeded synthetic ledger therefore changes. No committed fixture depends on them (Decisions [1.9]: no fixture export is committed), and every test regenerates its own ledgers. Stray rows take their fields from their source, not their type, so they remain rows project() could return; the Done-when property asserts this with observationIntegrityProblems.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [TECH-C1/TECH-C4] Small-ledger replay and tamper properties use exactly the sizes QA-R2 asked for (1–20 claims, 1–100 observations, 2–4 batches, 0–3 overrides per gap) at 1,000 examples. This costs about 86 s more test time at load 9–15. qa_tester rule 4 wins over CI time.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [TECH-C1/TECH-C4] The SEC-7 fix escapes in two places on purpose: attest-snapshot quotes keys in paths and replaces Zod's key-echoing messages, and the CLI passes every output line through printableLines. A later message that echoes input still cannot drive the terminal. Unassigned code points (\p{Cn}) are escaped too, so what counts as printable depends on Node's Unicode tables, which errs on the safe side. Protects Hard Rule 10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [TECH-C1/TECH-C4] Measured perf: grading gate median 1604.2 ms CPU (runs 2007.7, 1604.2, 1256.7 ms) and candidate lookup median 372.3 ms CPU, at load 6.9–7.9. Interleaved before/after comparison of batch retrieval plus orphan discovery (5,000 claims × 50,000 observations, 20 rounds): old 167.9 vs new 173.6 ms CPU, then old 185.7 vs new 184.0 ms CPU at load 6–8. No slower within noise, so no further optimisation.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] src/core may import exactly three things: its own sibling modules written './kebab-name' (no subfolder, no extension, the file must exist), the bare package 'zod', and '../../../policy/default.json', the path src/core/policy.ts uses today. Type-only imports and re-exports fall under the same allowlist, because a type import from '../clock' is a step toward a value import. The named bans stay for their clearer messages. Protects Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] WeakRef and FinalizationRegistry are banned in src/core as globals and as window/globalThis/self properties, because their results depend on when garbage collection runs. Protects Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] attest/core-determinism uses type information when the parser provides it (the real `pnpm lint` run). It reports date.toString(), a bare date.toString reference, String(date), and join/toString over arrays or tuples of dates. Template literals and + on a Date were already reported by restrict-template-expressions and restrict-plus-operands. Without type information (virtual files in lint tests) the check is skipped, so a test also proves the real config gives src/core the projectService. This supersedes the [0.close] note that Date#toString 'cannot be told apart without types'. The test:tz CI run stays as the fallback. Protects Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] eslint.config.js was split into eslint-rules/fences.js (extension scopes) and eslint-rules/core-fences.js (src/core) to stay under 400 lines. I compared the effective config for 20 representative files across every scope with HEAD: identical except src/core, which only gained entries. Neither attest/no-act nor no-restricted-imports was weakened. Protects Hard Rules 1, 3 and 4.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] The grep test (core-purity.spec.ts) scans for the whole word 'performance', not just performance.now. It also catches `new Date` without parentheses, Date() called as a function, setImmediate, 'crypto.' and 'chrome.'. It requires at least 67 .ts files, the count after this close's fixes (60 were committed before it). Protects Hard Rule 4 and the no-vacuous-pass rule.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] A Vitest tag 'gate' is declared in vitest.config.ts. Vitest's strict tags reject an undeclared one, and tooling-config.spec.ts pins the tag. All five describe blocks of core-purity.spec.ts, and every describe block of core-poison.spec.ts, carry it, so a gate run also runs the negative controls that prove the scanner works. The title keeps '@gate' for grep.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] There is one suite-wide test timeout of 60 s and no retries anywhere. The per-file PROPERTY_TIMEOUT_MS, TIMEOUT_MS and COMPILE_TIMEOUT_MS constants were left for a later change in this close (see the [infra] timeout rule entry, which supersedes this sentence). Decisions [1.5] is not changed, because the 300 s value it records exists. Protects qa_tester rule 10.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] Coverage gates only on lines >= 95 for src/core. Branches (92.9%) and functions are reported but not gated, because the sprint Gate names lines. Reporters are text and text-summary only, so no HTML artifacts land in the tree. CI runs coverage as its own step after `pnpm test`, which roughly doubles the unit-test time in CI. Protects Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] A bench gate passes on the median CPU time of fresh runs, the perf tests' statistic. The bench keeps 5 runs where the perf tests use 3: same statistic, more samples. Wall median, min and max are still reported, and gateMetric/gradingGateMetric rows name the gated metric in RESULTS.md. Any metric named gatePassed or *GatePassed that is not exactly true counts as a failed gate, so a malformed result cannot pass. `pnpm bench` writes its numbers first, then exits 1. Protects Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] Bench provenance records the commit SHA, a commitDirty flag that ignores the bench's own outputs (bench/results/, RESULTS.md), and the node version. Without git, the commit is 'unknown' and the tree counts as dirty. Protects Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] bench-gates.spec.ts pins bench/core.ts's gate flags by reading its source, because running the 5,000 × 50,000 bench in a unit test would take minutes.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] check-docs looks up a quoted pnpm script in the package the command runs in, not in the union of all package.json files. A `cd` carries only to the end of its code block. A directory a doc cannot know (cd ~, an absolute path, --dir leaving the repo) is reported as a problem, not passed. -r excludes the root unless --include-workspace-root is given, as pnpm does. Indented code is list-aware: four spaces inside a list item continue the item, and code needs four more. HTML comments hide links, commands and headings. Protects Hard Rule 12: no doc claims a command that does not exist.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [infra] extension/tests/unit/bench/bench-gates.spec.ts is the test of the bench tooling (bench/*.ts) and lives with the other unit tests; it is new in this close.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [golden] features-golden check 1: I chose the TECH-R4 option of applying the same 'seen by the grading instant' and calendar.created first-seen filters, not the option of restricting check 1 to scenarios without events. Restricting would leave it wrong for an event-free scenario with a row observed after evaluatedAt, and would drop coverage. The cross-check now reads exactly the §3/§4.1 candidates grade() sees. Hard Rule 4: a correct oracle for deterministic grading; Hard Rule 5: it never credits evidence that was not visible.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [golden] I added a third scenario (97) beyond the two the review asked for. Without it nothing proved the first-seen part of the cross-check (mutant FG5 survived), which breaks the 'a test that fails without the fix' rule. Hard Rule 5: an event already on the calendar can never verify 'created'.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [golden] Scenario 95 ends PARTIAL, PENDING, not VERIFIED, FINAL (the probes' shape). It needs a better row observed after the regrade, so the observedAt filter in check 1 is exercised. A FINAL regrade needs a sync at or after 14:48, and by the observer obligations that pass would have seen the better row. Hard Rule 5: realistic, self-consistent ledgers.
- 2026-10-02 [1.close] [golden] Scenario 95's first pass has no Gmail coverage at all (no_coverage). A pass covering 14:02 that did not see o1 would contradict the §3 observer obligation that a pass sees every row in its span. Observed-after-evaluatedAt values are written explicitly, because the loader default only reads entries synced by the first pass.
