Anything missing or invalid makes the whole pass DEGRADED with the landmark's code, and nothing is extracted. Why: a row skipped in silence would report its send as absent. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] Value-level time failures refuse only that row, through the 2.0 contract: the extractor returns the row with empty fields, project() refuses it (redact.missing_occurred_at), the other rows are still posted, and visibleWindow returns null (window.unknown), so there is no coverage. The time-unresolved fixture's label was aligned from row.time_unresolved to [redact.missing_occurred_at, window.unknown], because plan.ts has no per-row extractor reason. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] Time reading: the time element's title, then its aria-label, then its text, each through normalize.resolveRelative at ctx.observedAt with ctx.offsetAt. The first that reads as a time decides, so a title naming only a day is never overruled by a clock in the text. Only minute precision is a send time. A day-only text ('Oct 1') or a DST-ambiguous or skipped clock time ('1:30 AM' on the fall-back day, '2:30 AM' on the spring-forward day) refuses the row. U+202F folds to a space. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] Conversation view: a list row's time is the conversation's newest message, which can be a reply the user received. A row is a send only when data-legacy-thread-id = data-legacy-last-message-id = data-legacy-last-non-draft-message-id. That rests on Gmail's legacy thread id being the first message's id (best knowledge; verify in 2.8): the row then holds exactly one message, and it is in Sent. Any other row (a reply in the thread, a draft on it, an id missing) is refused and the pass is DEGRADED. Known cost: on real mailboxes, any Sent page holding a replied thread (or, with conversation view off, a reply the user sent inside a thread) will report DEGRADED. 2.8 must measure how often. Hard Rule 5 is put above coverage yield.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] Coverage window per docs/grading.md §3. windowStart is the oldest row's minute. windowEnd is ctx.observedAt at the head of the list (route page 1 and no visible enabled [role=button][aria-label=Newer]; the English label is only a contradiction guard, and if it is not found the route decides), else the newest row's minute. A list with no rows is selector.missing.row, because an empty Sent folder cannot be told apart from a list still loading. Never a window in the future for gmail.*: plan.ts enforces window.future. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] Account: the role=banner element whose aria-label ends with '(<address>)'. The localised words before it are ignored. Addresses are compared after ASCII lowercasing, and two different addresses are selector.ambiguous.account. Health requires the account rather than sending coverage without one, because a silent capped coverage would hide the break. Only accountHashOf(address) leaves the content script. readGmailAccount/gmailAccount in src/observers/gmail/account.ts can be reused by the receipt observer. Hard Rules 2, 6, 10.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] Rows: one raw row per recipient, so the counterparty is part of the contentHash. counterparty is the address exactly as read (case kept), through normalize.email. subjectHint is oneLine of the subject anchor's text; it is omitted for an empty subject or a selectors.json text.noSubject placeholder ('(no subject)'), and clamped to 80 code points in the extractor as well as in project(). rowKey is data-legacy-last-message-id. The snippet and the screen-reader summary are never read. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] Bench: bench/observers/gmail.ts runGmailBench() runs the real pass (planPass with DEFAULT_POLICY) over every labelled Gmail fixture. Field accuracy pairs each labelled observation with a produced one of the same rowKeyHash, by recipient first, then in order; each pair scores 3 exact-string fields, and an unpaired label or an invented row scores 3 misses. Gates: gmailSentAccuracyGatePassed (≥ 0.95), Health, Negatives, Coverage, Dedup, SubjectHint, Seeds. gmailSentRealFixtures reports 0 so the number is never mistaken for a live-page figure. Proposed registration name 'gmail-sent'. Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] No new dependency. The bench reaches happy-dom (already an extension devDependency) through extension/tests/unit/observers/gmail/sent-fixtures.ts, because the root has no happy-dom. bench/observers/gmail.ts carries `/// <reference lib="dom" />` so the root tsc can typecheck the DOM-typed observer sources it imports. Fixtures are parsed in an inert happy-dom window (no JS, no file loading, no navigation, fetch answered 451) opened at the fixture's liveUrl, so doc.URL carries the route. Hard Rules 1, 9.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] Selectors anchor on role, aria-*, data-*, structure and Gmail's route only. A unit test rewrites every class attribute on the page and gets an identical pass, and a test refuses any class or id selector in selectors.json (sprint_2 note on random class names).
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] Second contradiction guard for the Sent folder: the document title. Gmail titles a list view '<the folder's nav link title> - <account> - Gmail'. If the title starts that way for a folder other than Sent while the route is #sent, the pass is DEGRADED not_sent_folder. This covers the moment after a route change when Gmail may still show the previous view, so an Inbox or Drafts drawn under #sent is never read as sent mail. Like the nav check, it only refuses: a title that names no folder, or names Sent, leaves the route to decide. The title is compared, never used as a selector, a policy or an instruction (adapter_engineer rule 6). Hard Rules 5 and 6.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] selectors.json gains attributes.navTitle and text.titleSeparator, and stays at version 1.0.0. The file has never been committed or recorded in Decisions.md, so 1.0.0 is the first version, and it is paired with tests/fixtures/pages/gmail/2026-10-03. Any change after the commit bumps the version (adapter_engineer rule 3). Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] The strict recipient rule is kept and now pinned by a test. If a recipient element's `email` attribute is present but is not an address, the row is refused (row.recipient_invalid), even when `title` holds an address. Something about the markup has changed, so the observer fails loud rather than guess which attribute is right. Hard Rules 5 and 6.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] Left unpinned on purpose: when the time gridcell itself carries a title or aria-label, the current code reads the cell before its inner span. Which carrier to prefer is speculative until a real capture exists, and the current choice fails safe: day-only rows are refused and the pass goes DEGRADED. 2.8 records the live markup. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] The bench gates are falsifiable. Unit tests show that Health, Negatives, Coverage, Dedup and Seeds each turn false when a wrapped observer breaks the property the gate names, so a gate can never be weakened in silence. The subjectHint gate cannot fail through planPass, because project() clamps to 80. It stays as a measured fact. Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] The receipt observer is `gmail.receipt` (vendor gmail, version = selectors.json version 1.0.0, hosts OBSERVER_HOSTS.gmail). It asserts NO coverage by design: the Observer contract gains `coverage?: 'none'`, and plan.ts gains plan status `OK_NO_COVERAGE` (health OK, rows, never a coverage.ping). Such an observer's account() and visibleWindow() are never called. base.ts maps the status to health OK, and assertObserver refuses any other coverage value. Why: an opened message cannot assert that every registry-sender message in a span was parsed (docs/grading.md §3) — Hard Rule 5
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] Receipt health is neutral where there is nothing to read. A conversation is open when the page address fragment names a thread (`#…/<16 hex | 24+ alnum>`), or role=main holds the subject heading or a message item; on every other view (lists, settings) health is OK. With a conversation open it is DEGRADED `selector.missing.<main|subject|message|sender|date>` when a landmark is missing, and `receipt.amount_missing` or `receipt.amount_ambiguous` when a platform parser recognises a receipt but cannot read its total. A missing role=main is DEGRADED only when the address names a thread — Hard Rule 6
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] Sender lists are domains: an address matches its own domain or any subdomain of it, after ASCII lowercasing (stripe.com, amazon.com, facebookmail.com, opentable.com, instacart.com, uber.com, shopifyemail.com). A confusable address (non-ASCII or xn--) gets no parser at all. Mail from the signed-in account itself gets no parser. A registry-domain sender is read only by its own platform parser, never by the generic one. The display name never chooses a parser — Hard Rule 5
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] A platform message is a receipt only when its subject matches one of the platform's receipt kinds in selectors.json (and not that kind's `exclude`). Its other mail (shipping notices, promotions, unpaid invoices, plain reservations, refunds) is not a receipt and is ignored silently. Kinds: Stripe receipt → purchase.made; Stripe subscription updated → subscription.changed (amount required); Stripe subscription cancelled → subscription.changed (amount optional); Amazon 'Your Amazon.com order'/'Ordered:' → purchase.made; Marketplace 'Your listing is live: <item>' → listing.posted; OpenTable prepaid/receipt → purchase.made; Instacart receipt; Uber trip; Shopify order confirmed — Hard Rule 5
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] The amount is the total line. A table row whose first cell is a total label, matched whole after collapsing white space, ASCII lowercasing and dropping a trailing colon; or a one-cell line '<label>[:] <amount>'. The value must be exactly one amount read by normalize.amount. Labels are tried in each kind's order of preference ('Amount paid' before 'Total'). If lines with the chosen label disagree, the total is ambiguous and is not guessed. The Marketplace price is the row labelled with the item the subject names, so the $5.00 boost upsell is never the price — Hard Rule 5
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] A bare `$` means USD, set per parser (`defaultDollar` in selectors.json). Only US storefront domains are in the registry: amazon.ca and amazon.co.uk receipts are not parsed rather than read in a guessed currency (the generic parser refuses them as impersonating a registry merchant). Known limit: a generic receipt from a non-US shop that writes a bare `$` is read as USD — Hard Rule 5
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] Generic rule, tightened. All of these must hold: (1) trigger (the sprint rule) — the sender address contains 'order', or the subject matches order/receipt/confirmation; (2) the subject has no refusal marker — reply or forward, promotion (% off, sale, order now, ends tonight, free shipping…), cart, shipped or delivered, refund, return, cancellation, due, statement, quote, estimate; (3) a total line with an unambiguous amount under one of Amount paid / Amount charged / Total charged / Total paid / Order total / Grand total / Total; (4) neither the sender's display name nor its domain wears a registry merchant token (amazon, uber, instacart, opentable, stripe, shopify, facebook). The labelled promo negative passes the sprint rule but fails both (2) and (3). The generic parser never makes a pass DEGRADED — Hard Rule 5
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] extractor.confidence: Observation has no confidence field. The generic parser's 0.6 stays a declared prior on the parser result (ReceiptFacts.confidence): unit tests check it against the labels and the bench can report it, but it is never sent, stored or graded. Platform parsers declare none rather than an unmeasured number — Hard Rules 2, 4, 12
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] occurredAt is the message date: the date cell's title, else its alt, else its visible text without the '(… ago)' suffix. It is resolved by normalize.resolveRelative against the pass instant in the browser zone; body dates are never used. The date cell is the header grid cell that is not the sender's, preferring one with a title, so a date shown only as text is still read. A date that does not name one minute (a day only, unreadable, or a clock time made ambiguous by a DST fall-back) throws ReceiptError('time_unresolved') from extract(), so the pass is DEGRADED (`extract.threw`) with no rows — Hard Rules 5, 6
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] The body is read lazily, and only for messages whose sender and subject already make them a receipt candidate. Lines are the innermost blocks outside the message header (header = elements carrying [email], role=gridcell or role=button). Only Money leaves the parser. Merchant is the canonical name (Amazon, Uber, Instacart, Facebook Marketplace), the sender display name (Stripe, Shopify, generic) or the subject's `merchant` group (OpenTable) — never body text — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] Only expanded messages are read (aria-expanded is not 'false'). Each gets its own observation, keyed by its data-legacy-message-id, so two receipts threaded together stay two — Hard Rule 2 §10 dedup
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] selectors.json is checked with strict Zod when it loads. An unknown key, a regex that does not compile, a non-ISO currency, two platforms claiming one domain, a subject-merchant kind without a `merchant` group, or a kind with no labels and no `item` group throws ReceiptError('invalid_selectors') before any page is read — Hard Rule 6
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] Sanitiser (tests/tools/sanitize.ts `keepSenders`, default: the registry): addresses at registry domains or their subdomains are kept without their +tag. Display names are kept for registry domains except facebookmail.com, which relays people's mail. Lookalike (non-ASCII) addresses are never kept. findForeignAts accepts the kept domains. A token a kept business name shares with a learned person's name stays replaceable, and founder --names always win — Hard Rules 2, 10
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] pickSeeds skips any seed whose value holds a URL query or a parameter fragment (url_with_token): the scrubber strips such text from pages, and SECURITY-2 forbids a token query in a committed fixture — Hard Rules 2, 10
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] Known limit, already present before this change: the sanitiser does not recognise an email address written in non-Latin script (e.g. a Cyrillic lookalike domain). Only its '@' is neutralised, and a display name beside it is not learned. My change only guarantees such an address is never kept as a registry sender — Hard Rule 2
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] Fixture labels: the 16 receipt fixtures (8 platform, 4 generic positives, 4 negatives) record selectorsVersion 'gmail.receipt@1.0.0' (observer id prefixed, because these pages also carry gmail.sent health). The promo-order note now explains how the tightened rule refuses it — Hard Rule 12
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] bench/observers/receipts.ts runs in Node via tsx with happy-dom, which is already an extension devDependency (resolved through the shared test helper extension/tests/unit/observers/receipts/receipt-fixtures.ts). No dependency added. `/// <reference lib="dom" />` types the extension code it runs, as the sibling gmail and gcal benches do — Hard Rule 12
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] Body lines: a table row with no nested row and at least two cells that hold text is one line (its cells), whatever wraps the text (<p>, <div>, <strong>). Empty spacer cells are left out. A cell's runs join with one space. Every innermost block, a row's own wrappers included, is still read as runs, so the reader sees a superset of the old lines. A <br> at any depth or a nested block edge ends a run. Only text nodes are mail text: comments, <style>, <script>, <template> and <noscript> are not. Why: real receipt templates (Shopify's default order confirmation) wrap cells, and a reader that misses them makes every real receipt DEGRADED or lost (Hard Rules 5, 6).
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] A message is read under its own conversation's subject: the last h2[data-legacy-thread-id] before it, within its nearest ancestor (up to role=main) that holds one. Why: Gmail can keep an earlier conversation's view in the page, and a stale subject turned an unpaid invoice into a purchase (Hard Rule 5). Stale views themselves are still read, because they can only be told apart by class-based styles, which the observer may not anchor on.
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] The address names a thread only as '#<view>/<id>' or '#(label|search|category|advanced-search)/<one segment>/<id>'. A bare label or search is a list, however long its name. Why: no false DEGRADED on list views (Hard Rule 6). Assumption to verify at 2.8: Gmail percent-encodes '/' in nested label names.
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] The generic parser's subject refusals are 6 families in selectors.json (each compiled alone to validate, then joined): reply or forward; marketing; cart reminders and orders still to confirm; payment failed or waiting, bills and invoices; shipping and pickup; refunds, returns, cancellations, updates and review requests. Precision over recall: an update, an updated receipt or an invoice is refused, even if paid. Why: a failed or abandoned order with a Total line must never verify a purchase claim (Hard Rule 5).
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] Amazon's purchase kind also excludes delay(ed), update(d), declined, problem, issue, action required, unable, cannot and could not. Why: such notices repeat the Order Total and would add a second purchase.made for one order (Hard Rule 5).
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] A total line may stick the label to its amount ('Total$23.84', from inline spans spaced by CSS). The remainder after the label must still be exactly one amount, so 'Totally', 'Totals:' and 'Total savings' are never totals. Why: real flex-layout templates, and the amount parser already enforces the whole-label rule (Hard Rule 5).
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] gmail.receipt selectors.json version 1.0.0 → 1.0.1 (threadUrl, generic.refuseSubject as a list, Amazon exclude). The receipt labels in tests/fixtures/pages/gmail/2026-10-03 record 'gmail.receipt@1.0.1'. Rule 3: record version and fixture folder together in Decisions.md.
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] The sanitiser keeps only the domain of a registry address. Its local part is faked deterministically (Faker.email's local part at the original domain), the same for every copy. A display name is kept only for a business sender at a keepSenderName domain: a +tag machine address (Stripe and Shopify, whose display name is the merchant) or a name holding a registry brand token (RECEIPT_BRAND_TOKENS = generic.impersonationTokens; 'Amazon.com', 'Uber Receipts', which receipt subjects also name). Why: registry domains are also where people work, and relay addresses carry tokens. A person's name or local part, the signed-in account's included, must never reach a committed fixture (Hard Rules 2, 10). Known residue: a person whose display name holds a brand token is kept, so the founder's §4 review is still needed.
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] The generic Done-when ('≥ 3 negative fixtures') counts only negatives the generic parser itself reads. A labelled negative from a registry sender would test a platform parser instead.
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] No dependency was added. Probes, mutants and bench checks ran from the scratch directory with the extension's existing tsx and happy-dom.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] First snapshot of a range: diffSnapshots(null, s) reports every visible event as a first sighting. A first sighting is a calendar.created row of the event as it stands, with observedAt set to the pass instant. The pass also emits first sightings for events an earlier snapshot could not have seen: a calendar just switched on, or a day that was hidden behind 'N more'. These are kept apart from `changes`, so the labelled diffs stay changes-only. Why: docs/grading.md §4.1 verifies a calendar.created claim only with an event first seen at or after the claim, so an event already on the calendar needs its own first sighting. A sighting and a later created row of the same event state share one contentHash, and the ledger keeps the earliest. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] One observation per event, never one per guest. Its counterparty is the event's first guest in ASCII-lowercase order, with the signed-in account's own address left out, or none. A calendar.updated that added guests names the first guest it added. So one created event is one calendar.created row and at most one UNASKED receipt. This supersedes Decisions [2.1/2.4] 'per guest on a Calendar event' for Calendar only. Hard Rules 7, 5 (the matching limit is in contractIssues).
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] Calendar coverage windows run from the first instant (local midnight) of the first whole visible day to local midnight after the last. A day that hides events behind 'N more' is left out, which splits the range into several windows. A window may end in the future; only gcal.event may. The 2.0 contract's single visibleWindow reports the longest whole run (the earliest of equal runs); visibleWindows() returns every run. A range longer than 42 days (year view: days but no events) is DEGRADED range.too_long. allCalendars is true only when every calendar in the side panel list is checked. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] Rows use rowKey = SHA-256 of data-eventid (core hashRowKey); the snapshot event key is the same value. Calendar names are stored only as SHA-256. Neither the snapshot nor any row holds the vendor id, because data-eventid is base64 of '<event id> <calendar id>' and a primary calendar's id is the owner's address, which may leave the page only as an account hash. A deleted row can still be built from a stored snapshot. Labels keep data-eventid; tests and the bench project a label with hashRowKey(rowKey). Hard Rules 2, 10.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] Times: chip labels carry absolute dates, so nothing is resolved against the read instant. A wall time becomes an instant through the browser's UTC-offset function (core wallToInstant). The page's own 'GMT±hh[:mm]' hint, read from a leaf element in role=main outside the chips, must equal the browser's offset at noon UTC of at least one visible day; otherwise the pass is DEGRADED time_zone.mismatch. Calendar's DST rules are not on the page, so a fixed offset would be a guess. The month view shows no hint and is read in the browser zone. A start or end that a DST change skips or repeats is DEGRADED event.time_ambiguous. A day whose midnight is skipped starts at the first instant after the gap. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] Chip label parsing (English, words in selectors.json): - Time forms: 'h[:mm]am to h[:mm]pm, …, Month D, YYYY'; 'Month D, YYYY at … to Month D, YYYY at …'; 'All day, …, Month D, YYYY'. 24-hour clocks are also read. An end at or before the start in the clock-only form falls on the next day. - Title: the longest line drawn in the chip's aria-hidden part that the label names right after the time and right before a calendar listed in the side panel, so a title with commas is read whole. Chips with no drawn part fall back to the first listed-calendar boundary. - Guests: addresses after the last ', Guests: '. - Response status, location and conference text are never read into a field. - Any other shape (another language, a relative 'Yesterday' label) is unreadable. Agent rule 6; Hard Rules 2, 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] Health is all-or-nothing. One unreadable chip (or chips of one event that disagree, or a chip in no visible day), a chip on a calendar the list says is off, or more than 500 events makes the whole pass DEGRADED: no rows and no coverage. Reason codes are a closed list: selector.missing.main, selector.missing.grid, selector.missing.calendar_list, range.invalid, range.too_long, time_zone.mismatch, event.unreadable, event.calendar_hidden, event.time_ambiguous, event.too_many. Why: a refused chip is not evidence of absence. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] The calendar list is a required landmark. Without it, a calendar switched off cannot be told from a deleted event, so its absence is DEGRADED selector.missing.calendar_list. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] Diff rules: - calendar.created needs a new key on a calendar previous showed and on days previous saw whole. - calendar.deleted needs a missing key on a calendar next shows and on days next sees whole. An event drawn over two days needs both days. - calendar.updated fires when start or end instant, all-day flag, subject hint, calendar or guest set changes. Instants are compared, not strings, and guests are ASCII case folded. - A previous snapshot read under another selectors version is not compared: everything is a first sighting. - Snapshots of two ranges throw GcalError('range_mismatch'), a store bug that the pass reports loud as extract.threw. - A stored snapshot that fails its schema counts as none, so it can only turn changes into sightings. - Rows are ordered by start instant, then key, then type. Hard Rules 4, 5, 6.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] Snapshot schema (CalendarSnapshot, strict Zod): - Top level: format 1, observer 'gcal.event', version (selectors), range '<first>/<last>', readAt, baselineAt, days[{date, complete}] (1–42), calendars[{key: sha256(name), shown}]. - events[{key, calendar, subjectHint ≤80 one-line?, occurredAt, endAt?, allDay, days, guests ≤200 sorted, guestCount}] (≤500). - Refinements: range matches the days; days are in order; event keys are unique; event days are inside the range; event calendars are shown; end is not before start; baselineAt is not after readAt. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] baselineAt: each snapshot carries the readAt of the first snapshot of the chain it continues (same range and selectors version), or its own readAt when it starts a chain. This gives 2.7 and the core the data for a 'changes followed since' coverage rule. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] The observer is a factory, createGcalObserver({offsetAt, previous}). health(doc) has no ExtractContext, so its zone check needs the offset function injected; extract and the windows use ctx.offsetAt. previous(range, accountHash) returns the stored snapshot (the hash, never the address). extract stays pure given its inputs and stores nothing. Observer id = 'gcal.event', the same as its source, so a coverage ping maps to its source without a lookup. Hard Rules 3, 4.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] A '(No title)' event gives no subjectHint, as Gmail's '(no subject)' does. A title that is not one clean line (a control character) makes the event unreadable. Hard Rules 2, 6.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] Bench gate gcalAccuracyGatePassed requires: - event-field accuracy ≥ 0.95 (start, allDay, subject, guests, calendar), the Gmail Sent bar from 2.2; - health, coverage windows and allCalendars exactly as labelled; - every labelled diff reproduced exactly with zero spurious rows. The result reports how many fixtures are synthetic (8 of 8). Fixtures are parsed with happy-dom through the test helper, which resolves the extension devDependency, so no dependency was added. The bench file carries /// <reference lib="dom" /> for the root typecheck. Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5] Labels: set selectorsVersion to 'gcal.event@1.0.0' in all 8 gcal entries, and rewrote the synthetic-gcal-week-two-calendars notes. They said 'zero observations', which contradicts the first-sighting decision; the diff fact still has no change. No expected fact changed. Selectors version 1.0.0 goes with fixture folder gcal/2026-10-03 (synthetic only; agent rule 3). Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5-verify] The observer reads only what is shown (Hard Rule 5): - the one shown role=main — two shown is DEGRADED selector.ambiguous.main, a hidden one is never read; - the shown grids in it, with every grid's days read together, so a week split into all-day and timed grids is one range and a year view (one grid per month) is range.too_long; - shown day cells and shown chips only. Why: single-page apps keep earlier views hidden in the DOM; the Gmail observer already follows the same rule. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5-verify] An 'N more' control is any button or link, by role or native element, outside a chip, whose label or text matches ^\+?\d+ more\b. Such a control in no day makes no day whole. Why: read broadly on purpose — a false 'hides events' only narrows coverage, while a missed one reports a day as seen that was not. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5-verify] Every distinct GMT hint on the page must equal the browser's offset on a visible day. No match is time_zone.mismatch; a partial match is the new time_zone.ambiguous. Why: chip labels are written in Calendar's primary zone, and with a secondary zone shown the page does not mark which hint is primary. A U+2212 minus is accepted. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5-verify] Diff rules: calendar.created needs a previous snapshot that saw every day of the range whole (otherwise the row is a first sighting); calendar.deleted needs a next snapshot that sees every day whole. Why: a missing event may have moved onto a hidden day, so its absence proves nothing. Cost: month views with any 'N more' day never report deletions, which feeds the changesSince contract issue. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5-verify] A calendar name the list gives more than once is stored as `ambiguous: true` (an optional SnapshotCalendar field; no snapshot has been stored yet, since that is 2.7). Created and deleted are never reported on it; updates are, by event key. Why: a chip names its calendar only by name. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5-verify] A page with no readable account is compared with nothing. previous() is never called and every event is a first sighting. 2.7 must never store a snapshot without an account hash. Why: otherwise two accounts' same week would be diffed against each other. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5-verify] A label whose start day is after every day its chips sit in is event.unreadable. An event that began earlier and continues into the range is still read. Why: a date taken from the wrong part of the label must never be stored as fact. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5-verify] Selectors 1.1.0 (was 1.0.0): dayButton and more widened, zone accepts U+2212. Fixture folder tests/fixtures/pages/gcal/2026-10-03 is synthetic only; labels selectorsVersion is 'gcal.event@1.1.0' in all 8 entries. Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5-verify] The location ', Guests:' injection was not mitigated. Every rule I considered either broke the documented guest-list reading (names listed among addresses; the last list wins) or needed DOM structure the synthetic fixtures do not have. It is recorded as a residual for a real capture and the red team.
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] [2.5-verify] gcal/shown.ts duplicates gmail/shown.ts rather than importing it. The Gmail file is another implementer's and is still changing; a shared src/observers/shown.ts is requested.
- 2026-10-03 [2.2] selectors gmail.sent 1.0.0 pairs with the synthetic fixture folder tests/fixtures/pages/gmail/2026-10-03 (adapter_engineer rule 3); real captures pending (2.1 BLOCKED(MANUAL)).
- 2026-10-03 [2.3] selectors gmail.receipt 1.0.1 pairs with tests/fixtures/pages/gmail/2026-10-03 (synthetic).
- 2026-10-03 [2.5] selectors gcal.event 1.1.0 pairs with tests/fixtures/pages/gcal/2026-10-03 (synthetic). SUPERSEDES the [2.1/2.4] line on one observation per guest for Calendar: Calendar is one observation per event, naming its first guest in ASCII-lowercase order (the account left out) or none, and an update names the first guest it added, so one created event is one observation and at most one UNASKED receipt.
- 2026-10-04 [SEC-1] CI's Node 22 resolved to 22.23.3 (ICU 78, Unicode 17.0), which drops U+0295 LATIN LETTER PHARYNGEAL VOICED FRICATIVE from Cased; the founder's machine runs 22.14.0 (Unicode 16.0). The readers decide casing and final sigma from the committed Unicode 15.0 tables, so no grade changes: on 22.23.3 every reader, Unicode property and golden test passes (1,498 tests). The engine-drift canary (tests/unit/core/unicode-tables.spec.ts) now keys its known drift by the engine's own Unicode version (16.0: U+1171E; 17.0: U+1171E and U+0295 Cased) and still fails on an engine version nobody has reviewed. .nvmrc stays '22' so CI keeps testing the newest Node 22 engine, which is how such drift is found. Protects Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] `changesSince` must be at or before `lastSyncAt`. Equal is allowed, because a first look may be stamped with its own instant: `[changesSince, lastSyncAt]` is the span over which a change would have been seen. The field is bound by `coverageHash` through canonical JSON, and `compareObservers` orders entries by it. An entry without it hashes exactly as before v1.9, so existing receipts still replay exactly. Hard Rules 4, 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] `changesSince` is compared on the change-time axis, against when the claimed change could have happened, not against the coverage window. `windowStart` and `windowEnd` stay the event-start axis (the visible days). The change span of a `calendar.updated` or `calendar.deleted` claim is `[start(p,t) − beforeClaimMin, end(p,t) + graceMin]`: from §4.1's first-seen bound to the calendar grace sync. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] An entry that did not follow changes over the whole change span is capped (counts as partial at most), not made ineligible. So a deletion row that a view saw just after it began comparing still verifies, because `verified` comes before `partial_coverage`. A capped entry still counts for grace, and NOT_FOUND needs one entry that followed the whole span. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] An entry with `changesSince` at or before the span start but `lastSyncAt` before the span end is also capped. Otherwise one chain that ended before the change and another that began after it could be pieced together into full coverage and a false NOT_FOUND. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] New reason code `coverage.changes_unfollowed`, with params `{source}`. It is a coverage cap, placed after `coverage.calendars_hidden` in §15 table order, and like the other caps it is emitted only on `partial_coverage`. UI text: 'Attest was not following changes in Google Calendar for the whole time this change could have happened.' Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] The followed-change cap is applied per claim in `coverageFrom`, with the span as a new third argument. It is never applied in eligibility, so the batch's shared eligibility memo (`eligibilityKey`) stays valid and the perf gate is unaffected. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] The followed-change orphan rule applies to rows from the source `gcal.event` (snapshot-diffed rows), not to `calendar.*` types as such. Under the shipped policy these are the same rows. A calendar-typed row from another source is not a snapshot difference, and an email row read by the calendar observer under a custom policy is one. Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] Orphan rule part (a) requires a covering entry with `changesSince < firstSeenAt ≤ lastSyncAt`. An entry last synced before the sighting says nothing about it, so a chain restarted at the sighting is not overridden by older entries from the previous chain. Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] Orphan rule part (b) is the stand-in for 'its own account', because observations carry no account or observer id. No covering entry may have `changesSince` equal to the first sighting (a first look then), and no covering entry without `changesSince` may have been synced at or after the first sighting. Entries without `changesSince` are read by their latest sync because the ledger merges them. The cost is a possibly missed UNASKED, never a false one. Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] The orphan rule is decided on each `contentHash`'s representative (its first sighting), never on a later re-sighting. `coveredObservations` dedupes first, then filters; replay applies the same predicate to the stored row of that id. Hard Rules 4, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] Receipt point intervals needed no core change. Merging, coverage, orphans and hashing already treat a point as a closed interval. The new unit tests and goldens 99 and 100 pin this behaviour. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] The contract now says that the `gcal.event` coverage a batch reads must be closed under overlap: with any `gcal.event` entry, every other one synced by evaluatedAt whose window overlaps it. Otherwise a second account's first look could be missing from the batch and its rows would accuse an agent. Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] The contract's merge rule: `gcal.event` entries merge only when their `changesSince` is equal, or both have none. This is the grouping the ledger's coverage store already uses (absent counts as a group value). Hard Rules 4, 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] Golden 86 gained `changesSince 13:00` on both its passes, so its rows (first seen 14:05 and 14:50) remain followed changes and its expectation is unchanged. Without it they would be first-look rows and never orphans.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] Golden files from 100 on have three-digit numbers: two digits ran out at 99. `SCENARIO_FILE` accepts 2–3 digits, `scenarioFiles()` sorts by number, and the unique-number check compares numbers. Golden 99 patches `receiptMerchants` to `['stripe', 'amazon']` so that only the points decide its coverage.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] The v1.9 rules got contract tags with proofs (`v19-rules.ts`), each shown to fail on a broken copy of its own file, following the v1.2–v1.4 pattern. They are registered in the 'every contract tag is proved' check.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] The §16.15 table does not link goldens 99–103 yet. `tests/unit/docs/worked-examples-check.ts` only recognises two-digit golden file names; the needed regex change is in outsideOwnershipNeeds.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] Property tests: the shared orphan oracle moved to `tests/property/orphan-oracle.ts` (keeping `candidates-shared` under 400 lines). A dedicated calendar property file asserts tallies for followed, unfollowed and first-look cases, and for full or capped change-claim coverage.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] coverageHash is now pinned by two values. One is the v1.8 hash of entries without changesSince, computed with the core of commit 193d7da, so receipts stored before v1.9 still replay exactly. The other is a v1.9 hash for entries that differ only in changesSince. Any change to how coverage is hashed now has to be a reviewed change. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] The contract (§3) now requires that a Calendar pass's rows be stored with its coverage, all or none. Rows carry no account and no pass, so a row kept from a refused pass, sitting inside a span another view followed, would be graded as a change Attest saw happen. The §7 promise that a first look never accuses an agent rests on this. For gcal.event only, it narrows the Sprint 1 carry-forward that each observation of a message is upserted on its own. Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] The false NOT_FOUND for calendar.created claims from a stale view stays in the contract for now. A full fix needs a per-entry sync bound for created claims and a ledger merge that never moves a no-changesSince Calendar entry's lastSyncAt over days it did not see again. That changes created-claim grading, which is outside this wave's interfaces. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] Kept the implementer's choice to cap an entry that did not follow changes (partial at most) rather than make it ineligible. Ineligible would let no_coverage, which comes before verified, block a deletion row Attest actually saw. A capped entry can never give full coverage, so it can never produce NOT_FOUND. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] Kept the orphan rule keyed on the gcal.event source rather than on calendar.* types. The two are the same set under the shipped policy and observers. The rule is stricter than interface 7's literal text: it also requires the first sighting to be at or before the entry's lastSyncAt, and it adds rule (b). Both changes can only cause a missed UNASKED, never a false one. Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] An observer declares exactly one window reader: `visibleWindow` or `visibleWindows`. `assertObserver` refuses both or neither, and refuses `allCalendars` or `snapshot` on any source but gcal.event. The `coverage: 'none'` option is removed because no observer uses it now that receipts post point windows. Why: one unambiguous way to say what a pass covers. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] An empty `visibleWindows` list is a deliberate no-coverage pass: plan `OK_NO_COVERAGE`, health OK, rows posted, no ping. A null list is DEGRADED `window.unknown`. Why: a fine page with nothing to vouch for must not raise a DEGRADED warning, while a reader that cannot say what it saw stays loud. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] A Calendar page still drawing is health OK with no rows, no coverage and no snapshot. No DEGRADED 'loading' reason was added. Busy means `aria-busy="true"` on the main region, on a shown grid in it, or on an ancestor of either, or a shown role=progressbar inside the main region; a progress bar elsewhere (the side panel) does not count. The attribute change that ends the drawing triggers the next pass. Why: a half-drawn week must never read as deletions, and every week navigation must not raise a warning. Best knowledge until the first live capture (2.8). Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] The Calendar selectors version is bumped from 1.1.0 to 1.2.0 for the new `busy` and `progress` selectors, against fixture folder tests/fixtures/pages/gcal/2026-10-03 (agents/adapter_engineer.md rule 3). Snapshots read under 1.1.0 are no longer diffed, so the first pass after the upgrade gives first sightings only. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] A Calendar page where every visible day hides events behind its own "N more" is OK with no coverage, and its snapshot is still posted so the stored chain records the hidden days. A "more" control outside every day stays DEGRADED `window.unknown`, as in 2.5, because the layout is unknown. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] `allCalendars` is read once per pass that has a window and sent as an explicit true or false on every gcal.event ping. A null read is DEGRADED `calendars.unknown`, a non-boolean `calendars.invalid`, a throw `calendars.threw`. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] The snapshot is read in the same synchronous read of the page as the rows and windows. A throw makes the pass DEGRADED `snapshot.threw`, with no coverage and no snapshot. The snapshot is posted after every ping, only when the account hash is known, and also on a clean pass with no windows. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] The account is read only when the pass has a window or a snapshot to post. Gmail Sent page 2, a list view and a busy Calendar page therefore never become DEGRADED because a half-drawn account switcher was read. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] Receipts post one point window per parsed message, deduplicated by instant (two receipts in the same minute give one ping). The account is the switcher's address from the receipt page reader; with no switcher the ping carries no account hash, which grading caps. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] When a row is refused, the pass still reads its windows so the health report names every reason (e.g. `redact.missing_occurred_at` plus `window.unknown`, as labelled), but it asserts nothing. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] The head of the Sent list is unchanged from 2.2: the `#sent` route is page 1, and a shown enabled Newer control overrides it; an absent control leaves the route to decide. The head is safe because a row holding more than one message is refused (sent-time.ts `holdsOneMessage`), which voids the pass. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] Receipts bench: the metric `coverageAsserted` (which had to be 0) is replaced by `coveragePages`, `coveragePagesExact` and `coverageWindows`, and the gate becomes "every page asserts exactly its labelled coverage". Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] Labels: page 2's gmail.sent coverage fact is removed, and each of the 12 positive receipt fixtures gains a gmail.receipt coverage fact at its occurredAt. Each change is explained in that fixture's notes, and the README coverage rules are updated. Hard Rules 5, 12.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] The skeleton's default `post` stays src/bus `send`, which the unit tests and the E2E harness use. Production content scripts must pass `postAcked`; the skeleton header now says so. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] I put the busy-page DEGRADED cases in their own describe block in gcal/busy.spec.ts instead of growing the existing one, to keep each test function at 60 lines or fewer (lint).
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] I left the head-of-Sent boundary-minute tie as a residual issue instead of fixing it. It predates C2 (2.2), its risk is very low (about 50 sends in one minute with no pass in between), and the fix changes an assertion in a non-owned spec (fixture-labels.spec.ts). Hard Rule 5 is protected in practice by the 2-minute beforeClaimMin.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] I did not add a 'collapsed calendar section' guard to allCalendars. Without a real capture the selector would be a guess (agents/adapter_engineer.md rule 1: fixtures first), so it is flagged for 2.8.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-observers] I ran all mutation testing through a Vite load() hook that serves a mutated copy from scratch, so no file in the tree was ever mutated while other implementers worked in it.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] On a Calendar page whose account is known, the content script posts no rows of its own. It posts its health, coverage pings and snapshot, and the service worker derives every Calendar row from the step between its stored snapshot and the new one. Otherwise a first look in a new tab would post each event as a first sighting, and an event edited while no Calendar tab was open would come back as a calendar.created of its new state. That row would be false evidence for a 'created' claim and a false orphan. A page with no account still posts the observer's own first sightings, and its coverage carries no changesSince. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] The calendar.snapshot message carries an optional accountHash: the hash, never the address, of the account the same pass read. The Calendar content script takes it from the pass's own read in extract. Snapshots are stored per account, and a pass may have no coverage ping to name the account (a month whose every day hides events). A snapshot without it is skipped, which matches the rule never to compare pages with no account. Hard Rules 2, 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] A Calendar coverage ping from a pass with a known account is held in the service worker, in memory, per sending tab, frame and document. It is recorded only after that pass's snapshot rows are stored, so coverage never comes before its rows. If the worker restarts, the snapshot never arrives, or CALENDAR_PASS_HOLD_MS (5 min) passes, that pass records no coverage. Every loss errs towards less coverage. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] A Calendar snapshot can vouch for an absence (it is 'complete') only when every day of its range was seen whole and no calendar name is listed twice. Changes are followed (baselineAt, then the coverage's changesSince) from the first snapshot of an unbroken run of complete snapshots of the same range, account, selectors version and shown calendars. A first look that is complete starts a run with changesSince = its own readAt. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] How a Calendar pass's windows are recorded: with changesSince when the pass is complete and no absence awaits confirmation; without changesSince when the pass is not complete (it covers what was there, not what changed); not at all when the pass is complete but awaits confirming an absence. While a deletion is unreported, no batch may read 'every change was seen'. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] A calendar.deleted is reported only after two consecutive complete snapshots lack the event. The first absence is kept in the stored record (pending, with the event kept in the base), and the second confirms it. The confirmed row is reported as first seen at the first absence, the closer instant for the attribution anchor. The event coming back clears it. A snapshot that cannot vouch carries the base's missing events forward on the calendars it shows, keeping any pending absence. Creations and updates come from the observer's diff as it decides them, since a created change and a first sighting are the same calendar.created row. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] A Calendar snapshot read at or before the stored one for its range (another tab's pass that landed late) is skipped. It is answered { ok: false, code: 'late_sighting' }, records no coverage, and does not turn the observer DEGRADED, because nothing is wrong with the page. Hard Rule 6 (fail loud, never cry wolf).
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] DEGRADED persistence: the service worker sets DEGRADED with reasons ledger.<code>. That report stays until a later pass of the same observer has one of its messages fully stored with no refusal in that pass. The observer's own report of that pass then replaces it. An OK report from the observer meanwhile is not stored (it is remembered in memory for the clear). A DEGRADED report from the observer is stored with the ledger reasons kept beside its own. A pass that sends nothing but its health clears nothing. Losing the memory on a worker restart only keeps the DEGRADED one pass longer. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] Every observer message is acknowledged, adapter.health included, with BusAck { ok: true } or { ok: false, code }. The codes are a closed list: invalid_input, id_conflict, late_sighting, refused, failed. A message with several refusals reports the worst, in the order invalid_input, id_conflict, late_sighting. A handler that throws (a PolicyError for a copied policy included) is logged at error as bus.handler_failed and answered failed, never caught as DEGRADED. Hard Rules 4, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] The service worker accepts observer messages only from the observers in src/config.ts OBSERVER_SOURCES (gmail.sent, gmail.receipt, gcal.event), each reading exactly its own source. A row or ping of another source, or from another observer id, is refused invalid_input before the ledger, and an unknown id gets no health row. A Gmail ping whose window ends after its sync, or a receipt ping wider than one instant, is refused invalid_input. Hard Rules 2, 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] coverageIntervals and calendarSnapshots are ledger tables like the others (LEDGER_TABLE_NAMES, INDEX_FIELDS, ROW_ID, the bus LedgerTable, the export, the logger's table names). The export carries both, and an export without them (from before 2.7) still parses with both empty, so attest replay keeps reading old exports. Schema v3 adds the Calendar snapshot store, keyed [observerId+accountHash+range] with a readMs index. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] changesSince is part of a coverage interval's merge group, so intervals merge only with the same changesSince or with none. coverageFor returns gcal.event intervals closed under overlap: with any it returns, every other one synced by evaluatedAt whose window overlaps it, whatever the account, so the orphan rule always sees the first look that keeps a row from accusing an agent. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] Retention purges Calendar snapshot records read before the cutoff. A range not looked at since is compared with nothing next time, so its events become first sightings again, never changes. An interval's changesSince is an instant of looking, not of the window, and is not cut. Hard Rules 2, 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] When Chrome drops the purge alarm across a restart, runtime.onStartup recreates it to fire PURGE_STARTUP_DELAY_MINUTES (1) after start, then daily, so a browser restarted every day still purges. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] Content scripts live in entrypoints/content/<name>.content.ts, inside the read-only lint scope. They are registered through WXT's entrypoints:found hook, because WXT only discovers content scripts at the top of entrypoints/. Their matches come from OBSERVER_HOSTS. WXT's start-up postMessage to the page is turned off (noScriptStartedPostMessage). WXT still dispatches one CustomEvent on document for its re-injection handshake: no input event and no user data. Hard Rules 3, 8.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] The shipped bundle is ASCII-only (minifier codegen.asciiOnly). Chrome refuses to load an extension whose content script contains a Unicode noncharacter, and the minifier wrote the U+FDD0 and U+1FFFE escapes of the core Unicode baseline out as raw characters. built-bundle checks every shipped script is ASCII. Hard Rule 9.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] E2E observer fixtures are served at the vendors' own addresses, plus a ?fixture=<name> query so each fixture has its own request URL, through Playwright routing that never reaches the network. The browser runs in America/New_York, the fixtures' zone. So the shipped content scripts run on them, there is no test build, and the network guard exempts exactly those routed URLs. Hard Rules 1, 8.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] The 5-minute network-intercept session now also opens the Gmail Sent, receipt and Calendar fixtures, and reloads a page every 8 s instead of every 15 s, so each of its nine pages is still reloaded at least twice in the session. Its negative control gets 3 minutes, room for the launcher to replace a hung Chromium start-up on a busy machine. Nothing it asserts was loosened. Hard Rule 1.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] The service worker refuses (invalid_input, DEGRADED) a gmail.sent coverage ping whose windowEnd is not the same instant as its lastSyncAt. Only the head of the Sent list writes coverage, with windowEnd = lastSyncAt (interface 4, grading.md §3). A window that ends earlier is an older page's, which cannot vouch for its span. Protects Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] The service worker refuses (invalid_input, DEGRADED) a Calendar ping whose window starts outside its snapshot's range or reaches into a day the snapshot hid behind 'N more'. The check is on local dates, as the observer writes them, so a DST shift of local midnight cannot trigger it. Such a window would report an unseen day as seen. Protects Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7] I did not patch the baseline-orphan defect in C3's files. A local patch (for example, not storing sightings of a refused pass) covers only one of the four routes, cuts against the 'store each row on its own' carry-forward, and pre-empts a grading-contract decision that belongs to C1. Proposed contract text and the C3 change are in residualIssues. Protects Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] Every distinct pushed commit is tested, in order, stopping at the first failure. Deleted refs are skipped, tags are resolved to their commit, and a commit pushed under several refs is tested once. Why: the remote receives each of them, and testing one twice adds nothing. Hard Rule 6 (fail loud).
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] A commit whose pnpm-lock.yaml differs from node_modules/.pnpm/lock.yaml (the lockfile pnpm installed from) stops the hook with a message to run `pnpm install`, rather than being tested on the wrong dependencies. Why: 'tests passed on commit X' must mean X's own pinned dependencies. This also catches a lockfile that was never committed, which CI's frozen install would reject. Hard Rule 12 (never report an unmeasured number) and Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] extension/.wxt is generated in the worktree with `pnpm exec wxt prepare` (about 1.3 s), not linked. Why: .wxt/tsconfig.json uses relative paths ('..', '../*'), so a link would aim type paths at the working tree's sources, and vitest fails without it ('Failed to load tsconfig .wxt/tsconfig.json', seen in the clone). Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] node_modules (root and extension/) are linked as whole-directory absolute symlinks to the installed copies. Why: pnpm's links inside are relative to their real location, so they resolve unchanged. Vite's temp and cache files land in the installed node_modules, just as on a normal run. The links are removed before the worktree, so nothing can follow them during cleanup.
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] Before any git command, the hook clears GIT_DIR, GIT_WORK_TREE, GIT_INDEX_FILE, GIT_PREFIX, GIT_COMMON_DIR, GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY and GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES, and it checks out with `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null`. Why: an inherited GIT_INDEX_FILE would make the worktree's checkout write the user's index, and a post-checkout hook must not run for a throwaway tree. Requirement: the hook never touches the user's working tree or index.
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] The temporary directory is created with mktemp and then fully resolved with `pwd -P`. Why: macOS's $TMPDIR sits behind /var -> /private/var, and tools that compare paths must see one spelling.
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] The hook command is `bash scripts/pre-push.sh` with no "$@". Why: it does not depend on the file's executable bit, and the script only needs the refs on stdin, not the remote name or URL. By hand it accepts revisions as arguments, or tests HEAD when stdin is a terminal.
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] No new root package script. Why: tests/unit/docs/changelog.spec.ts requires every runnable root script under CHANGELOG [Unreleased], and CHANGELOG is not mine. The hook stays hook-only, and the CLAUDE.md Commands line describes it under `git push`.
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] The verdict line says 'did not pass ... (exit N)' rather than 'FAILED'. Why: pnpm catches Ctrl-C and exits 1, so an interrupted run and a failing run look the same to the hook. The line states only what is known. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] The proof used a scratch clone with a scratch-only commit that set the root `test` script to a 4-target vitest subset, not the full suite plus the perf pass. Why: the wave's concurrency rule forbids the full suite and the perf pass while other implementers run tests. The script under test is the real one, unchanged.
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] The hook refuses a pushed commit that has node_modules, extension/node_modules or extension/.wxt in any form, rather than linking or generating through it. Why: a tracked symlink there makes `ln -s` or `wxt prepare` write into the user's installed modules, and cleanup cannot undo that. `.gitignore`'s `node_modules/` ignores only directories, so a symlinked node_modules is committed by `git add -A`. Both effects were reproduced. Protects the requirement that the hook never touches the user's tree; Hard Rule 6 (fail loud, with the path to remove).
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] The docs and the script header say the hook tests 'the commit each pushed branch or tag points to', not 'exactly the pushed commits'. Testing every commit in the pushed range would take about 5 minutes per commit, and the tip is what the remote branch, and CI, end up on. Why: the founder must not read a push of 15 commits as 15 tested commits. Hard Rule 12 (never report an unmeasured result).
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] The hook's behaviour is pinned by running the real script against a throwaway repository with a fake `pnpm`, not only by its text. The run is hermetic: every GIT_* variable dropped, no global or system git config (so a user's gpgsign or hooksPath cannot change it), and TMPDIR inside the sandbox so a leftover worktree is visible. Why: text pins let every behaviour mutation through, including 'tests nothing and passes'. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-04 [tooling] Shared interfaces 1 to 7 do not apply to C5. The hook only runs the suite on the pushed tips, so none of them were checked for this task.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] check-docs gains rule 7 (scripts/check-docs-allowlist.ts). For each source in policy/default.json `allowlistedFields`, docs/privacy-architecture.md must have exactly one section whose heading holds the word 'allowlist' and names the source as its only code span. In that section, every allowlisted field needs a table row whose first cell is the field as a code span, with a description beside it. A row naming a field the policy does not allowlist fails, and so does a section for a source the policy lacks. Nothing is checked when the policy has no allowlistedFields. Why: a field cannot start being stored without a word on what it holds, and a dropped field cannot stay documented as stored. Hard Rules 2, 12.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] A unit test requires a section in docs/privacy-architecture.md for every ledger table (LEDGER_TABLE_NAMES), naming every field of the strict schema the ledger stores and reads with. That includes the nested snapshot, event, day, calendar, pending, claim-extractor, receipt-reason and override fields, plus every derived index field. Why: the privacy review's 'every table, every field' becomes a test, so a new table or field fails until it is described. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] A unit test requires every reason code a stored health report can carry to appear in a docs/adapters.md reason table, under its own observer's section: PASS_REASONS, SENT_HEALTH_REASONS, the receipt `selector.missing.<landmark>` and amount reasons, GCAL_REASONS and the `ledger.<code>` reasons. Every BusAck code must likewise appear in the acknowledgements table. The receipt landmark list and the row-code list are written out in the test, with a compile-time check that they cover their unions. Why: a DEGRADED report nobody can read is no better than a silent one. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] docs/adapters.md is reorganised with limitations first. The Sprint 0 capture sections become steps of the capture → label → test → version loop (§6), and the bus table moves to §9 with the 2.7 rows (C3's text). Why: agents/docs_writer.md rule 2 puts limitations before features, and task 2.9 asks for the whole loop in one place. Hard Rules 5, 12.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] The founder's `@` check is now `grep -oE '@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+' <fixture>.html | sort -u`. It accepts @example.com, .org and .net plus the receipt-registry sender domains and their subdomains, and the founder reads every display name beside a registry address, because a person whose name holds a brand token is kept. Why: the sanitiser now keeps registry domains on purpose (Decisions [2.3]), and the old check would have failed every real receipt capture. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] docs/privacy-architecture.md states that the calendarSnapshots table holds more than an observation's allowlist (every event in a viewed range, up to 200 guest addresses, end time and all-day flag), and that its own strict schema governs it, not allowlistedFields. It also says hashes of guessable values (addresses, calendar names) can be confirmed by anyone who guesses them, and that the ledger and export are unencrypted. Why: a privacy document must not claim more protection than the code gives. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] Known gaps are written into docs/adapters.md as open items rather than describing the contract as met. One: Calendar rows, the snapshot record and the coverage windows are separate writes, and a refused pass keeps its accepted rows, while grading.md §3 requires all or none. Two: the Gmail Sent head window has a same-minute edge case. Three: the clock-jump and moved-event Calendar limits. Four: the live-smoke checks for aria-busy clearing and collapsed calendar lists. Why: docs_writer rule 2 (limitations first). Hard Rules 5, 12.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] Until the Sprint 4 popup exists, docs/adapters.md §4 explains how to read health: run `chrome.runtime.sendMessage({ type: 'ui.query', query: { kind: 'health.list' } }, …)` in the options page's DevTools console. An extension page may send ui.query; a content script may not. Why: live smoke (2.8) needs a way to read DEGRADED reasons without new UI. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] The check-docs summary line now ends with 'N allowlisted fields checked'. The two expected CLI strings in check-docs.spec.ts are updated to match.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] worked-examples-check.ts reads golden files with 2- or 3-digit numbers. The docs/grading.md §16.15 table links goldens 99–103 exactly as the grading contract specifies: unasked 100 and 101, verified 99 and 103, partial_coverage 102, not_found 103. The closing sentence names 99's receiptMerchants patch. Why: every rule row must cite files that really end on it. Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] The Gmail Sent same-minute known limit now states what really triggers it: the send and the next one in the same minute, then a page's worth of later sends before any pass of the head. The old wording ('about 50 sends inside one minute') made a false NOT FOUND look far less likely than it is for an agent sending a burst of messages. A limitation must not understate its risk. Hard Rules 5, 12.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] The receipt observer's account reader is documented as it is (English 'Google Account:' prefix, first link, no two-account check), with its consequences: no account hash means coverage is capped, and the user's own mail is not excluded. Before, the doc said it reads the account as gmail.sent does. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] privacy-architecture.md now says the calendarSnapshots base can keep an event the latest pass did not show: one that an incomplete snapshot (a day hidden behind 'N more', a calendar named twice) could not vouch was gone. A privacy document must list every stored fact, including the ones carried forward. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] privacy-architecture.md now says chrome.storage.local holds only attest.installId today. attest.flags is described as reserved and not yet written, instead of as stored. Hard Rule 2: never claim to store more or less than the code does.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] privacy-architecture.md §6 now says how each kind of value is hashed: row and event ids as their canonical JSON string, addresses and calendar names as UTF-8 text. Before, it said 'SHA-256 of the id', which someone checking a guess would compute wrongly. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-04 [2.9] Calendar ordering in adapters.md §8.3 follows the code: the account and window check runs before any write and refuses the whole pass. Then rows, the snapshot record and the windows are written separately; the atomicity gap stays documented in §3.6. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] gmail.receipt coverage is a point at the parsed message's date (windowStart = windowEnd): it asserts only that this one message was read. An exact receipt can then verify a purchase claim (coverage partial, never full, so never NOT_FOUND), and a receipt nobody claimed can be UNASKED with the usual attribution. Before this, no receipt pass posted coverage and no purchase claim could ever be more than UNVERIFIABLE. Protects Hard Rule 5 and makes the receipt parsers useful.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-contract] gmail.sent posts coverage only from the head of the Sent list (no refused row on it); later pages post rows and no coverage, OK by design. In conversation view (Gmail's default) a send can be listed on an earlier page under a later reply's date, so a later page's date span does not show every send inside it; a span from it could give a false NOT_FOUND. Protects Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [selectors] gcal.event selectors 1.2.0 pair with tests/fixtures/pages/gcal/2026-10-03 (synthetic); gmail.sent 1.0.0 and gmail.receipt 1.0.1 with tests/fixtures/pages/gmail/2026-10-03 (adapter_engineer rule 3).
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] Decision 2 reuses the existing reason code coverage.changes_unfollowed instead of adding a new one. A view last synced before end(p,t)+graceMin did not follow the calendar for the whole time the claimed change could have happened, which is what that code already says for change claims. Its UI text, params {source} and §15 position (after calendars_hidden) stay as they are, so the closed enum, schema, reason-text and golden vocabularies need no change. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] The stale-view cap is `calendarBound(claim, policy)` = {syncMs: end(p,t)+graceMin, sinceMs: change span start, or null for calendar.created}, and replaces changeSpan as the third argument of coverageFrom. It is applied per claim and per entry, never in the shared eligibility memo. A stale view of one part of a window and a fresh view of the rest are never pieced together. A capped entry still counts for grace, and a row it saw still verifies. Updated and deleted claims grade exactly as in v1.9. Hard Rules 4, 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] In the contract, a first-look marker carries its pass's allCalendars. A marker from a page with no account has no accountHash but still has changesSince = lastSyncAt (a span of one instant, which follows nothing). Change rows get no marker. A marker blocks every row sharing its start instant and first sighting, so a real change seen by the same pass on an event starting at that exact instant is also spared: at worst a missed UNASKED, never a false one, documented as a known limit in §7. Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] Three new ledger obligations are written into §3. First, retention never removes a marker before its row; a marker's lastSyncAt is the row's first sighting, which can be earlier than the row's observedAt. Second, markers are part of the overlap closure a batch reads. Third, a merge never moves a gcal.event entry's lastSyncAt over days the later pass did not see again; without this the stale-view cap would be defeated by merged entries without changesSince. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] For the stored-all-or-none rule, §3 also states the page with no account: its rows message and the rows' markers are stored all or none, and its coverage entries only after them. Gmail passes keep the 2.7 rule: each row on its own, refusals collected, no coverage after any refusal. Hard Rules 5, 6, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] batch-orphans.ts seenWindows files one-instant gcal.event entries by instant (`points`) and keeps longer windows in the reach scan. followedChange reads both through one verdict function (`spanVerdict`), so the outcome is identical; this is proved by the existing oracle property and the new marker property. Each row now pays one lookup for its markers instead of a scan over every marker in range. gradeBatch and replay still decide with the same predicate. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] Goldens 104–108 carry two new proved contract tags, calendar_first_look_marker and calendar_stale_view_capped (v110-rules.ts, registered in v13-rules.spec.ts), plus one edge: tag per case. Besides the static proofs and broken-copy checks, v110-rules.spec.ts grades broken copies: the marker decides 104, 105 and 107; the all-or-none rule decides 106; a fresh resync decides 108.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] Golden 106 models case (iii) as the grader sees it after decision 3. The refused pass leaves nothing, and the next clean first look stores the row first seen at its own instant, with its marker. In that file the marker is not the only protection (the pass's own first-look entry also blocks the row); its graded broken copy shows that the old row-by-row state would have accused an agent.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] Kept the marker's effect on the account count rather than leaving markers out of it. A marker of a second account is real evidence that Attest saw that account, and capping is the cautious direction (Hard Rule 5). The contract and a unit test now say so, instead of the old claim that a marker never lowers coverage.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] Pinned the one-rule-for-every-entry property of the new points path with an oracle property, not unit cases. A point of any shape (marker, followed, or compared nothing) must give the same verdict a wider window holding the row's start would give (§3: a point is a window), so a future change to the lookup cannot drop rule (a) or rule (b) for points without failing.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] Fixed golden 107's allCalendars to match its own story, and left golden 103's extra 14:05 entry alone: it changes no grade. Golden 107 is a v1.10 file; golden 103 predates v1.10, which changes no golden from 01 to 103.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] **First-look marker.** A gcal.event coverage entry whose window is the row's occurredAt (a point) and whose lastSyncAt = changesSince = the row's first sighting, as the observer saw it (equal to sightedAt when the ledger stamps the write later). It carries the observer and account of the pass. It is written by `upsertFirstSighting` (src/ledger/first-look.ts) in the row's transaction, and only when the upsert inserted the row: a re-sighting of a stored row changes nothing, and the orphan rule reads the stored row's first sighting. Markers go to coverageIntervals only, never to the latest-ping table. Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] **allCalendars on a marker.** True only when every calendar in the snapshot is shown and named once. This is never more than the observer's own allCalendars, so the cap reasons stay accurate. It is omitted on a page with no account (unknown, so read as hidden). A point never makes coverage full, so this flag only affects which partial-coverage reasons are listed. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] **Markers are their own merge group, without a schema change.** coverage-merge.ts `isFirstLookMarker` recognises a gcal.event point whose changesSince equals its lastSyncAt (a pass window is a run of whole days, never a point), and `groupKey` includes that flag. So a marker never merges with a pass's window, with or without changesSince, and no window merges with a marker. The v1.9 rule is kept: a window without changesSince never merges with one that has it. Hard Rules 4, 5, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] **Calendar merge containment rule (needed for decision 2).** Two gcal.event intervals merge only when they synced at the same instant, or the earlier-synced window lies inside the later-synced one. The union is then the later window. A union must never carry a later lastSyncAt over days only an earlier pass saw: D1's `calendarBound` cap would otherwise be bypassed, for example an older week merged with a newer neighbouring week gives a false NOT_FOUND. Gmail merges as before. The cost is more Calendar rows, one per distinct window per group, bounded by retention. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] **One transaction per Calendar pass.** `inCalendarPass` opens a readwrite transaction over observations, meta, calendarSnapshots, coverageIntervals and coverage; its scope is always wrapped in an async function, which Dexie needs to keep the transaction across native awaits. The first ledger refusal of a row is converted to `CalendarRowRefused(code)` and aborts the whole pass. The answer carries that first code, not the worst of several (Gmail still collects every refusal and answers the worst). The DEGRADED count is the number of rows the step derived. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] **Page with no account: rows stored together with what blocks a false UNASKED, not held.** gcal.event rows arriving in observer.observations are all first sightings of that pass. They are stored all or none, each with a marker that has no account, in one transaction. A row of another source in the message refuses the whole message. The pass's pings come afterwards and are recorded as before, only if the rows were stored. Holding rows until the pings would need a pass-end signal the no-account page does not send, and markers already make the rows safe. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] **Record read in the future.** If a stored Calendar record's readAt is later than the service worker's clock (ctx.now(), no tolerance: the content script and the worker share one system clock), it is dropped inside the pass's transaction and the pass becomes a first look. This is logged as warn `observer.calendar_restarted` with reason `future_record`. Rows stored while the clock was ahead can still refuse the restarted first look with `late_sighting`. That is loud (DEGRADED), never silent. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] **A refused pass restarts its range's chain.** The record is dropped in its own transaction after the abort, logged with reason `refused`. Without this, a row derived again from the same record by every pass would refuse every later pass. Example: a confirmed deletion first seen at the first absence, which another range's chain stored earlier with a later first sighting. A restart only loses a pending absence and turns changes since the last stored pass into first sightings: a missed UNASKED, never a false one. Hard Rules 5, 6, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] **Row codes moved to src/background/row-codes.ts.** ROW_CODES, RowCode, worstCode and rowCodeOf now live there, so calendar-rows.ts and observer-messages.ts share them without an import cycle. observer-messages re-exports `RowCode` and `worstCode` for existing importers such as tests/unit/docs/adapters-reasons.spec.ts.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] **Test clock.** The handler test clock (tests/unit/background/observer-harness.ts HANDLER_NOW) moved from 12:00 to 14:00, after every pass instant the tests use. A record read after the worker's clock now correctly restarts its chain, and the old 12:00 clock made the at(10)–at(30) records look future-dated.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] A creation or update that a Calendar pass derives is stored as a first sighting, with its first-look marker, when another stored Calendar record holds the event in the same state and was read strictly after this range's base and strictly before this pass. The record can be of any account and any range except this account's own range. The change then happened before that read, so the pass's sighting is not when it was first seen. Converting can only remove orphans (a missed UNASKED at worst), and the view that saw the change first still reports it. Deletions are never converted, since they report an absence. Hard Rule 7 (src/background/calendar-seen.ts).
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] 'Same state' compares exactly the fields the Calendar diff compares: key, start and end as instants, all-day flag, title, calendar, guest count, and the guest list ASCII-folded. A guest list that differs only because another account's view leaves that account out gives no match. For such a case the change stays as before, the conservative direction. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] coverageFor closes gcal.event coverage under overlap by reading the source once and sweeping in memory. The connected runs of closed intervals that overlap or touch are the overlap graph's components, so the result set is exactly the old fixpoint (property-tested). The old way made one query per joined entry, which the markers made quadratic. Hard Rules 4 and 7 unchanged (same set, same sorted output, so coverageHash and replay are unchanged).
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D2] The future-record boundary is pinned at the stored record's own instant: a record read at exactly the worker's clock is not in the future, and the next pass compares with it. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-D1] SUPERSEDES the [2.7-contract] entry that kept the false NOT_FOUND for calendar.created claims from a stale view in the contract: v1.10 caps a gcal.event entry for any calendar claim unless it was synced at or after end + graceMin (calendarBound), so a view looked at before the claim never makes it NOT_FOUND. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-sim] A read-only differential simulator (5,500 seeds; two accounts and a page with no account; overlapping ranges; loading, forged and out-of-order passes; clock jumps) ran the real service-worker handlers, ledger and gradeBatch after wave D.
