The Hard Rule 7 invariant held (no event already on the calendar was ever UNASKED; replay exact). It found 291 true calendar claims graded NOT_FOUND from six causes (CAL-SIM-1..6), which wave E addresses by simplifying the rules rather than patching each case.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] (E-a) A calendar.updated or calendar.deleted claim caps every gcal.event entry over its window with the new closed reason coverage.change_unprovable {source}. It sits in §15 order right after coverage.changes_unfollowed, with UI text: 'Attest reads <source> as snapshots, so it can see a change that was made but cannot show one never was.' A change claim carries only this code from the calendar caps: its stale entries are not also marked changes_unfollowed, which now applies to calendar.created claims only. The cap reads the claim's type, so it is applied per claim and never enters the eligibility a batch shares. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] (E-a) Removed as dead code: conditions.ts changeSpan, the sinceMs part of calendarBound (now {syncMs, change}), and changesMs on EntryInstants and EligibleEntry. changesSince stays in ObserverCoverage, coverageHash and the orphan rule (batch-orphans.ts is unchanged). The coverage-level code moved to a new internal module, conditions-level.ts, re-exported through conditions.ts, to keep conditions-coverage.ts under 400 lines. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] (E-a) What NOT_FOUND means for calendar.created (§6.1): in views of the whole claim window, each synced at or after end(p,t) + graceMin, every calendar shown, of each bound account, no matching event was on the calendar. 'Matching' means at thresholds.partial, not held as evidence by another claim, and not first seen before the claim's first-seen bound. Known limits, documented: an event created and then deleted, moved or renamed past matching before the first fresh view is NOT_FOUND; an event that existed before the claim gives NOT_FOUND (golden 52). Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] (E-b) New closed reason coverage.account_partial {source}, with UI text naming the service. It is emitted only when the union of the bound accounts' uncapped entries contains the window but some bound account's own entries do not, so it marks exactly the cases the per-account rule changed. Where even the union falls short, the receipt carries only the entries' own caps. An account named twice in the grant counts once. Applies to every source. An unbound grant keeps coverage.multiple_accounts. A first-look marker, being a point, never stands for its account's view. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] (E-c) The gate reads every content feature (counterpartyExact, counterpartyDomain, counterpartyName, subjectOverlap, amountExact, merchantMatch; that is, every feature but timeProximity), not just the type's policy.identityFeatures. Reading only the identity list would turn mandatory golden 02 (same domain, partial subject, required to be PARTIAL) and the name-claim partials in goldens 19, 54 and 71 into NOT_FOUND, FINAL: false NOT_FOUNDs on likely-true claims such as 'Raj Mehta' sent to rmehta@. With the content-feature reading, every PARTIAL in the existing goldens is unchanged. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] (E-c) A gated pair's score is exactly 0 (Score 0/1), not a cap. Every reader (assignment, best candidate, bestScoreGte and bestScoreLt, observationIds, confidence, signals.score) therefore sees one value. The receipt's signals still show each feature's own value. The weighted mean is computed before the gate, so RatioError('zero_weight_sum') still fires where the mean is undefined. RowScorer.fraction applies the same gate after the mean. Hard Rules 4 and 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] (E-c) The policy loader refuses timeProximity in identityFeatures for every type, with the message '"timeProximity" says when, never which action'. Every claim that is not underspecified then has an applicable content feature, so the gate never leaves a claim with nothing that could match it. Two policy unit tests that used time as an identity feature were moved to the invalid list. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] (E-c) Goldens updated only where the hand computation changed. In 78 and 85 a time-only best candidate's score is now 0/1 (5/14 and 5/18 before; grades unchanged). Golden 13's notes restate c1 × o2 as 0, which was 5/13. Outside the goldens, the batch-history unit expectation changed from 1/3 to 0/1. Known limit, documented in §5.3: a calendar claim with no guest whose subject the event does not share (a paraphrased title) is NOT_FOUND under fresh, whole views, where v1.10 called it PARTIAL on time alone if the event started within about 14 hours of targetAt.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] (E-e, contract) The attribution window is the narrowest of the §7 windows, min(unasked.windows.sessionMin, idleMin, adjacentMin): 2 minutes in the shipped policy. A change row is stale when its first sighting comes more than that after its base was read, or when the base is unknown. For a confirmed deletion the base is the snapshot its first absence was compared with. This matches E2's calendar-step.ts staleChangeMs. A stale change is stored as a first sighting with its marker: it can still be evidence for a claim but is never an orphan. Known limit: when passes come further apart than that window, the changes found are never UNASKED (a missed UNASKED, never a false one). Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] (E-d, contract) §3 'Clock safety (v1.11)' is written to match E2's src/ledger/clock-guard.ts. A pass, ping or row read after the service worker's clock is refused (clock_behind). The ledger keeps a high-water instant. When the clock is found below it, one transaction deletes every coverage interval or marker synced after now, every Calendar record read after now (its chain restarts, nothing carried across), and every Calendar row first seen after now that no batch has read. A row a batch read stays, and an earlier re-sighting of it is a no-op, so a range is no longer locked out. A clock floor that is never lowered bounds every chain's changesSince. One ledger.clock_regressed log line, counts only. Known limit: a jump that comes back before anything is stored is invisible. Hard Rules 4, 5, 6 and 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] Goldens 109–111 add three contract tags, proved in tests/golden/v111-rules.ts and registered in v13-rules.spec.ts: calendar_change_unprovable, per_account_coverage and no_evidence_on_time_alone. The v1.9 proofs calendar_change_unfollowed and calendar_change_followed now check v1.11 behaviour: change_unprovable, and never NOT_FOUND. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] bench/core.ts adds a first-look marker for every other gcal.event row, up to SCHEMA_LIMITS.GRADING_INPUT, using D1's withMarkers without its console output, so the perf gate also measures the marker lookup. Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E1] Receipts that v1.10 graded differently from v1.11 (calendar change NOT_FOUNDs, cross-account NOT_FOUNDs, time-only PARTIALs) would not replay under v1.11. No grading batch is wired before Sprint 3.6, so no stored receipt is affected; the change goes through the contract version (v1.11), as v1.9 and v1.10 did. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E2] **Write instant (E-d).** It is the service worker's clock read for the message (`ctx.now()`, which is src/clock.ts nowIso in production): the same wall clock the content scripts and the ledger's row stamps read. A message holding anything read after it is not stored (`clock_behind`, a new closed AckCode; warn `observer.clock_behind`; not DEGRADED, since the page is fine and its next pass is read on the clock as it is). Clock checks live in src/background and take the clock as a parameter, never inside ledger primitives. The ledger tests pin the clock before their fixtures by design. Hard Rules 4, 5, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E2] **High water.** Meta `clockHighWater` holds the latest instant stored since the last repair. A new row or claim raises it with its sighting (not with the +1 ms batch floor, which would read as a regression within the same millisecond); `recordCoverage` with `lastSyncAt`; `putCalendarRecord` with `readAt`. Batch instants are not counted: a batch reads, it is not evidence, and the e2e records a batch 2 minutes ahead. It lives in its own module (clock-high-water.ts) so the writers do not depend on the guard. Hard Rules 4, 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E2] **Repair and floor.** A regression is detected when now is below the high water, or below the latest Calendar record's `readAt` (records stored before the high water existed). The repair deletes: - coverage intervals (any source, markers included) and latest pings synced after now; - Calendar records read after now; - `gcal.event` rows first seen after now whose `observedAt` is after the last batch instant (no batch read them, so replay is unaffected). It then sets meta `clockFloorAt` = max(old floor, high water), keeping that instant's own spelling (not re-formatted, so the result does not depend on the time zone), and clears the high water. Claims, Gmail rows and rows a batch read are never deleted. It is logged once, `ledger.clock_regressed` {intervals, pings, records, rows}. The guard runs in its own transaction before the Calendar pass transaction, so a refused pass never rolls back a repair. Hard Rules 4, 5, 6, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E2] **Floor semantics.** No `gcal.event` window is recorded with `changesSince` below the floor. It is raised to the floor when the pass was read at or after it, and dropped (window recorded without `changesSince`) while the pass was read before it. The floor is permanent but only affects chains whose base lies below it. First-look markers are never lifted, because rule (b) needs their `changesSince` equal to the first sighting. When a deleted future marker leaves a batch-read row without a marker, the floor keeps that row from satisfying orphan rule (a). Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E2] **Late-sighting recovery (CAL-SIM-7).** `upsertObservation(db, row, policy, { clockMs })` (and `upsertFirstSighting`) treats an earlier sighting of a stored row whose first sighting is after `clockMs` as a no-op, not `late_sighting`; nothing stored changes. The service worker passes its clock for both Calendar and Gmail rows, so a jump forward no longer locks a Gmail page out either. Without `clockMs` the ledger behaves exactly as before. Hard Rules 4, 6.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E2] **The removed restart reason.** D2's per-record `future_record` restart in calendar-pass is removed; the guard covers it, and it also catches records stored before the high water existed. `observer.calendar_restarted` now has reason `refused` only. The `future_record` log value was dropped.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E2] **Stale-change bound (E-e).** `staleChangeMs(policy)` = min(unasked.windows.sessionMin, idleMin, adjacentMin) minutes: 2 minutes under the shipped policy, and it follows the loaded policy. Reason: within that bound, every attribution window read around the first sighting contains every instant the change could have happened at. That includes the idle window, which is what says the user was not there. A wider bound (sessionMin) would let a user who was active at the change, then idle at detection, make an agent 'likely'. 'Older than' is strict: a gap exactly equal to the bound still accuses. Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E2] **Which rows the stale rule applies to.** It turns creations and updates whose base (the stored snapshot) was read more than the bound before the pass into first sightings with markers. It does the same for confirmed deletions whose first absence was compared with such a base. That base is kept as optional `PendingAbsence.baseAt`; a pending absence without one (stored before wave E) is read as late. The windows still record `changesSince` as before: only the row is kept from accusing. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E2] **coverageForMany (D2 verifier perf leftover).** It answers a list of queries in one read transaction with a per-transaction cache keyed by source and instant: the closed source's runs and each source's latest interval per account. Results equal `coverageFor` query by query (property-tested), so the coverage, its hash and replay are unchanged. `coverageFor` is the same path with one query. For a first-look marker, `recordCoverage` reads only the intervals ending at its instant: a marker merges only with an identical marker. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E2] **Test policy for other rules.** The cross-range tests (calendar-seen.spec) run under a policy with hour-long attribution windows (`WIDE_WINDOWS_POLICY` in calendar-pass-harness.ts), so their 10-20 minute gaps test that rule alone. first-look-orphans.spec keeps the shipped policy, with one extra pass of account A one minute before each change. Other pass tests moved their change passes within the 2-minute bound.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E] Calendar NOT_FOUND is simplified to state-based rules instead of patching each simulator finding: a snapshot reader cannot prove that an update or deletion did not happen between two looks, so calendar.updated and calendar.deleted claims are never NOT_FOUND (coverage.change_unprovable), and calendar.created NOT_FOUND means the event was not on the calendar in a whole, fresh view of its day. The wave E simulator (5,740 seeds, 23,420 receipts, replay exact) found 0 change claims NOT_FOUND, 0 pre-existing events UNASKED and 0 late-noticed changes UNASKED. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E] S2-SIM2-1 is scheduled, not fixed: when the wall clock moves back around a calendar.created claim, the event's sighting (stamped after the clock repair) can read as earlier than the claim, and a true claim goes NOT_FOUND, FINAL. Measured: 8 of 23,420 receipts, all in simulator histories built with clock jumps of up to 6 hours; 0 in histories without jumps. The fix needs the grading-batch runner (Sprint 3.6) to pass the repaired spans to grading so replay sees them; sprint_3.md 3.6 carries it with a test that reproduces the simulator's two cases, and docs/attacks-that-work.md lists it until then. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-04 [2.7-E] Visible Calendar tabs are read at least once a minute (OBSERVER_HEARTBEAT_MS = 60,000 ms, startObserver heartbeatMs) and again when they become visible. Without it, a quiet tab is read only when its DOM changes, its snapshot ages past the 2-minute attribution window, and almost no Calendar change could be attributed: the wave E simulator at the old pass cadence found 1 UNASKED where wave D found 2,399. With passes inside the window (its 20–200 s mode), agent changes were UNASKED as designed and none was false. A test pins heartbeat + max batch wait below staleChangeMs. Hard Rule 11 holds: only a tab the user has open and visible is read.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Gmail Sent head window (QA-1, TECH-8): the head of the Sent list starts its window at its oldest row's minute only when the page provably ends the list (an `Older` control is shown and every one is `aria-disabled="true"`). While the list may go on (an enabled `Older`, or none seen: a markup change or another display language proves nothing) it starts one minute later, written in the offset in force then. Gmail shows a send's minute only, so a send in that minute that is older than the oldest row is listed on the next page, unseen, and a window holding it would grade a claim about it NOT FOUND although it happened. The rows of that minute stay observations. A head whose every row is in the minute being read, with a list that may go on, posts no window (OK, no coverage), never one that ends before it starts. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Generic receipts are evidence, never coverage (SEC-6): a receipt the generic parser reads (a sender outside the registry) posts no coverage point, and a registry receipt dated in the same minute as a generic one of the same pass posts none either, so no point a pass posts holds a stranger's row. A generic receipt still verifies or supports a claim; platform receipts keep their point coverage. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Generic receipt display names (SEC-6): before the brand check, the generic parser refuses a display name that is not plain Latin text: once diacritics are taken off (é becomes e) every letter must be an ASCII letter, and no format, private-use or lone-surrogate character may appear. A mixed-script look-alike ("Аmazon" with a Cyrillic А), a name written in a script with Latin look-alikes (Cyrillic, Greek, Lisu, Cherokee) and a letter that passes for an ASCII one (ɑ, ı, fullwidth, mathematical) are refused: telling a stand-in from a real letter needs the whole Unicode confusables table, and the parser's triggers and total labels are English. A merchant whose name is not Latin is missed, never misread. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] A stranger's mail never makes gmail.receipt DEGRADED (SEC-V2): the generic parser also refuses a message whose display name is not one line of at most SCHEMA_LIMITS.MERCHANT characters, whose subject (cut to its 80 code points) holds a control character, or whose amount is over SCHEMA_LIMITS.AMOUNT_CENTS, because `redact.project` would refuse that row and the pass would lose the coverage of a real receipt beside it. A generic receipt whose date names no single minute is dropped; a platform receipt with such a date still throws `time_unresolved` (DEGRADED). Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Known limit, accepted until rows carry their parser: the coverage point of a registry receipt read in one pass holds a generic receipt read in another pass when both are dated in the same minute, so that stranger's receipt can be an orphan, and an UNASKED worded "likely". Measured on hand-built pages: a $499 generic receipt and a $1 Stripe receipt opened in two passes in the same minute make the generic row an orphan candidate (1 of 1); a minute apart, it is not (0 of 1). Not measured on real mail. Closing it needs an allowlisted row field that marks registry receipts and an orphan rule that reads it (a grading contract change). Listed in docs/attacks-that-work.md. Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] One shown-view rule and one Google-account reader for gmail.sent, gmail.receipt and gcal.event (TECH-5). `src/observers/shown.ts` (the `hidden` attribute and computed style, one memoised check per read) replaces the two copies in gmail/ and gcal/, and the receipt reader now reads only the `role="main"` Gmail shows, exactly one (two are `selector.ambiguous.main`). `src/observers/google-account.ts` reads the account from a banner label ending with `(address)` in any display language, two different addresses being ambiguous: gmail.sent DEGRADED `selector.ambiguous.account` as before; gmail.receipt DEGRADED `selector.ambiguous.account` where a conversation is open (before, it read the first English label and noticed no second account, so a non-English page sent pings without an account hash and did not tell the user's own mail apart); gcal.event refuses the account (`account.invalid`) as before, and keeps its English `text.accountPrefix` only to stay loud about a switcher drawn without an address. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Selector versions (agents/adapter_engineer.md rule 3): gmail.sent 1.1.0 (`older` and `account` selectors; the Gmail host now comes from OBSERVER_HOSTS.gmail), gmail.receipt 1.1.0 (`observer`, `fixtures`, `account`, `accountAttribute`, `dateTitle`, `dateAlt`, `text.noSubject`; `generic.refuseSubject` written to run in linear time), gcal.event 1.3.0 (the shared account reader), each checked against its tests/fixtures/pages/<vendor>/2026-10-03 folder. Every label of a folder names, as `selectorsVersion`, each observer whose `selectors.json` lists the folder (`gmail.receipt@1.1.0 gmail.sent@1.1.0`), and tests/unit/observers/fixture-selector-versions.spec.ts fails on a stale one (TECH-4). Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] SEC-7: the first alternative of `generic.refuseSubject` is `\d\s*%\s*off\b`, which matches what `\d+\s*%\s*off\b` matched in linear time; a 100,000-digit subject is classified in under 250 ms (about 13 s before), and every subject pattern is timed on long hostile subjects. The receipt selectors file states that its patterns must run in linear time on any subject, because a stranger writes it and the pass runs on Gmail's main thread. Hard Rule 3.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] One read of the page per pass (TECH-2) for gmail.sent and gmail.receipt: `src/observers/pass-memo.ts` `perPass` keeps a read for the synchronous run that made it and drops it at the next microtask, so health, rows, windows and account share one read and the next pass reads afresh. No observer writes the page, so nothing on it can change inside one run. Hard Rule 3.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Bench corpus honesty (QA-4, QA-V3): `gmailSentRealFixtures` counts real captures among the Sent lists the Sent accuracy is scored on, and the new `receiptRealFixtures` among the receipt positives and negatives, never among every Gmail page, so a real Drafts or Inbox capture never reads as a real Sent list or receipt page beside an accuracy measured on synthetic pages. Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Calendar changes other people make (SEC-1; Hard Rule 7; docs/grading.md v1.12 §3, §7). A Calendar snapshot holds no fact that tells a change the account made from one other people made: an invitation Google adds from anyone, an organiser's reschedule or cancellation, a colleague's edit on a shared or subscribed calendar. So the service worker stores every Calendar row (creation, update, confirmed deletion, first sighting) as a first sighting with its first-look marker: no gcal.event row is an orphan and no Calendar row is UNASKED, while the rows still verify claims. The rule is one predicate, src/background/calendar-own.ts accountMadeChange, which returns false. The late-change rule and the seen-elsewhere rule stay in calendar-step.ts and calendar-seen.ts, unit-tested with ownChange = () => true; handler tests of those rules run with that predicate mocked to true. src/core is unchanged, so replay is exact. Carry-forward to 2.8: confirm the organiser and owned-calendar facts on real pages (read-only, best knowledge), add them to the snapshot as derived facts, and answer accountMadeChange from them. Measured: 0 Calendar orphans over 40 seeded handler histories with other people's changes; golden 112.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Stored Calendar snapshot form (SEC-2; Hard Rule 2). calendarSnapshots keeps format 2: per event its first guest's address (a deletion's row names it) and guestKeys, SHA-256 of every guest address ASCII-lowercased, ascending, at most 200. That is all the diff and the seen-elsewhere check compare, so the store never holds every guest address in clear. The calendar.snapshot bus message keeps the page form (format 1, every guest as written), because an update's row names the first guest the update added, which only the new page shows. It is transient and never stored. A record of format 1 is read as format 2 and rewritten by its range's next pass. No data migration: Sprint 2 never shipped, and untouched records age out with retention. A guest must be one address exactly as normalize.email reads it, listed once in ASCII-lowercase order, at every boundary.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Calendar coverage windows are bounded (SEC-8; Hard Rule 5): a gcal.event entry spans at least a day less an hour and at most 42 days plus an hour (MAX_RANGE_DAYS, a DST hour either way), with an account or without; src/background/calendar-window.ts calendarWindowFits, applied to every gcal.event ping by the service worker. Local-midnight alignment is not required: in zones where DST starts at midnight a day's first instant is 01:00.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] A Calendar page that stays busy is DEGRADED page.busy (SEC-10; Hard Rule 6): busy passes form a run from the first one's instant; once a run has lasted 5 minutes (BUSY_LIMIT_MS) the next busy pass is DEGRADED until a pass finds the page drawn. A gap longer than two heartbeats (BUSY_GAP_MS, 120 s), or a clock that went back, starts a new run, because nothing says the page stayed busy while it was not read. Drawing itself stays OK (never cry wolf on a page loading).
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] At most 20 calendar.updated rows per Calendar event per day (SEC-11; Hard Rules 5, 6): CHURN_LIMIT = 20 and CHURN_WINDOW_MS = 24 h in src/background/calendar-churn.ts, counted in the range's record (updates: key, since, count). Past the cap, an event's updates are held back while its comparison base still follows the page. The pass records its windows without changesSince and breaks the run of followed changes, and the observer is DEGRADED ledger.event_churn until a pass holds nothing back. Creations, deletions and first sightings are never held back. A change claim a held-back row would have verified is partial_coverage, never NOT_FOUND (§6.1). Measured: 980 of 1,000 scripted edits of one event in a day held back.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] One Calendar event-state predicate (TECH-6; Hard Rule 7): sameEventState in src/observers/gcal/diff.ts decides both the diff's calendar.updated rows and src/background/calendar-seen.ts's 'held first by another range', in either snapshot form. A property test pins it to every field the diff reads, a guest's ASCII case excluded.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] The service worker loads no Calendar page code (TECH-7; Hard Rule 6): the plain-day helpers moved to src/observers/gcal/days.ts, so the worker and the ledger reach only gcal/days.ts, diff.ts, errors.ts and snapshot.ts, which read no selectors and no DOM. A selectors.json that fails its schema can then stop only the Calendar content script. The modules stay in src/observers/gcal (the observer builds the snapshot, the worker diffs it); tests/unit/background/calendar-imports.spec.ts holds the worker's import graph to those four.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Calendar content script (TECH-12): the decision that a page naming its account posts no rows is the observer's (src/observers/gcal/extract.ts), and the pass's account hash on its calendar.snapshot is added by src/observers/gcal/script.ts snapshotPost; entrypoints/content/gcal.content.ts only wires them, and never stops on ctx.onInvalidated (SEC-3).
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Calendar bench measures the shipped step (TECH-13; Hard Rule 12): bench/observers/gcal.ts runs each labelled diff through calendarStep as the service worker does (the policy's staleChangeMs, accountMadeChange), from a base of the `from` page read a minute earlier (a visible tab is read every minute), with a deletion confirmed by a second look a minute later. It reports gcalStepDiffsExact, gcalStepRowsCorrect, gcalStepRowsSpurious and gcalStepRowsUnmarked (every row must carry its marker under v1.12) instead of gcalDiff*, which scored the bare diff. The gate now also requires 0 unmarked rows. Supersedes the diff wording of the [2.5] bench gate entry. The observer's previous-snapshot option, its range member, its baseline continuation and the test-only passRows export are removed: the service worker alone holds a range's base.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] One read of a Calendar page per pass (TECH-2): src/observers/gcal/pass-read.ts reads the page once per synchronous pass through the shared perPass memo, keyed by the offset function (a caller with another one still gets its own read, so extract fails loud on a page health read differently). The observer lists the attributes its reads depend on (GCAL_ATTRIBUTES: the selectors' and the reader's) for the content script's watcher. Hard Rule 3: nothing is written to the page.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] The Calendar diff property suite moved to extension/tests/property/gcal-diff.property.spec.ts (TECH-15), with the event-state and stored-form properties; the handler property for other people's changes is extension/tests/property/calendar-third-party.property.spec.ts.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Page sync (S4, QA-V2; Hard Rule 5). A pass posts coverage only when its page can vouch that it shows the present. No pass posts coverage while the page reports itself offline (navigator.onLine false). After the page comes back, each observer's first pass and every pass within PAGE_RESYNC_MS = 30 s post no coverage, and a pass is requested when the 30 s are up. Coming back means: an online event; a resume after the tab was frozen; becoming visible after at least PAGE_RESYNC_HIDDEN_MS = 5 min hidden; or a gap of at least PAGE_SLEEP_GAP_MS = 3 min in the page's own PAGE_TICK_MS = 30 s tick, because a sleeping machine sends no event. Such a pass still posts its health (OK) and its rows, but no coverage ping and no Calendar snapshot, so the next snapshot is compared with the last trusted base. Why: a stale page would vouch for a span it never showed. 5 min is when Chrome starts waking a hidden tab's timers only once a minute; 3 min is three of those wake-ups, so a throttled tab never looks asleep. Known limit: a page online but cut off from its server reports itself online (live smoke 2.8).
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Observers stop only when the extension is gone (S5, SEC-3; Hard Rule 6). They are never stopped on WXT's ctx.onInvalidated, which a page can fire by dispatching WXT's start-up CustomEvent. The skeleton stops on a post rejected with BusError context_invalidated, or when the runtime id is gone at a flush (src/bus extensionContextAlive). Accepted: a page that knows Attest's public id can still tell it is installed, because WXT dispatches that event in its ContentScriptContext constructor unconditionally and removing it would need a patched WXT. tests/e2e/observer-startup-event.spec.ts pins both facts.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Attest does not run in incognito (S3, SEC-9; Hard Rules 2, 11). The manifest sets incognito: not_allowed, so Chrome never loads Attest in an incognito window, even when the user allows extensions there. No incognito Gmail or Calendar fact reaches the ledger, which lives in the regular profile and would outlive the incognito session.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] One watcher per content script (TECH-2; revisits [2.0] batching). startObservers runs a content script's observers with one MutationObserver, one activity tracker, one page-sync tracker, one batcher and one heartbeat, and one serial runner per observer. The watcher sees the tree, the text, and only the attributes the observers read (Observer.attributes) plus class, hidden and style. If any observer names no attributes, every attribute is watched. Why: hover and focus attribute churn started passes. A missed attribute can only delay a pass, never change what a pass reads (Hard Rule 5).
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Cost of a Gmail pass in the service worker (TECH-2; revisits the [2.0] note to revisit re-posting in 2.7). Each pass still re-posts its rows: the upsert is idempotent, and a record in the tab of rows already sent could skip a row that a restarted worker never stored (Hard Rule 5). A message's rows are now stored in one readwrite transaction with the clock guard. Each row's refusal is still collected: it is decided before the write and raised after the upsert's nested transaction settles, because a nested Dexie scope that rejects aborts the caller's whole transaction. A repeat of the stored health (same status, reasons and version) is not rewritten or announced until it is OBSERVER_HEALTH_REFRESH_MS = 60 s old; any change is written at once. Measured on an 11-row Sent pass: 3 readwrite transactions (was 18), 2 for a repeat pass.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Bounded coverage reads (TECH-1; Hard Rule 5). Schema v4 gives each coverage interval kind (point or span) and lenMs, indexed [source+kind+endMs] and [source+kind+lenMs]. Points are read only inside a window. Spans are read only where they can overlap it: ending within it plus the source's longest stored span. That bound is exact and read from the index, not a fixed maximum, so a longer window can never be missed. Gmail reads never go past the evaluation instant, because a Gmail window never ends after its sync. The Calendar overlap closure widens from its seeds instead of reading the whole source. Rows read, as counted by tests/perf/coverage-reads.perf.spec.ts with 2,000 markers and 90 days of windows (before → after): Calendar week ping 906 → 7, Gmail head ping 1 → 2, a batch of 100 claims 27,651 → 2,150.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Observer health follows the clock and the observer list (SEC-4; Hard Rule 6). A report for an observer the extension does not have is refused invalid_input and nothing of it is kept. A report checked after the worker's clock is refused clock_behind. The clock guard runs before a report is stored. Every health instant written raises the clock high water. The guard repairs adapterHealth: the observer's own report checked after now is deleted; a DEGRADED the worker set is re-stamped at now. A refusal is never stamped after the worker's clock, so a later clean pass can always clear it.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Observer messages are checked against the sender's site (SEC-V1; Hard Rules 5, 6). Health, rows, pings and snapshots are accepted only from a page whose origin is on the observer's site (OBSERVER_SITES, OBSERVER_HOSTS). The origin comes from sender.url, and sender.origin must agree when Chrome sets it. A refusal is invalid_input, logged observer.wrong_site, and does not turn the named observer DEGRADED, because a page on another site cannot vouch for that observer's health in either direction. Extension pages speak for no observer.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] The retention purge trusts the wall clock only as far as the ledger does (SEC-5; Hard Rules 2, 4, 5). It purges at min(now, max(the latest instant the ledger stored, the last purge's trusted instant + PURGE_CATCH_UP_DAYS = 2 days)). The latest stored instant is the clock high water, or the floor a repair left. The trusted instant is kept in meta purgeTrustedAt, and ledger.purge_clock_ahead is logged when now is more than 2 days past it. A grading batch's instant is not counted: batches run on alarms at whatever the clock says. Daily use purges exactly as before; a clock a year ahead deletes nothing current; an idle ledger catches up one day faster than real time per daily run.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Typed errors are logged by name and code (TECH-3; Hard Rule 6). Every Error class under src/ is in LOG_ERROR_NAMES and every code it can carry is in LOG_VALUES, enforced by a source scan (tests/unit/lint/log-errors.spec.ts). ObserverError gains invalid_selectors for the selectors.json loaders.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Fence edges (TECH-10; Hard Rules 2, 3). Extractors may not import the skeleton (base) or its planner (plan). The helpers next to each skeleton may not import the bus, any extension API or the skeleton. Code on a vendor page may not use extension storage (browser.storage, chrome.storage however reached, or WXT's storage helpers): it posts to the service worker through src/bus.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Zod's JSON Schema code is not shipped (TECH-17). A Vite plugin replaces zod classic's two JSON Schema modules with stand-ins that throw if called; aggregateChecks stays real. Gzipped sizes: Gmail 71,247 → 66,574 B, Calendar 66,753 → 62,056 B, the worker's shared chunk 23,524 → 18,737 B. Budget: 75,000 B per content script (tests/e2e/built-bundle.spec.ts). Script evaluation time in Chrome is a live-smoke (2.8) measure.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Observer coverage is gated (QA-8; qa_tester rule 8). pnpm test:coverage:observers measures src/observers, fails below 85% of lines, and runs in CI. Measured: 98.93% of lines.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Property suites live under tests/property (TECH-15). The ledger property suites are in tests/property/ledger, where they now also run in the test:tz time zones. They and calendar-third-party.property.spec.ts stay out of the poisoned run, like the other ledger suites that use fake-indexeddb with a pinned clock. A layout test refuses a property suite anywhere else.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] QA's restart and perf tests are kept (QA-2, QA-3). The 10,000-row Gmail Sent gate runs in Chromium because happy-dom cannot hold the page (about 0.5 MB of heap per row); median 532 ms CPU against 2,000 ms. Its expected head window follows the QA-1 rule. The pass-level Calendar attribution scenarios mock accountMadeChange to always true, so they keep testing the rules that apply once the live smoke can tell the account's own changes; the shipped v1.12 rule is tested by calendar-third-party.spec.ts. Carry-forward: an E2E where a receipt finalises after the worker restarts mid-grace, in Sprint 3.6.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Schema v4 drops the v1 *counterpartyTokens index on observations (TECH-13). Only tests read it; candidate retrieval reads [type+timeBucket] with core's own tokens. The upgrade removes the field from every stored observation, because the strict read would otherwise refuse those rows (Hard Rule 5: no evidence lost).
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Superseded (TECH-16): the two [2.9] entries that call a Calendar pass's rows, snapshot record and coverage windows separate writes, with the atomicity gap left documented, no longer hold. Since [2.7-D2], `inCalendarPass` (src/ledger/first-look.ts) stores a Calendar pass's rows, first-look markers, snapshot record and windows in one readwrite transaction, all or none, as docs/grading.md §3 requires, and docs/adapters.md says so. The [2.9] entry's other open items are answered by [2.close] entries: the Gmail Sent head minute (QA-1) and the live-smoke checks (MANUAL.md, task 2.8). Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] RETENTION_DAYS = 90 is in src/config.ts with the other constants, as the [2.6] entry asked; src/ledger/retention.ts imports it. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Closed: the [2.5-verify] request for one shared shown-view module is met by src/observers/shown.ts (TECH-5, entry above).
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] Two bounds recorded (TECH-16). REFUSED_KEPT = 32 (src/background/observer-state.ts): the service worker remembers the last 32 refused pass instants of each observer, so the coverage pings and snapshot of a pass whose rows were refused are refused too. A pass posts its coverage right after its rows, so 32 later refusals of the same observer would have to arrive in between, which takes more of that observer's tabs than a person has open. MAX_LABEL = 20,000 (src/observers/gcal/label.ts): a Calendar chip label longer than 20,000 characters is not parsed. Its event is `event.unreadable` and the pass DEGRADED, never a silent skip. A label listing 200 guests is about 7,000 characters, and the bound caps the work a page can make one label cost. Hard Rules 3, 6.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] The options page's Export section saves the whole ledger as one JSON file now, pulled forward from Sprint 4.5 because the live smoke needs it (MANUAL.md, task 2.8, step 8). The page asks the service worker over the bus (`ui.query` `ledger.export`), checks the answer with the ledger's own export schema, and hands the browser the file through a download link on an object URL: nothing is sent anywhere (Hard Rule 1) and no `downloads` permission is asked for (Hard Rule 8). The file is named for the export's own date (`attest-ledger-<date>.json`, the service worker's clock and offset), in the ledger's export format; `pnpm attest replay` reads it once Sprint 3.6 adds each receipt's inputs and policy to the export (sprint_3 3.6), and refuses it until then. A refused, malformed or unsaveable export is said on the page and logged as `options.export_failed`, never saved (Hard Rule 6). Receipts as JSON and CSV stay in 4.5.
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] The Gmail observers name the attributes their reads depend on (TECH-2, completing the one-watcher change above): gmail.sent `SENT_ATTRIBUTES` (its selectors' attributes and the ones it reads: aria-disabled, aria-label, data-hovercard-id, the three data-legacy ids, email, href, role, tabindex, title) and gmail.receipt `RECEIPT_ATTRIBUTES` (alt, aria-expanded, aria-label, data-legacy-message-id, data-legacy-thread-id, email, name, role, title), so the Gmail tab's watcher no longer starts a pass on a hover or focus change that touches only other attributes (one that changes `class` or `tabindex` still does; real pages are measured in Sprint 6.4). Each list must hold on its own: tests/unit/observers/gmail/watched-attributes.spec.ts removes every attribute a labelled Gmail page holds that an observer does not name, then sets it to "true" and to "false", and finds no change in what that observer's pass reads; changing a named one does change it. An attribute missed from a list could only delay a pass, never change what a pass reads (Hard Rule 5).
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] The gmail.sent and gcal.event selectors.json loaders throw ObserverError('invalid_selectors') on a malformed file, as gmail.receipt's throws ReceiptError('invalid_selectors'), instead of a bare ZodError the logger could only print as `Error` (TECH-3; Hard Rule 6). A malformed file never half-loads (tests/unit/observers/selectors-load.spec.ts).
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] An observer's `health(doc, ctx)` also receives the pass's context (src/observers/types.ts; build_spec §7's `health(doc)` with one optional argument, so every other observer is unchanged). The Calendar observer times its run of busy passes there, on every pass, so a run that already made the page DEGRADED `page.busy` still ends on a gap longer than BUSY_GAP_MS or a clock that went back, as the SEC-10 entry says. Before, the run was timed in `extract()`, which a DEGRADED pass never reaches, so a tab that sat hidden after going DEGRADED reported `page.busy` again on its first redraw (review R-5; Hard Rule 6: never cry wolf).
- 2026-10-05 [2.close] The live smoke (MANUAL.md, task 2.8) checks only what its evidence can show (review R-4). Three exports are taken: during the Wi-Fi-off minutes, within 30 seconds of waking, and two minutes later, so the agent can see that no coverage was written while offline or catching up. Steps were added for an undone send, a scheduled send and hovering rows. The founder checks the text-only items in the last export (subject lines, event titles), since the agent reads shapes, times and health only. The organiser line, the owned calendars, Gmail's `Older` control on the last page and the account label in another language come from the named captures. How often a real tab is read and how long a read takes cannot be seen in an export (nothing records passes), so it is carried to Sprint 6.4 with a pass counter that records no page content (Hard Rule 12).

The founder signed in to a Chrome profile used only for captures; the pages were opened by address and by page structure (Gmail searches by sender, Sent rows chosen by their ids, Calendar's own event editor), and `pnpm capture` saved 12 Gmail and 9 Calendar pages under `tests/fixtures/pages/<vendor>/2026-10-06/`. On the Calendar one test event, "Attest test", with no guests, was created, moved and deleted, and calendars were ticked and unticked and put back as they were. What is kept in clear: event titles (the founder agreed; the tool cannot hide them without breaking the label the reader parses), receipt subjects and bodies (merchants' mail, amounts faked). Hidden on Gmail pages: subjects, previews, attachment names and the screen-reader summary. Names, addresses and amounts are faked everywhere, the founder's own name and address included, and each file was checked for them in plain text and inside encoded ids. A Shopify receipt, a generic order confirmation and two newsletters were not captured, for the founder to run. Facebook Marketplace and OpenTable have no mail to capture. Hard Rules 2, 12.
- 2026-10-06 [2.1-real] gmail.sent reads only the subject anchor and the recipients a row shows: real Gmail repeats both, ids included, in a `display: none` summary for screen readers, and the reader had taken the copy as a second subject (`selector.ambiguous.subject`, DEGRADED on every real Sent page). Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-06 [2.1-real] Conversations in gmail.sent (grading contract v1.13): a row whose thread, last and last non-draft ids are all there but differ holds several messages, and its time is its newest message's. It gives no row, and the head's window starts no earlier than one minute after the newest such row, so the window holds only sends the pass timed. A row missing an id is refused (DEGRADED), so a markup change stays loud. Measured on the first real Sent page: 48 of 50 rows are conversations, the newest on 17 Sept 09:55, so the window was [17 Sept 09:56, now]. A reply sent in a thread is therefore UNVERIFIABLE from the Sent list, never NOT FOUND. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-06 [2.1-real] gmail.receipt message items (selectors 1.2.0, checked against gmail/2026-10-03 and gmail/2026-10-06): a `role="listitem"` with the message key `data-legacy-message-id` on it or on an element inside it (real Gmail puts it on an inner element), or with `aria-expanded`. Before, real Gmail matched no message item, so no real receipt was ever read. Measured on the real captures: Stripe, Instacart and Uber read as one receipt each; the Amazon capture is a shipping notice and reads as none. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-06 [2.1-real] The Google account is the last parenthesised address in the switcher's label: a second signed-in account's label carries a note after the address, so the old "ends with (address)" rule read no account there. Language-free as before; an address inside the display name still comes earlier. Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-06 [2.4-real] gcal.event on real Calendar (selectors 1.4.0, checked against gcal/2026-10-03 and gcal/2026-10-06): the side panel lists calendars as native checkboxes, read by their live `checked` state (ticking one changes the property only); lists drawn but still empty mean the page is drawing (OK, no coverage), since they fill seconds after the grid and only while the tab is shown, while a list whose items hold no box the reader knows stays DEGRADED `selector.missing.calendar_list`; month view's events are an element with `data-eventid` around a button. An event id the page shows that no chip reads now makes the pass DEGRADED `event.unreadable` instead of leaving the event out while a window vouches for its day. Real month view names each event's day only through a heading's text, so its events cannot be placed on days yet and a month view is DEGRADED until they can (carried forward). Hard Rules 5, 6.
- 2026-10-06 [2.4-real] What the real Calendar shows for re-enabling Calendar UNASKED (task 2.8, decision S1): the one event the account created itself (the test event, no guests) has no reply word in its chip label ("3pm to 4pm, <title>, <calendar>, No location, <date>"), while every other event from June to October reads "Accepted", the week's reservation included, which Google added from Gmail. None of eight events' editors showed reply buttons or an organiser line, and their titles were read-only. So a reply word may tell an invitation from an event the account organises, but one event without guests is not enough: the live smoke (step 5 adds a guest) checks what an event the account organises with guests reads before UNASKED relies on it. No invitation from another person was found to capture (`week-invited` not captured). Hard Rule 7.
- 2026-10-06 [tooling] The capture tool (tests/tools) now: writes a checkbox's live ticked state into the fixture (Calendar changes the property only); takes `--keep-selector` areas a redacted text is never replaced in elsewhere (a hidden summary's dates had replaced every row's date, and a hidden list's subject the open receipt's subject); and fakes the name in Google's short calendar ids inside event ids (`<name>@m` for a Gmail address), which it had left in every Calendar capture. Each has a test that fails without it. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-06 [2.1-real] The account's own address is left out of a Sent row's recipients (ASCII case folded), as the Calendar reader already left it out of guests: the first real Sent page held an email the founder sent to the account itself, whose recipient is the account, so its address would have been stored in clear although docs/privacy-architecture.md promises it leaves the page only as its hash. A send only to the account is still one row, with no counterparty: dropping it while the window vouches for its minute could grade a true "I emailed you" claim NOT FOUND. A page knows only the account it is signed in to, so an email from one of the founder's accounts to the other (also on that page) stores the other address as an ordinary recipient; the privacy document now says so, and the bench's account check is per page (a page's own account never in that page's output) rather than any account of the corpus in any output. tests/unit/observers/gmail/sent-own-address.spec.ts fails without the rule, with a dropped self-send, and with a case-sensitive compare. Hard Rules 2, 5.
- 2026-10-06 [tooling] The capture copy leaves out the page's own stylesheets (`style` joins the tool's drop list). It already writes each element's computed `display` and `visibility` inline, which is all a reader's shown-check uses, and a real Gmail page carries about 4 MB of style text: from it a test DOM took 1.4 s for one computed style and 22 s and 2.4 GB for one Sent pass; without it, 22 ms and 0.3 s and 154 MB, with the same result. The 21 captures of 2026-10-06 were taken before the change, so their `style` elements were removed the same way after capture (a check confirmed every other byte is unchanged; 63 MB became 15 MB). tests/e2e/capture-tool.spec.ts now checks a captured page holds no `style`. Hard Rule 12 (the bench measures pages it can run).

Each check still fails on the page it was written for. Hard Rules 5, 12.

The receipts bench counts 4 real pages among those it scores (Stripe, Instacart, Uber, Amazon); the Sent bench 4 real Sent lists; the Calendar bench 9 real pages. bench-corpus-honesty.spec.ts compares each count with the pages that bench scores, as each bench defines it (QA-V3), not with every page of the vendor. It now runs on every Gmail page, the real ones included (21 s); its sensitivity check (a named attribute does alter a read) runs on the synthetic pages, where each named attribute costs passes of its own. What they held, described without values: the founder's own address and Uber rider id inside Uber's click links (base64url of a zlib-compressed query, the `@` written `%40`); the trip's pickup and drop-off addresses in Google Maps link paths; card endings, the driver's and the Instacart shopper's first names, a private person's name with an order number, a Stripe receipt link token, a referral link and code. The Amazon page was a birthday gift-card email from a company, not the shipping notice the [2.1-real] entries call it, and those entries' "checked in plain text and inside encoded ids" missed the compressed links. In the 17 pages kept, the Inbox tab previews (a bank's offer, job alerts, a recruiter's name), a `tel:` number, links to other sites (Google One paths, one holding a region code) and four digits a display name kept from a real handle were removed after capture by the tool's own new rules; a deep leak test (fixture-deep-leaks.spec.ts) now decodes every link, token and path segment of every capture (percent, JavaScript escapes, base64 at four offsets, zlib, gzip, raw deflate, truncated streams, `<wbr>`-split text) and fails on any address that is not a fake, any short calendar id no fake address names, any path on another site's link, and any contact number. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-06 [2.close-r2] Capture tool (tests/tools), from the security re-review: a link to any host but the captured page's keeps only its scheme and host, parsed as Chrome parses it (WHATWG URL: backslashes, one slash, tabs in the scheme), and a link to the page's own host is kept as written only in a plain form; any scheme but http(s) keeps only its scheme, except `mailto:`, whose address the scrubber fakes in every encoding; a web address written as text or in any other attribute is cut the same way, a namespace (`xmlns`) excepted. A card ending after a mask, "ending in", "payment method" or a brand is faked, read in linear time. Digits glued to a learned name are faked with it. The documented Gmail capture commands redact the Inbox tab previews (`[role="tab"] .aKs`). Found on the way: the query stripper's first pattern began with a lookbehind and scanned back from every character (22 s on a line of 100,000 bullets); the `?` is now matched first, with the same results. Each rule has a test that fails without it. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-06 [2.close-r2] The account's own mailbox in Gmail Sent rows (this replaces the morning's [2.1-real] own-address entry): a recipient that is the account, in any case, with a `+tag` or (at Gmail) with other dots, is never stored (src/observers/own-address.ts), and a row that names it narrows the head's window like a conversation row. The morning's rule kept a send only to the account as a row with no counterparty, and the tech reviewer showed grading then made a true "I emailed you" claim NOT_FOUND (a pair with no content feature is no evidence, v1.11): now such a claim is UNVERIFIABLE. Storing the address again, which the claims table does whenever a claim names it, was the alternative; it was not taken, so the address leaves a page only as its hash. The same rule now leaves the account out of receipts (its own mail gets no parser) and Calendar guests; on a page that names no account the generic receipt parser reads nothing and Calendar stores no guest, since the account cannot be told apart there. A Google Workspace alias of the account is not caught. Hard Rules 2, 5.
- 2026-10-06 [2.close-r2] Grading contract v1.14 (docs/grading.md; golden scenario 113; tech re-review TECH-R2): every claim caps each `gmail.sent` entry last synced before its grace sync (`coverage.list_unsettled`). A Sent list read before it shows a reply an agent sent through the API (listed under its conversation's newest minute once the open tab refreshes, and a conversation row is never a row since v1.13) had vouched for the reply's minute, and a later pass's window starts after that conversation, so their union made a true claim NOT_FOUND. A too-early view's cap (`changes_unfollowed`, `list_unsettled`) is named only for a span no later view saw, and the ledger merges two `gmail.sent` intervals only as it merges two `gcal.event` ones (synced at one instant, or the earlier inside the later): without that bound the merge would have carried a later sync over the stale span and undone the cap, and the coverage property test showed the store and the raw passes naming different caps. Effect: an email claim is NOT_FOUND only on a view taken after its grace sync whose window still reaches the claim, which on a busy account (most rows conversations) will be rare; the live smoke (2.8) and Sprint 6.4 measure it. Hard Rule 5.

- 2026-10-07 [2.11] A third health status, IDLE: a page an observer does not read is neither OK nor broken. For gmail.sent that is any view but the Sent list (`not_sent_folder`, `not_sent_list`); for gmail.receipt a view with no conversation open (`no_conversation`). The service worker stores an IDLE report only where no page the observer reads has reported (nothing stored, or an IDLE), never lets a stored IDLE hold back a report from such a page, and never keeps an IDLE as the pass report that clears a DEGRADED it set. So a second Gmail tab no longer flips the observer's health (live smoke finding 2), and a Sent list that broke stays DEGRADED while other tabs show other folders (Hard Rule 6). Rejected: posting nothing from such a page (every pass reports its health, and a fresh install with only an Inbox open would show no row at all rather than one that says nothing was read); one row per tab (the health table is one row per observer, and the popup will show one status).

While Gmail is between two views it costs one DEGRADED pass, as `not_sent_folder` did before. gmail.sent 1.4.0 and gmail.receipt 1.5.0 name the readers that report IDLE (a version names a reader); every Gmail label was restamped, and its 30 not-Sent health facts relabelled IDLE. The Gmail bench compares IDLE like any status, and the receipt bench counts a list view as neutral when it is IDLE with nothing read.

- 2026-10-08 [2.close-r3] Fakes are keyed (SEC-L2). With the public default seed every fake was an unkeyed hash of its original, so a card ending, an amount or an order number could be recovered by running candidates through the faker, and a name or an address confirmed. Every fake is now HMAC(key; kind, original); a live capture reads its key from `~/.config/attest/capture-key` (32 random bytes made on first use, owner-only, never printed or committed), and `--seed` must be a secret of at least 32 characters, so `attest` is refused. The 8 unpushed captures of 2026-10-07 were re-faked under the key without their originals (`extension/tests/tools/capture-refake.ts`: amounts, card digits, phone numbers, addresses, labelled ids and codes, Meet codes, attribute UUIDs; emails, names and phrases kept, since they only confirm a guess, and the seeded secrets untouched), the Shopify order number named with `--also` because the capture had already faked its label, and the labels updated from the tool's record, which stays outside the repository and is deleted once the history is rewritten. Rejected: holding the 8 pages out until a re-capture (it would reopen task 2.1 for a founder step, while a keyed re-fake of the old fakes is as unlinkable without the key). The pushed pages of 2026-10-06 and every capture's names, emails and phrases are a founder step before the repository goes public (MANUAL.md). (Hard Rule 2.)

- 2026-10-08 [2.close-r3] An IDLE replaces the report of its own tab (QA-L8, SEC-L5, TECH-L3): a one-pass `view.mismatch` stayed DEGRADED for hours once its tab moved on, and a Sent tab that left Sent kept an OK of any age. The service worker keeps, in memory, the tab and frame whose report is stored (`tabKey`: a navigation or a reload is the same source); that tab's IDLE replaces it, another tab's never does, and nothing replaces a DEGRADED the service worker set. Lost on a restart, an IDLE replaces only an IDLE until a tab reports again: a DEGRADED may stay longer, never a made-up OK. Rejected: reporting `view.mismatch` only once it persists (a page that stops changing is never read again, so a stuck mismatch would go silent), and reading the Sent nav under another route as a mismatch (Gmail may keep Sent marked during a search started from Sent, a lasting false DEGRADED). A route Gmail changed now reads as IDLE, never as a stale OK. (Hard Rule 6.)

- 2026-10-08 [ci] A batch table ranks its ids by number (`CandidateTable.ranks`, batch-table.ts); the grading perf gate is unchanged. CI measured the gate at 1,977 ms on `main` and 2,040 ms on Dependabot PR #9 against its 2,000 ms limit. PR #9 bumps only `@types/node` and `eslint`, neither of which runs in grading, and the same code has measured 1,302 to 1,977 ms on CI, so the failure is the runner's variance. Past the head every id of the table shares, each id's next Crockford characters (as many as keep the key exact: base32.ts `idDigitsLength`) are read as one number (`idDigitsValue`; the alphabet is in code-unit order, so the numbers compare as the characters do), times the row count plus the row, and the keys are sorted natively; rows that tie there are ordered by whole id, and a table with an id outside the alphabet keeps the text sort. A property test pins it to the text order over 2,000 cases (heads shared to every depth, a repeated id, an id outside the alphabet). Measured on the bench workload, from CPU profiles of 5 warm runs each: `ranks()` 37 → 18 ms per batch, about 1.6% of gradeBatch, and all 5,803 receipts are byte-identical. Measured and not kept: coverage levels found by binary search over each source's entries (58 → 34 ms, but the sorted index cost 8 ms more: a net 16 ms, for a rewrite of the §6.1 level rules that no independent oracle pins); a faster time parser (75 → 69 ms per 300,000 timestamps, under 1% of a batch); a memo of month starts in that parser (the runtime guard's negative control needs every parse to call `Date.UTC`); and SHA-256 rounds written out in full (1.28× on hashing, but sha256.ts would pass the 400-line limit, and a second file would need its own literal exemption). Whole-batch timings of these changes stay inside the noise of same-code runs on this machine (up to ±70 ms), so no whole-batch saving is claimed. The limit and the method (median CPU of 3 fresh runs) are unchanged; whether CI should measure differently is the founder's call (MANUAL.md). (Hard Rules 4 and 12.)

This deviates from 3.0's order, which puts the hosts first. (Hard Rule 8.)
- 2026-10-08 [3.0] Claim patterns (rules in src/adapters/patterns.ts, tables in src/adapters/pattern-tables.ts). A sentence that asks, offers, plans or drafts is a proposal: `other` at 0.2 when it names an action (how a Muse approval prompt is captured, 3.2), no claim when it does not. A refusal or a step still under way ("I couldn't…", "Sending email…") is no claim: the finished step reports itself, and an `other` receipt for an action the agent says it did not take would only be noise. Patterns of two types give `other` at 0.4; a report that says it acted without saying how, or an action outside the ActionTypes (an RSVP, a share, a sign-up), is `other` at 0.3; a typed match carries 0.7 to 0.9. The two mistakes are not alike: a sentence wrongly read as an action can end in a NOT_FOUND against the agent, so anything that reads as not done is never an action. Ambiguities are resolved toward no type: a message with no channel named and a document sent to a name are `other`; anything sent to an email address is an email unless it is an invitation; `send_message` is Gmail's only with a Gmail prefix (Slack names its tool so too); adding or removing a guest updates an event rather than creating or deleting one; a meeting's notes, agenda or room are not the meeting. A sentence over 400 code units is no claim and every pattern is a bounded scan without nested quantifiers: the slowest crafted sentence classified in 0.4 ms warm, and a test bounds them. Page text is only ever matched against these fixed tables. (Hard Rules 4 and 5.)
- 2026-10-08 [3.0] The pattern corpora are invented: tests/fixtures/patterns/muse.json, chatgpt.json (Dots reads with ChatGPT's table) and claude.json hold 47 or 48 sentences each, every ActionType as a positive beside proposals, refusals and chatter. No real transcript exists yet (3.1, 3.3 and 3.4 wait on captures, MANUAL.md), so they check that the tables do what their rules say, not how agents really write. Tuned on them, every table types its whole corpus right. Twenty sentences written afterwards and measured unseen scored 10 of 20 for every vendor, every miss a lost or vague claim and none a wrong action. The tables then learned what generalises (plural event words, reminders, live listings, other actions as `other`, "nothing was ordered" as a refusal), and the 20 became tests/fixtures/patterns/shared.json, held to every table, with three misses kept on purpose (a new start time, a renewal that will change, a move of something not named as an event): reading those would also read a delivery moved to Friday as a calendar change. The test gates each vendor at 90% of its corpus plus the shared one (95.5% for ChatGPT, Dots and Claude, 95.6% for Muse) and checks that no sentence is ever typed as an action it is not labelled. Real accuracy is measured on captured fixtures in 3.8. (Hard Rules 5 and 12.)
- 2026-10-08 [3.0] The adapter skeleton (src/adapters/base.ts) runs on the observer skeleton's loop instead of a second copy of it: `startObservers` became `startPasses(targets, runOne)` plus the observer checks, with the same steps in the same order, and plan.ts exports `checkHealth` for both. A pass posts `adapter.health`, then `adapter.claims` (at most 500 Requests and 500 Claims per message, none when the page holds neither), then, from a settings page whose pass was clean, `adapter.scope`; a post that rejects ends the pass, and a message that fails its schema leaves only a DEGRADED `message.invalid` report, as an observer's does. The skeleton's own reasons are `ADAPTER_REASONS` (docs/adapters.md §10.4). `assertAdapter` refuses an adapter whose hosts are not its agent's (Dots runs on ChatGPT's) and, until its host is confirmed, any Muse adapter: `AdapterError('invalid_adapter')`. (Hard Rules 3, 6 and 8.)
- 2026-10-08 [3.0] A turn whose time the page does not show, shows in a way neither reader reads (an ISO 8601 instant with an offset, or vendor-relative text through `resolveRelative`), or puts after the pass gives no Request and no Claim, and the pass is DEGRADED `time.unknown` while still posting what it read: a claim placed at a guessed time could end NOT_FOUND. A vendor whose chat shows no time per message (to be checked on the captures of 3.1 to 3.4) needs another source of time in its adapter; until it has one, its claims are reported lost. An ISO instant is written again in the user's offset, so every `claimedAt` ends in `±hh:mm` and the §10 `rawHash` truncates it in that offset. (Hard Rules 5 and 6.)
- 2026-10-08 [3.0] What §10 leaves to the adapter: a Request's `textHash` is the SHA-256 of the turn's text as one line (white space collapsed, NFC), its `textHint` the first 200 code units, never half a surrogate pair; a Claim's `text` is its sentence, one line, NFC. A page without a conversation id gives no Requests (the schema needs one, and a title is never one). A claim links to the conversation's last Request before its turn; a user turn whose Request was lost breaks the link rather than lending the claim an older request; activity entries link to none. A claim's counterparty is the address its sentence names, else the capitalised words a pattern captured (never a day, a month or an article); a bare `$` keeps its cents and no currency, which the grant's `spendCurrency` reads (§6.1). A calendar claim carries no `targetAt` until a vendor adapter reads the event's own time, so it grades `no_target` until then. (Hard Rules 2 and 5.)
- 2026-10-08 [3.0] Scope snapshots travel as a new bus message, `adapter.scope`, whose `ScopeSnapshot` (agent, the scopes as the page names them, one line and at most 120 code units each, at most 200, `observedAt`) is defined in src/bus/messages.ts with the same item rule as `Grant.observedScopes`, not in src/core/schema.ts, which it would push past the 400-line limit. The service worker answers it from 3.7, when it updates the grant; until then the listener drops it as `no_handler`, like `adapter.claims` before 3.6. (Hard Rules 2 and 8.)

That follows the generic receipt parser, whose 0.6 prior lives in its selectors.json. The schema keeps every level in [0, 1] and every `other` level below every action level; the pattern test holds every `other` to the sprint's 0.4 at most. Otherwise "Done! I sent an email to …" gave an `other` beside the email, and a two-sentence approval prompt two `other` claims, each a receipt that says nothing. - 2026-10-08 [3.10] Meta (Muse). muse.ai is Meta Platforms, Inc.'s: its Supplemental Terms (last updated 8 September 2026) bind the user to them, the Meta Terms of Service and the Meta AI Terms. Meta Terms of Service §3.2 (effective 1 January 2025, US): "You may not access or collect data from our Products using automated means (without our prior permission) or attempt to access data you do not have permission to access, regardless of whether such automated access or collection is undertaken while logged-in to a Facebook account." Decision (the founder, 2026-10-08): ship disabled. The Muse reader is off, with a note in the options page, until Meta permits it.
- 2026-10-08 [3.10] OpenAI (ChatGPT, Dots). Terms of Use (effective 1 January 2026; read from archived copies of 6 to 8 October 2026, because the live page refused the fetch), under "What you cannot do": "Automatically or programmatically extract data or Output." Decision (the founder, 2026-10-08): ship disabled. It was a close call against ship with notice: the user owns the Output, but the clause has no exception for it.
- 2026-10-08 [3.10] Anthropic (Claude). Consumer Terms (effective 8 October 2025) §3: "To crawl, scrape, or otherwise harvest data or information from our Services other than as permitted under these Terms", and, except with an API key or explicit permission, "to access the Services through automated or non-human means, whether through a bot, script, or otherwise." Decision (the founder, 2026-10-08): ship disabled, a close call as for OpenAI.
- 2026-10-08 [3.10] Google (Gmail, Calendar). Terms of Service (effective 30 July 2026, US) bar "using automated means to access content from any of our services in violation of the machine-readable instructions on our web pages (for example, robots.txt files that disallow crawling, training, or other activities)". robots.txt governs crawlers, and Attest fetches nothing and reads only pages the user opened. The Gmail Program Policies bar automating the Gmail interface to send, delete or filter mail in a misleading way, and Attest never acts. Decision: ship. That was the agent's recommendation; the founder's choice of 2026-10-08 kept off only the three agent readers. (Hard Rule 3.)

Attest reads no agent's site. Only claims cross to the service worker, as `claims.import` from extension pages, and the text is dropped once the preview closes. The parser returns the `AgentRead` an adapter returns, so `claims.ts`, the pattern tables and the §10 ids are reused, and pasting the same log twice adds nothing. Times come from the log in the person's zone, day precision under a day header without a time; an entry with no time is not imported (Hard Rule 5). - 2026-10-08 [3.11] The paste parser (src/adapters/paste.ts) takes a day only from the paste. A clock time is read on the day of the heading above it. With no heading, or under a heading that names a period or a date in digits ("Earlier", "Previous 7 days", "10/6/2026"), the entry is "no day" and not imported, although on a live page the resolver reads a bare clock as today or yesterday: a paste may be a few entries copied from an older section without its heading, and those would land on the wrong day. An entry right above a day heading is "no time", since that day could be its own time or the next section's heading. A time counts on an entry's own line only at its start, before a separator ("4:33 PM · …"), so a sentence that ends in a time ("for Friday at 12:30 pm") or a time range stays text. A line holding a clock time is never a heading, even when the resolver reads it as a whole day (a time that happened twice as the clocks went back). The log's shape was checked, by kind only, against the founder's saved Muse page: one day heading, then 16 entries of a title line, a summary line and a clock line, and no title reads as a claim. So a title adds no second claim, and `claimsOfTurn` is reused unchanged. Relative words are read against the paste instant, and the preview (3.12) shows each claim's date and time before the person confirms. The labelled pasted-log corpus and its bench (agents/adapter_engineer.md rule 10) come with 3.8's measurement and the amended gate at the sprint's end, since 3.11's Done-when is unit and property tests. (Hard Rules 2 and 5.)

The text lives in the component's state only while the form or the preview is open; closing drops it, and only the claims are passed on. The preview writes each claim's time from its own ISO text (the person's wall clock when the log was read), not through the page's time zone, so it shows what the log said. It grades the claims with no receipt or a PENDING one `gradedAt` admits, fixes `preAssignments` of every receipt, and stores the result by one pure rule now shared with the replay simulator (src/core/batch-store.ts `storeBatch` and `supersede`, moved out of tests/tools/attest-ledger.ts), so replay is tested against the ledger the service worker writes. Coverage is one `Coverage` per batch (src/ledger/coverage-batch.ts): each graded claim's window, plus, for every type, the span in which an orphan's PENDING UNASKED can still change (`finalizeUnverifiableHours` + `graceMin` + `beforeClaimMin` back from `evaluatedAt`, derived from the policy, not a new number); a union of sets closed under overlap stays closed (v1.9). Batches queue in the service worker, each reading the clock when it starts. Default grants (every type, no allowlist, no ceiling, as 3.6 planned) are written for any agent without one, never over the user's. A test stores the §2 demo as the service worker would, grades before and after grace, and replays the export: 2 of 2 reproduced. (Hard Rules 4, 5 and 7.)
- 2026-10-08 [3.13] The import and the grace alarm, the third part of 3.13. `claims.import` is a bus message only an extension page may send (listen.ts drops it from a content script as `untrusted_context`, as `ui.query`); its schema admits only claims the paste parser read for that agent (extractor `import.<agent>`, an activity view, no request), never text, and its answer is its own schema, `{ok: true, received, added}` or `{ok: false, code}`, so the side panel says how many claims were new (a log pasted twice adds 0) rather than a bare acknowledgement. The handler guards the clock, writes missing default grants, stores each claim on its own through `upsertClaim`, records the import instant as the agent's last import (meta `lastImportAt.<agent>`, never moved back: the adapter sync that UNASKED finality reads, since the pasted log shows the agent's actions up to then), runs a batch, and answers once the receipts are stored; a failing batch is logged loud (`grading.failed`) and the claims stay for the alarm. The grace alarm `attest.grade` runs a batch every 5 minutes (recreated a minute after a browser start), so a service worker stopped mid-grace finalises at the next alarm: a test closes the ledger and drops every module's memory between the two batches of the §2 demo. A batch is not run after each observer pass: the alarm regrades within 5 minutes. The policy document's schema moved to src/core/policy-document.ts, and the replay records check a document by hashing its canonical JSON (as `loadPolicy` names a policy), so the ledger never imports policy/default.json, which Node cannot load without an import attribute (the Playwright tests import the ledger's schemas). The popup no longer says Attest reads no page (untrue since Sprint 2): it says there are no receipts yet. (Hard Rules 1, 2, 4 and 5.)

The paste path had neither: no session pings without reading the agent's site, and no idle intervals at all, while the activity-log signal is suppressed there by the email claim a minute earlier (§7). - 2026-10-09 [3.14] The UNASKED signals on the paste path. Sessions: the minutes a pasted log's timed entries name, a claim or not (a search is the agent working too), read by the claim builder's own `statedTime` at minute precision (a day, or no time, names none), carried by `claims.import` as `activeAt` (at most GRADING_INPUT) and kept once per agent and minute in the new `activity` table (schema v6: exported, purged at retention, raising the clock high water). Idle: opt-in through the optional `idle` permission (Hard Rule 8: the first permission beyond the manifest's three, asked only on the person's click in the options page's Privacy section). While it is held, Chrome's idle states (60-second detection) open an interval at the last instant input could have been and close it when the person is back; a locked screen counts from the instant it locked; the open interval is named in meta so a restarted worker closes it, and one never closed keeps only what was confirmed. Turning it off gives the permission back and deletes every interval. Content-script activity (3.5's first idea) was not used: a quiet Gmail tab is no evidence the person was away. The batch reads both over the orphan horizon plus the widest attribution window. The §2 demo stored the paste path's way, with Muse's log minute and a recorded 14:00 to 14:09 away interval, grades the vendor row UNASKED, likely Muse, FINAL after a second paste past its grace, and replay reproduces 3 of 3; without the interval the session alone accuses no one. Two more gaps for the end-to-end test: a pasted calendar claim has no event time (`no_target`), so the demo's NOT_FOUND cannot happen until a claim's own words give one (new task 3.15; the end-to-end test is 3.16 and S2-SIM2-1 3.17); and an away interval on the demo's day cannot be recorded live, so the end-to-end test will write the one away detection would have recorded into the extension's own IndexedDB from its service worker, as it simulates grace. PRIVACY.md 0.2 and docs/privacy-architecture.md §3 and §5.11 describe both. (Hard Rules 2, 5, 7 and 8.)
- 2026-10-09 [3.15] Event starts from a claim's own words. `upcomingIn` reads the first day-then-time phrase of a calendar claim's sentence forward from the claim's time in the person's zone: a weekday (the first on or after the claim's day), today, tomorrow, or a month and day (the first on or after it; with a year, exactly), then 2pm, 2:00 pm, 14:00 or noon. Nothing else is read, and the claim stays `no_target`, UNVERIFIABLE (Hard Rule 5): a day alone, a time alone or before its day, midnight (which night?), the claim's own weekday or date at a time already past (this week or next?), a weekday that disagrees with its date, and a clock that happens twice or never on a DST day. It lives behind the resolver's own text cleaning, as a reader export of src/core/normalize-relative.ts, so the later-engine Unicode property holds it like `resolveRelative`; the phrase logic and the name patterns (written out, as the core Unicode fence requires, with a test holding them to the tables) are in normalize-upcoming.ts, and the ASCII name tables and clock parsing moved to normalize-names.ts. A pasted entry now gives at most one claim per action type, the one naming most facts (the later sentence on a tie): a title that reads as an action ("Put a review on the calendar": put is past and present alike) beside its summary would otherwise claim one action twice, and one of the two would be NOT_FOUND once the other took its evidence. Two actions of different types in one entry stay two. The §2 demo's own log, pasted at 14:05, gives the calendar claim Thursday 14:00 and grades VERIFIED and NOT_FOUND through the ledger; the email's subject ("with Q3 deck") is no pattern's capture, so it verifies on name and time. (Hard Rules 4 and 5.)
