- 2026-10-09 [3.17] S2-SIM2-1 is fixed (grading contract v1.16). Each clock repair keeps its span in `meta` (`clockRepair.<from ms>` = `<from>/<to>`, written in the repair's own transaction, src/ledger/clock-repairs.ts), every grading batch passes the spans to grading in its coverage (`Coverage.repairs`), and a `calendar.created` claim whose first-seen bound `start(p,t) − beforeClaimMin` lies in a span has every `gcal.event` entry capped with the new reason `coverage.clock_repaired` (§6.1): `partial_coverage`, UNVERIFIABLE once final, never NOT_FOUND. The spans are hashed into each receipt's `inputsHash` through `coverageHash` and stored with its replay record, so replay sees the same fact (Hard Rule 4); an empty list hashes as none, so no v1.15 hash or golden id changed. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-09 [3.17] The cap reads the claim's first-seen bound, not its window, although sprint_3.md 3.17 says "whose window meets a repaired span". A repair can mislead grading only through §4.1's first-seen rule, which compares a sighting with the claim's bound. Of the sightings stored after the claim, one stored after a repair reads at or after the repair's `from`, and one with no repair between it and the claim reads at or after the claim's own sighting, which the high water held; so such a sighting can read as earlier than the bound only when the bound lies in a span (a sighting stored before the claim is the rule working: the event was already there). A claim's window (event start ± 3 days) meeting a span says nothing about that comparison, and capping on it would make true NOT_FOUNDs UNVERIFIABLE for no reason. Hard Rule 5.
- 2026-10-09 [3.17] The span is `[now at the repair, the high water the repair found]`, not up to the clock floor as the Sprint 2 report proposed: the floor is the latest of every repair's high water, so after a second repair it would stretch the new span over instants an earlier span already holds, or that no clock stamped since. Two spans from one instant keep the later end. A grade sees the spans found by its `evaluatedAt` (`from` at or before it), as it sees only the coverage entries synced by then, so replay at an earlier instant sees what grading saw. A batch passes every kept span; beyond 200 (`SCHEMA_LIMITS.LIST`, the coverage's cap) the oldest are joined into one span from the earliest `from` to the latest `to`, which holds every instant they held and caps more claims, never fewer. A span is purged with the dated history once it ended before the retention cutoff: by then no claim it bears on is still graded, and replay reads the spans from each receipt's stored inputs, not from `meta`. Hard Rules 4, 5.
- 2026-10-09 [3.17] The simulator's two S2-SIM2-1 cases, as tests: the Sprint 2 simulator is not in the repository and its run outputs are gone, so the two cases are its two clock moves (§3 v1.11), each played through the real Calendar handlers, clock guard, ledger and grading batch (tests/unit/background/clock-repair-grading.spec.ts): a clock three hours ahead while the claim is stamped, then set right; and a clock that steps back 110 minutes for good just after the claim. Each is UNVERIFIABLE with `coverage.clock_repaired`, and replay reproduces it from the export; each control, the same history with the kept span removed, is NOT_FOUND, FINAL, the defect itself. Golden 114 pins the rule in core, with a claim from before the jump that is still NOT_FOUND. The new limit (a false creation claim stamped in a span is UNVERIFIABLE, never NOT_FOUND) replaces the defect in docs/attacks-that-work.md; its rate is not re-measured. Hard Rules 5, 12.
- 2026-10-09 [3.close] SEC-1 (HIGH): a time away the browser exited during is closed as it stands by the next browser session, never stretched over the hours in between. Chrome reports no return from it (a shutdown from the lock screen, an update, a crash), so the open mark in `meta` outlived the session and the next return extended last night's interval over a working day: in the review's probe the person's own send became UNASKED, FINAL, likely Muse. `chrome.storage.session` holds one flag, `attest.idleSession`, true once a browser session has started watching; Chrome empties it when the browser closes, so the first watch of a new session finds it missing and closes the left-open interval at its confirmed end (`endOpenIdle`), while a service worker restarted inside one session finds it and leaves the interval open for the return Chrome will report. Not `runtime.onStartup`, which the tech checklist keeps to the alarms, and no random token, which would be a second identifier beside the install id. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-09 [3.close] SEC-2: a session ping and an activity entry of one agent at one instant are one fact (grading contract v1.17, §7). On the paste path a log's timed entry is both, so one pasted calendar action fired agentSessionActive and activityLogAdjacent together, 8000 bp against the 6000 threshold, and made a send of the person's own two minutes later UNASKED, likely that agent; Decisions [3.13] relied on UNASKED needing more than one signal. When adjacency fires and every session ping in reach sits at the instant of an entry that fires it, only the heavier of the two signals fires (adjacency on a tie); a ping at any other instant, such as the minute of a line that is no claim, still counts. The review left the rule to the founder; it is decided here in the direction Hard Rule 7 asks (fewer accusations, never more), and the §2 demo is unchanged, since its email claim suppresses adjacency for the vendor send and its ping plus the time away still give 0.7. Simply dropping claims' minutes from the session pings was rejected: it would have taken the demo's only ping. No existing golden changed; golden 115 holds the rule with a control. The founder can overrule. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-09 [3.close] SEC-3: the clock repair now repairs activity too: every session ping and time away stamped after the clock's now is deleted, a time away that reaches past now is cut at now, and the open mark goes with a row it removed (`repairActivity`, step 6 of the guard). Either row would have counted as UNASKED evidence once real time reached it. It is logged as its own event, `ledger.clock_activity_repaired` with a row count, because the logger forbids spreading optional fields into `ledger.clock_regressed`, whose shape stays as it was. Receipts already graded keep their attribution in their replay inputs, so replay is unaffected. Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-09 [3.close] TECH-1 (HIGH): each grading batch's coverage is stored once (schema v7, `batchCoverages`, keyed by the core `coverageHash`), and each `receiptInputs` row names it by hash instead of holding a copy. Every receipt of a batch was graded with the same coverage, and at 200 Calendar markers a copy was 62 KB, rewritten for every PENDING receipt by every batch; the export, one runtime reply, would have passed Chromium's 64 MiB message cap at about 1,000 receipts. A coverage is released when the last input naming it goes (on regrading, and in the purge's full sweep). The export gains a third map, `batchCoverages`; the replay reader checks each coverage hashes to its key and joins every input with its shared coverage object, so `src/core` replay is unchanged. The v7 upgrade moves every stored copy into the new store. Measured in a test: three batches of twenty receipts each with a 200-marker coverage store three coverages, and the export stays under three copies plus two kilobytes per receipt. The export still travels as one message; with one coverage per batch it grows with the number of batches that wrote kept receipts, not receipts, and a chunked export is not needed at that size. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-09 [3.close] TECH-3 and SEC-6: the paste parser refuses a log whose timed entries name more minutes than one import carries (`too_many_times`, at SCHEMA_LIMITS.GRADING_INPUT), so every log it accepts is one valid `claims.import` message (a property holds it); before, an 87,445-character log passed the parser, failed at send without a trace, and the panel said "Try again" forever. Every catch the reviews found silent now logs: `sidepanel.import_failed`, `options.away_check_failed`, `options.away_change_failed`, and the import's own `bus.import_refused` and `bus.import_unavailable` instead of `bus.query_unavailable`. A permission check Chrome cannot answer now says Attest could not tell whether away detection is on, with Turn on offered, instead of claiming it is off; it is still never read as on. Hard Rules 6, 10.
- 2026-10-09 [3.close] The paste parser 1.1.0 (QA review GAPs 1, 2, 6, 7): (1) after the resolver has had its turn, a line made only of date words and numbers (month and weekday names, ago, last, this, ordinals, years) is a heading whose day is unknown, so an entry under "September 2026", "2 weeks ago", "Last Thursday" or "Oct 1st" is never read on the day of the heading above it; "Earlier today" and "Oct 1st" name a day, but the resolver does not read them, so they too give no day rather than a guessed one (Hard Rule 5). (2) A time line is classified after NFKC folding and dropping the characters a copy can carry that show as nothing (U+00AD, U+180E, U+200B to U+200F, U+2060 to U+2064, U+FEFF), so a full-width or invisibly split clock stays a clock on its heading's day. (3) The whole paste is read in NFC, so a log copied in NFD gives the same claims and never an `id_conflict`. (4) A move's event start is read after its last "to", the start it moved to; a move that names none there has no target, never the start it left. The extractor version is 1.1.0; the ledger's claim identity ignores the extractor, so a claim read the same way still re-imports as the same claim. Hard Rule 5.

- 2026-10-09 [3.close] TECH-2: a grading batch with nothing to grade skips: no receipt PENDING (finality is the only rule that reads the clock) and no fact stored since the last batch that graded. The signal is a count, not the clock high water: the high water is raised with a row's sighting, which can be earlier than a batch that ran before the row was stored (a claim pasted, a batch, then the import), so a high-water test could skip that claim for good. Every writer already calls `noteStoredInstant` in the transaction that stores the fact, so it now also counts it (`meta` `storedWrites`), and a batch that grades records the count it saw (`gradedWrites`); a ledger that never recorded one always grades. Measured by a new perf gate over 2,000 claims and receipts in 90 days (Node, fake IndexedDB): a steady-state batch 394–411 ms median CPU (gate 1,500 ms), an idle one 1.3 ms (gate 50 ms). The full-table read of a batch that does grade stays: bounding it needs an index the claims do not have (their `observedAt` instant), measured above and gated so it cannot grow unnoticed. A receipt cannot go from PENDING to FINAL by grace alone in a test that lasts minutes, since a claim's grace sync is at least its end plus graceMin (30 minutes); so the claim is pasted with no Gmail tab open (UNVERIFIABLE, PENDING), Chrome stops the worker through CDP, the Sent tab then opens and its pass wakes the worker, and the next grace alarm's batch finalises the receipt NOT_FOUND from the ledger alone; replay reproduces it. - 2026-10-09 [4.0] The pages read the ledger themselves, on their own connection to it (src/ui/data.ts over src/ledger/views.ts), never through the service worker: a stopped worker takes time to start, and the popup must paint in under 100 ms. They only read. Every change a person makes still goes to the service worker over the bus, so it stays the ledger's one writer. Pages run on the extension's own origin, which already holds the ledger, so this exposes nothing new; content scripts still cannot import the ledger (the lint fence). `ledger.changed` tells a page to read again (`onLedgerChanged`, filtered to the tables it shows). Hard Rules 1, 2.
- 2026-10-09 [4.0] Schema v8 indexes receipts by `[agent+computedMs]` and `[agent+grade+computedMs]`. `computedMs` is `computedAt` in epoch ms, a derived index field: stripped on read, never exported, and holding nothing the receipt does not. A page of receipts newest first is one read per agent (or per agent and grade when a filter asks for grades), each stopping at the page size plus one. So a page reads the same number of rows however many receipts the ledger holds, and a filter for a rare grade reads none of the others. `[agent+date]` could not do this: a day's receipts come in id order, and ids are hashes. The v8 upgrade derives the field over every stored receipt. A row that fails its strict read is skipped and logged by id, and still counts toward its page, so the next page starts where this one ended. Hard Rules 2, 6.
- 2026-10-09 [4.0] The popup's "today" is the receipts graded since local midnight in the page's zone (`computedAt`, the last grading), newest first by that instant, at most 50 (`POPUP_RECEIPT_LIMIT`; the side panel's timeline pages 50 at a time, `TIMELINE_PAGE_SIZE`). A PENDING receipt is regraded by every batch, so every open check is in it. A check still open from yesterday joins it at the next batch (within 5 minutes, or a minute after the browser starts), and the popup reads again on `ledger.changed`. The popup also gets the last batch instant, so 4.2 can tell a PENDING receipt the batches no longer regrade (a claim-less one that holds reverted overrides) and not call it "checking" forever. A grade filter matches the grade Attest computed: an overridden receipt is found under that grade and shows its override beside it, so an override cannot hide a problem from a filter. Sprint 4 rule: the UI is a view of the ledger.
- 2026-10-09 [4.0] "Every query hits an index" is a test: a recorder sits between Dexie and IndexedDB (a DBCore middleware, tests/unit/ledger/read-recorder.ts). It sees every read with its table, index and range, and counts the rows each table delivers. Any read of a whole table fails the test, except the three tables Attest keeps one row per observer and account, adapter or agent (coverage, adapterHealth, grants), which the views read whole by design. A negative control proves the recorder catches a scan. The "< 50 ms" is measured on the done-when's own ledger: 5,000 receipts, 1,800 of them graded today, ten tied at each batch instant. It is gated at 50 ms of median CPU in tests/perf/ui-reads.perf.spec.ts (popup about 17 to 19 ms; a timeline page, a page filtered to rare grades and a detail read are held to the same budget), and recorded by `pnpm bench` as the `ui` section of RESULTS.md. That run is Node over fake IndexedDB, 50 to 165 times slower than Chrome's; 4.2 measures the paint in Chrome. Hard Rule 12.
- 2026-10-09 [4.1] Design tokens: `src/ui/tokens.css` defines Tailwind `@theme` tokens, so each is a utility class (`bg-grade-verified-bg`, `text-grade-not-found-fg`, `p-gutter`). There are three per grade (text, fill, edge), the degraded yellow, the page's surface, ink, muted ink and rules, and three spacings. Dark values replace them under `prefers-color-scheme: dark`. The values are Tailwind's own palette, written out, because Tailwind emits a palette colour only where a utility uses it. The grade colours are those of agents/ux_engineer.md rule 1: VERIFIED green, PARTIAL amber, NOT FOUND red, UNASKED purple, OVER SCOPE a red outline on the page's own surface (so it reads apart from NOT FOUND), UNVERIFIABLE grey. Every chip carries its name ("Grade: Not found" to a screen reader), so colour is never the only signal. axe-core finds no serious or critical violation, contrast included, in either theme. ux rules 1, 10.
- 2026-10-09 [4.1] The words. Grades are named as nouns: Verified, Partial, Not found, Unasked, Over scope, Unverifiable. An UNASKED receipt's badge reads "Likely agent: Muse" (Hard Rule 7). Times are 24-hour on the page's own clock, as in the ux rules' examples ("14:10", "09:12"), with the day when not today and the year when not this year. The coverage header has one line per source in the schema's order (Gmail Sent, Gmail receipts, Calendar). A line says "checked HH:MM" when the latest check of any account is at most 10 minutes old (`COVERAGE_FRESH_MINUTES`: an open tab reports at least every 5 minutes, even hidden), "not checked since HH:MM" when older, and "not checked yet" with none. Lines that are not current are drawn in the degraded yellow and say so in words. Re-planned for the paste path ([3.gate]): the sprint's "Muse active" becomes "Muse activity added HH:MM", from the agent's last import, because Attest reads no agent's site and cannot see an agent active. A piece of evidence reads in one line ("Sent-folder row, 14:02, to priya@example.com, “Q3 deck”"), then every stored field, with "First seen" (its `sightedAt` when the ledger stored it later, else `observedAt`) apart from "Stored" (`observedAt`), as the Sprint 1 close asked of the detail drawer. ux rules 2, 4, 5; Hard Rules 5, 7.
- 2026-10-09 [4.1] The degraded banner is a yellow alert. It names each DEGRADED reader and its version and says its receipts may be Unverifiable. Its "Report on GitHub" link opens a new issue on the project's repository (`ISSUES_URL`), in a new tab, with the title and body filled in: the adapter's id and version and the Chrome major version, and nothing else (no reason code, no page, no account). Nothing is sent unless the person follows the link, and following it is their own visit to GitHub, so no request leaves from Attest itself. The link needs the repository to be public; that is the existing MANUAL.md item on making it public, which now mentions the link. IDLE is not degraded and shows no banner. Hard Rules 1, 6; ux rule 3.

It renders every shared component with invented data, each value parsed with its ledger schema, and its times follow the moment the page opens. Every build has it, so the end-to-end tests can open it, except the store build: `pnpm build:release` sets `ATTEST_RELEASE=1`, and wxt.config.ts `releaseEntrypoints` then leaves it out. The `dev` surface joins the logger's values (`ui.root_missing {surface}`). It drives the "axe-core E2E reports zero serious violations" done-when of 4.1 and the axe checks of 4.10. It runs only in the end-to-end tests and is never bundled. Hard Rule 9.
- 2026-10-09 [4.2] Grading contract v1.18 adds `finalFrom` to every receipt it does not finalise: the instant from which a later grading can make it FINAL, written by `grade()` in the offset of `evaluatedAt`. The popup's "checking, final from 14:48" must be a stored value (the Sprint 4 rule: the UI computes nothing), and no stored value held it. For VERIFIED it is `end(p,t) + graceMin`. For PARTIAL, NOT_FOUND, an OVER_SCOPE that rests on its candidate, and PENDING, it is the end of the grace; a PENDING receipt's own deadline is a backstop, while a grading past its grace with every source synced decides its grade. For the coverage-based UNVERIFIABLE it is the deadline `end(p,t) + finalizeUnverifiableHours`; for UNASKED, the earlier of its adapter sync instant and its deadline. No grade, state or reason changes. Replay compares it. A PENDING row stored before v1.18 has none until the next batch regrades it (at most 5 minutes), so the schema allows its absence, and the golden runner checks it on every receipt it grades. Five goldens pin its value, each worked by hand from the default policy: `03` 14:48, `14` the next day 14:03, `49` 14:35, `61` 14:33 and 14:41. Hard Rules 4, 12.
- 2026-10-09 [4.2] The popup's rows (src/ui/ReceiptRow.tsx, src/ui/receipt-row-labels.ts) put the grade chip first, then the claim in one line ("Email sent: priya@example.com"), then the agent. A PENDING receipt says "Checking — final from 14:48", and once that instant has passed, "Checking — waiting for a check after 14:48". It says "final from", not the sprint's "finalises by": whether it finalises then depends on the person's own tabs being checked, so it is a countdown, not a promise (ux rule 6). A PENDING receipt graded before the last batch says "No longer checked since HH:MM": a claim-less receipt that holds reverted overrides is never shown as "checking" forever (the Sprint 1 close carry-forward). An override shows its grade and reason; a superseded UNASKED receipt shows the core's sentence. An UNVERIFIABLE, OVER_SCOPE or NOT_FOUND receipt gives its first explaining reason in the core's words (src/core/reason-text.ts), so a calendar claim with Calendar never opened reads "Attest did not see Google Calendar during this claim's time window." An UNASKED row names the action and its signal count ("Sent-folder row, 14:03, to vendor@supplier.example (2 signals)"), its badge says "Likely agent: Muse", and its signals, each saying "likely", are one tap away under "Why likely: 2 signals". Page reads join the observation a claim-less receipt is about, by key. Hard Rules 5, 7; ux rules 1, 4, 5, 6.
- 2026-10-09 [4.2] What the popup loads. Its first read starts in a layout effect, as soon as the skeleton rows are in the page, not after Chrome paints them. It listens for `ledger.changed` only once that read has settled, in an effect that runs after the paint; the bus listener's module loads then, and one catch-up read follows, so a change announced before it listened is read too. Its start loads only what it reads. The shared ledger handle (src/ledger/shared.ts) and the last-import keys (src/ledger/agent-keys.ts; the file name corrected in the Sprint 4 close, tech review TECH-10) are modules of their own, re-exported where they were. src/ledger/views.ts reads by key itself instead of importing the repo, whose writers, policy and retention code ran at the popup's start before. A receipt's detail (src/ledger/detail.ts) loads when the side panel first opens one. The popup's reads run in parallel in one read transaction. "Open timeline" opens this window's side panel in the click itself (Chrome opens a side panel only in answer to a gesture) and closes the popup; "Open settings" opens the settings. A refusal is said and logged (`popup.side_panel_failed`, `popup.options_failed`, `popup.window_unknown`), as is a failed read (`popup.read_failed`) and a listener that did not load (`ui.listen_failed`). Hard Rule 6; ux rule 9.
- 2026-10-09 [4.2] How the paint gate is read and measured. The gate (tests/perf/popup-paint.perf.spec.ts, `pnpm test:perf`) builds the extension and runs it in Playwright's Chromium. It loads the popup five times over 5,000 receipts, each load a new document after one warming open, since Chrome keeps a page's compiled scripts as it does for a popup opened every day. It reads, from the popup's navigation start, its first paint (the skeleton rows, which ux rule 9 makes mandatory so that the popup paints before the ledger is read), the paint of the day (today's list), and the page's main-thread CPU time. The medians are 56 to 64 ms to first paint, 88 to 92 ms to the day's paint, and 70 to 82 ms of CPU. The gate holds the first paint under 100 ms. The day's paint is under 100 ms too, but with about a tenth of the budget to spare, against two fifths for the first paint. A paint is a moment the person sees, so it is wall time, which a test running beside it inflates: two parallel end-to-end workers pushed the first paint to 100 to 120 ms. So it is timed with the other perf gates, serially and in one process, never in the parallel end-to-end run. A `projects` split of the Playwright config was tried and dropped, because the mandatory-test guard pins that config to filter nothing. Most of the time is fixed, not the ledger's: with an empty ledger the day paints only 10 to 15 ms sooner, since every read is paged (204 rows, 4.0). The rest is loading Zod and Dexie with the page, opening the ledger and presenting a frame. `pnpm bench` records all three (`popup`: the gate is the median first paint, wall time, the statistic its gate test takes), and the Playwright trace of the measured runs is written to bench/traces/popup-paint.zip and committed with the results. Hard Rule 12; ux rule 9.
- 2026-10-09 [4.3] A person's mark on a receipt travels as one bus message, `receipt.event` (`{ receiptId, event: override | revert }`), which only an extension page may send, like `claims.import`. The service worker applies it: pages read the ledger but never write it, so the worker stays its one writer and stamps every event on its own clock. The handler runs the clock guard first, as every write does. src/ledger/user-events.ts then applies the event in one readwrite transaction over `receipts` and `meta`: read the receipt, call `writeUserEvent` with `notBefore` = `lastEventAt(receipt)`, and in `write` run `transition` with the `at` it passes and store the result. That is the Sprint 1 close's carry-forward exactly. Nothing is ever stamped with `nowIso()`. A refusal writes nothing and is answered with a code the panel says in words. `refused` covers the state machine's refusals, including a reason with no visible character. `not_found` and `failed` also exist. `evidence_taken` covers the revert of an override from FINAL to NOT_FOUND once another claim's receipt names one of its observations (read through the `*observationIds` index): the carry-forward allowed saying so or refusing, and refusing keeps every observation named once. A saved event is announced (`ledger.changed`: receipts) and logged as `receipt.overridden` (the receipt's id and the grade) or `receipt.reverted`; a refusal as `receipt.event_refused` (id, kind, code). The reason is the person's own words: stored on the receipt (§5.10 of docs/privacy-architecture.md already lists it), never logged. Hard Rules 4, 6, 10.

Its filters combine: the agents checked, the grades checked (every grade checked is no grade filter at all), and From and To (both days included). A change reads again from the top. Older receipts come a page at a time, 50, as the list's end scrolls into view, or with "Show older receipts" for the keyboard. When the service worker announces a change, the panel reads again as many receipts as it shows, so its list keeps its place, and the open detail too. A receipt's detail shows the row itself, then the request's hint, then the claim as Attest read it (every field, the precision of its time, the source and the extractor with its confidence). Then each piece of evidence with every stored field, first seen apart from stored. Then how it was graded: grade, rule, confidence, score, each signal by name, when it was graded, final at or final from, and every reason in the core's words. Then what the grading read: the grant in force (types, recipients, ceiling, declared access, accounts), the context (earlier spend; the likely agent, its score and signals), and every coverage entry, folded under its count. Last comes the person's mark. The mark needs Verified or Not found and a reason of at most 120 characters with a visible one, checked with the receipt's own override schema; an inline line says what is missing. It shows "You marked it Not found: “…”" with Attest's own grade beside it, and "Undo my mark". Focus moves to the detail's heading when it opens and back to its row's Details button when it closes. Sprint 4 rule (the UI computes nothing); Hard Rules 6, 7; ux rules 1, 4, 5, 7, 10.

The schema refuses `observedScopes`, `accounts` and `updatedAt`. The service worker keeps what Attest observed from the stored grant, read in the save's own readwrite transaction (src/ledger/grant-save.ts), so an options page read before a settings pass (3.7) can never put back the observed scopes that pass replaced. It stamps `updatedAt` there, at now or 1 ms after the last batch instant when the clock reads at or before it (`stampEventAt`, as an override is stamped), so a grant is in force only in batches after it and replay finds every grade (Hard Rule 4). Then it runs a batch. A batch grades only claims whose receipt is still PENDING, so a grant change regrades only the agent's open receipts. A FINAL receipt is never regraded (`regrade` refuses it): what a grant decided stays decided (docs/grading.md §3 Grant changes, §8). It logs `grant.saved` with the agent only (Hard Rule 10).
- 2026-10-09 [4.4] A saved grant is a stored fact. It raises the stored-fact count, so the batch after it grades even when no receipt is PENDING. Otherwise the skip of tech review TECH-2 would hold back the scope receipt of a narrower declared access until some unrelated fact arrived. It also raises the clock's high water, at the clock's now. The clock repair gains a step (src/ledger/clock-guard.ts step 7): every grant stamped after the later of now and the last batch instant + 1 ms is re-stamped there, its fields kept. Here is why. A batch ignores a grant stamped after its `evaluatedAt`, and the grants table holds one row per agent. Suppose a grant is saved while the clock ran ahead, its own batch never recorded (the worker stopped between the save and the batch, or the batch failed), and the clock comes back. The agent then has no grant in force, and every batch grading it throws `missing_grant` until real time catches up: grading stops for every agent. The step is logged as `ledger.clock_grants_restamped` with a count. Hard Rules 4, 6.
- 2026-10-09 [4.4] The form's rules (src/ui/grant-form.ts). **Recipients:** one per line; blank lines are skipped. A domain is lower-cased before repeats are dropped, so `Company.com` and `company.com` are one entry. An address is kept as written. Each entry must be a domain (labels of letters, digits and hyphens, then a top-level label of 2 to 63 letters) or an address (up to 64 characters without spaces or `@`, then `@` and a domain). There are at most 200 entries of at most 320 characters each (`SCHEMA_LIMITS`). **Spend ceiling:** a whole number in the currency's main unit, stored in its minor units (`CURRENCY_MINOR_UNITS`), up to `SCHEMA_LIMITS.AMOUNT_CENTS`; blank means no ceiling, and then no currency is stored. The sprint says "integer dollars → cents". A whole main unit carries that to every currency the schema knows, since a yen ceiling has no cents. The currency starts at the stored one, else USD. **Declared access:** one per line, at most 200 entries of at most 120 characters. When none was declared, it is seeded from the observed scopes, as the sprint asks. **Checking and saving:** every field is checked on Save. Each wrong field says what to fix beside it (`role=alert`), and the save is refused with "Not saved: fix the fields marked above." Each agent has its own Save. The grant reads in plain language above its fields (agents/ux_engineer.md rule 7), for example "Muse may: send email, create calendar events; write only to example.com; spend up to $50.00." A saved form says "Saved. It applies to this agent’s open receipts now, and never changes a final one."

Grading hashes the grant into every receipt's inputs, and a mute must change no grade and no hash. Grading never reads the mute; the alerts of 4.6 will. The options page reads grants and mutes itself (src/ui/data.ts `loadGrantSettings`, one read of the grants table, which holds at most one row per agent, and one meta `bulkGet`). The done-when asks that turning a reader off removes its content script's registration at runtime (`chrome.scripting.unregisterContentScripts`), and Chrome can unregister only a script registered at runtime. So both page readers (Gmail, Google Calendar) are now WXT `registration: 'runtime'` scripts, and the manifest lists no content script. The service worker registers each with `chrome.scripting.registerContentScripts`, on exactly its `OBSERVER_HOSTS`, at `document_idle`, top frame only, kept across sessions, while the person keeps it on (src/background/readers.ts). `scripting` gives no host access beyond `host_permissions`, which stay the two observer hosts; Attest uses it for nothing else. Its `executeScript` would reach only those two hosts, and no code calls it; the `attest/no-act` and fence lints still hold for the content scripts. - 2026-10-09 [4.5] Which readers are off is kept in `chrome.storage.local` (`attest.readersOff`, a list of reader ids), not in the ledger. It is a choice about which scripts run, not evidence, and the service worker reads it at every start, before it has any reason to open the ledger, to bring the registrations in line (`syncReaders`: register each reader that is on, update one registered with other details after an update, remove each that is off; one sync at a time). Every reader is on until turned off. A stored value that is not a list of readers reads as every reader off, logged as `readers.choice_invalid`: a choice that cannot be read is never taken as consent to read (Hard Rule 11). A tab opened before a reader was turned off keeps its script, so the service worker answers each message from that reader's observers with a new acknowledgement code, `reader_off`. Nothing of the message is stored and the observer is not DEGRADED; `postAcked` rejects with `BusError('reader_off')`, and the observer skeleton stops every observer of the tab, as it does when the extension context is gone. So "off" holds at once, even in a tab already open. The e2e launcher waits until the registrations match the stored choice (tests/e2e/support/extension.ts `waitForReaders`), so no test opens Gmail before its reader exists.

It is stored in ledger meta `retentionDays` (src/ledger/settings.ts), so an export carries it and Delete all data resets it. `purgeAtTrustedClock` reads it inside the purge's own transaction, so a window saved between two purges applies at the next daily purge. Saving does not purge at once: the page says the purge runs once a day. A stored value that is not one of the choices reads as 90, never as a shorter window. The telemetry choice is a disabled checkbox, off, saying "Coming in a later version" (Sprint 5 enables it). - 2026-10-09 [4.5] Export: the options page builds both files itself, from its own connection to the ledger, never asking the service worker, whose single-message export (`ui.query` `ledger.export`) stays for the test harness (Sprint 0 close, TECH-10). The ledger file (src/ledger/export-file.ts) reads every table and replay map in primary-key order in one read transaction, a page at a time (`EXPORT_PAGE_SIZE`, 500 keys, then those rows by key), and turns each page into JSON text and a Blob part before the next is read. Its bytes are `JSON.stringify(exportLedger(db), null, 2)` and a newline: each row is written as that call writes it at its depth, and a unit test compares the bytes at page sizes 1, 2, 3 and 500. The e2e compares the file with the worker's export after the Sprint 2 and 3 flows (the Gmail Sent list, a Calendar week, the §2 demo), the `exportedAt` line aside: two exports taken at two instants cannot share it. The comparison counts only when the worker's export read the same just before and just after the file, since an alarm's batch can change meta in between. The receipts CSV (src/ledger/export-csv.ts) is one line per receipt, newest first, paged through `receiptPage`: receipt facts, the action's type, time, counterparty and amount, but no override reason and no subject hint (free text; the JSON holds everything). Lines end in CRLF (RFC 4180), with no byte order mark. A cell beginning with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return is written after an apostrophe (OWASP CSV injection), and a cell with a comma, a quote or a line break is quoted.
- 2026-10-09 [4.5] Delete all data: an inline confirm in two more steps, as the install id reset. The person clicks Delete all data, then ticks "I understand that everything is deleted for good", and only then is Delete everything enabled. Focus starts on Cancel. The service worker (`ledger.wipe`) closes its connection and deletes the IndexedDB database (src/ledger/shared.ts `deleteLedger`); every open page's Dexie closes on Chrome's `versionchange` and reopens, empty, at its next read. It then clears `chrome.storage.local` (install id, flags, readers' choice), announces every table, and syncs the readers back on. The optional `idle` permission is the person's to remove in Chrome; it holds no data. The in-memory pass state is left as it is: it is safe to lose, and anything it still holds errs toward less coverage. The unit test checks every table and the storage empty afterwards, and a page's open connection reading the new ledger. The e2e checks the same on the built extension, allowing only the two bookkeeping keys an alarm's batch may write in between.

Several older tests had used `ledger.wipe` as their example of a type that does not exist; they now use `ledger.erase`. Attest uses it for one notification per FINAL NOT_FOUND, UNASKED or OVER_SCOPE receipt, and for nothing else. A notification is shown by Chrome and the operating system on this device; nothing is sent anywhere (Hard Rule 1). Because a notification can show on a locked screen, it names the grade as a noun, the agent and the action with its time, and never a counterparty, an amount or a subject (src/ui/alert-text.ts). Every UNASKED alert says "likely" (Hard Rule 7). The icon is the extension's own `icon/128.png`. Pinned by tests/unit/manifest-hosts.spec.ts and tests/e2e/built-bundle.spec.ts.
- 2026-10-09 [4.6] One per receipt, ever, is kept in the ledger (extension rule 3: nothing durable in memory). It is a new table, `alerts` (schema v9), keyed by receipt id with `state` indexed. The grading batch adds a `pending` row, inside its own transaction, for each receipt it wrote FINAL with an alerting grade and not superseded, unless the receipt has a row. A FINAL receipt is never regraded, so no later batch adds it again. After each batch, inside the batch queue, and at every start of the service worker (for a worker that stopped in between), `takeAlerts` settles the pending rows in one readwrite transaction before any notification is asked for. The states are `shown`, `muted` (meta `alertsMuted.<agent>`), `superseded`, `overridden` or `gone`. The order is at most once: a worker that stops after settling and before Chrome shows the notification loses that notification rather than showing it twice. A settled row is never shown again. A mute holds for what came while muted, even after unmuting. The `alerts` table is a ledger table: exported (`.default([])` for older exports), announced, and purged with its receipt. GradingDeps has an optional `alerts`; the shipped deps carry Chrome's, so a test that builds its own shows none. A rejected `chrome.notifications.create` is logged (`alerts.show_failed`) and not retried.
- 2026-10-09 [4.6] A click on an alert opens the side panel on its receipt. Chrome opens a side panel only in answer to a user action, so `sidePanel.open` is called at once, in the click's own turn, with `windows.WINDOW_ID_CURRENT` (the last focused window), before anything is awaited. When Chrome refuses, the side panel's page opens in a tab (`sidepanel.html#receipt=<id>`), which needs no permission. The receipt is named in `chrome.storage.session` (`attest.openReceipt`). The side panel reads it when it opens and while it is open, opens only a receipt id, and removes the entry, so each click opens one detail once. The alert is cleared. A click on a notification id that is not a receipt id does nothing. The click's routing is unit-tested with fakes: no end-to-end test can click an operating-system notification.

The side panel's first open-request hook then removed the key, the removal fired a change event, and the hook read again: an endless loop of microtasks, which no test timeout can interrupt. It hung the unit run. The hook now acts only on a value, and listens only for a string set. (2) Since 4.2, `pnpm test` reported two unhandled rejections and exited 1 with every test green: the popup shell, mounted in tests/unit/options/options-ui.spec.ts, read `.id` of the `undefined` window the fake browser's `windows.getCurrent` resolves. That test now gives the popup a window. The run is green and exits 0, and the pre-push hook depends on that. (3) The side panel's "Saved." after a mark lived in the mark form's own state, and the form gives way to "Your mark" as soon as the ledger reads the mark back, taking the line with it. The 4.3 end-to-end test saw it only while the event's answer beat the re-read; in 4.6 it lost every race. The line now lives in OverrideControl, keyed by receipt, so "Saved." (and "Undone.") stays after the switch and never shows under another receipt. A unit test reads the mark back and finds the line still there.
- 2026-10-09 [4.7] The weekly digest is a ledger record: a new table `digests` (schema v10), one row per week, keyed by the week's Monday (`YYYY-MM-DD`). The service worker builds it from the ledger on the digest alarm (src/ledger/digest.ts) and keeps it, so history can be browsed. The digest page reads the record and computes nothing: the sprint's rule that the UI is a view of the ledger. Every number on the page is a stored count, "4 of 6 checked claims verified" included. `checked` counts the agent's FINAL claim receipts Attest could check (Verified, Partial, Not found, Over scope), not Unverifiable ones, so the rate never includes claims nothing could check. With none checked, the page says "No claim could be checked" rather than showing a rate (Hard Rule 12). A receipt belongs to the week its `computedAt` (its last grading, which for a FINAL receipt is when it became final) falls in, read per agent through the `[agent+computedMs]` index. FINAL receipts are counted by grade, PENDING ones as still checking, the person's marks as marked, and superseded receipts not at all. Digests are exported and go in the retention purge once their week ended before the cutoff.
- 2026-10-09 [4.7] The week and the alarm follow the browser's clock: Monday 00:00 to the next Monday 00:00, built by calendar arithmetic (a week across a daylight saving change is 167 or 169 hours). A day the change starts at 01:00 starts there. The alarm is one-shot, at the next Monday 09:00 (`DIGEST_WEEKDAY`, `DIGEST_HOUR`). It is set again first thing each time it fires, so a failed digest never stops the weeks after it, and a 7-day periodic alarm can never drift an hour off 09:00. Chrome fires a missed alarm when the browser next starts. A late alarm makes the digest of the last whole week before it, the same week, replacing itself. Tested over 2,000 random instants in New York, Santiago, Lord Howe, Kiritimati, St John's and Tehran.
- 2026-10-09 [4.7] What a digest keeps of a notable receipt (FINAL NOT_FOUND, UNASKED, OVER_SCOPE; newest 200, and the total) is the action's type and time, the amount, the subject hint the ledger keeps, and the counterparty reduced to its domain when it is made: an address gives its domain, a domain stays, and a name gives nothing. So neither the page nor its share can hold an address or a name, whatever renders it. The coverage gaps are the days of the week no `gmail.sent` or `gcal.event` window reached. The page says "Calendar was not checked Tue–Thu.", not the sprint's "was never open": the ledger merges windows and keeps no record of each sync, and a window is exactly what Attest could check.

Nothing is sent anywhere. Times are written as stored, on the clock they were read on, so the text does not change with the viewer's time zone. Text that came from a page (hints, domains) has every markdown character escaped, so it can never become a link or a heading. The PNG the sprint mentions is made by the same redacted renderer as 4.8's receipt share (the `offscreen` document of 4.8) and lands with 4.8. A notification says a digest is ready (`attest.digest`; a click opens digest.html in a tab), and the popup has a "Weekly digest" button.
- 2026-10-09 [4.7] "Alarm fires in E2E (clock advanced)": the end-to-end helper (tests/e2e/support/digest.ts) moves the service worker's `Date.now` to the next Monday 09:30, sets the digest alarm to fire at once, waits for the digest to be in the ledger, and puts the clock back. The digest test checks the §2 demo's counts on the stored record and on the page. The mandatory no-leak test now makes the seeded session's digest the same way and searches its share as a new channel, `share`, both as written and with its markdown escapes removed. A seed with a hyphen is escaped in the markdown and would otherwise pass. Its negative control stores a digest whose hint is a seed with a hyphen, behind the ledger's back, and the collector catches it in the share.

An offscreen document is needed only to draw where there is no DOM, and that is the service worker, where no share is ever made. A share is made in the page that shows it, the side panel or the digest page, and that page has a canvas. So the PNG is drawn there (src/ui/share-png.ts), and the manifest's permissions stay as they were. The sprint's "`offscreen` permission justified" is met by not needing one.
- 2026-10-09 [4.8] What a shared receipt holds (src/ui/receipt-share.ts): the grade; the agent ("Likely agent" on an UNASKED receipt); its state (final, checking, or the person's mark's grade, never its reason); the claim's type and time (at its precision) with its counterparty's domain, amount and subject hint; why, in Attest's own words (codes and numbers only); and each piece of evidence with its source, time, domain, amount and hint. A counterparty, and a merchant, is reduced to its domain by one function shared with the digest (src/ledger/domain.ts): an address gives its domain, a domain stays, a name gives nothing. Never shown: the request text, an address, a name, the reason of a mark. Times are written as stored, page text is escaped, and the image draws the same lines, escapes removed. The redaction is tested over every receipt of every golden scenario (226 receipts across the 115 scenarios), and the test was checked to fail when the share held a raw counterparty.

It takes the page's light or dark palette (near-black ink on white, or near-white on near-black, both above 15:1), chosen from `prefers-color-scheme` when the share opens. "Legible in dark and light" is an end-to-end test on the built extension. It reads the shared image back pixel by pixel in both schemes and checks that the background is light or dark as the panel is, that text pixels cover enough of the card (over 0.5%), and that its contrast with the background exceeds 12:1, against the 4.5:1 text needs. One preview serves both shares (src/ui/SharePreview.tsx): the text in full, then the image (alt text "This receipt as an image" or "This week as an image"), then Copy text and Copy image (the clipboard, at the person's click), each saying what came of it. The end-to-end test (tests/e2e/auto-check.spec.ts) opens the tab on the built extension, whose manifest still asks for `storage`, `alarms`, `scripting` and `notifications` only (tests/e2e/built-bundle.spec.ts pins them), and asserts that the manifest has no `tabs`. To see the tab it opened, the test queries `chrome.tabs` by the Gmail address from the service worker; the Gmail host permission the readers already hold is what shows that address.
- 2026-10-09 [4.9] When a claim is due for Check Gmail for me (src/background/auto-check.ts): an `email.sent` claim whose receipt is PENDING, with no mark of the person's, 15 minutes (`graceMin` / 2) after Attest read it (its `observedAt`, which the service worker stamps when it stores the claim), when no `gmail.sent` coverage ping has synced since that instant. The sprint's "PENDING for graceMin/2" is counted from when the claim became PENDING, which is when Attest read it, not from the end of its window. A pasted claim is usually read long after its action, so counting from the window would make every pasted claim due at once, opening Gmail right after each paste, before the person could open it themselves. "No Gmail coverage" means no Sent list read since the claim was read. An open Gmail tab on the Sent folder reports at least every 5 minutes, so a tab already showing Attest the folder holds the auto-check back. A list read before the claim was read, even one inside its window, cannot settle it (docs/grading.md v1.14, `coverage.list_unsettled`), so it does not hold it back. Only the Sent list counts: a receipt pass (`gmail.receipt`) does not.
- 2026-10-09 [4.9] "Once per grace window" is two rules. After an open, no tab opens for `graceMin` (30 minutes). And a claim that was due when a tab opened is never due again, so each claim opens at most one tab, even when the person closes it unread or Gmail asks them to sign in. One tab serves every claim due at that moment. The open is kept in ledger meta (`autoCheckAt`, `autoCheckUntil`, `autoCheckClaims`, `autoCheckAgents`; extension rule 3: nothing durable in memory), written after the tab is created, so the popup never says a tab opened that did not. A service worker stopped between the two could open a second tab at the next batch, but only if the first was never read, since a read makes no claim due. A record later than the batch's clock means the clock went back, and it holds nothing back. Checks run one at a time.
- 2026-10-09 [4.9] "Pinned, visible, never background": the tab is created pinned and active in the normal window the person last used. While no window is open, or that window is minimised, no tab opens and nothing is recorded; the next batch, 5 minutes later, tries again. Nothing opens while the Gmail reader is off: the person chose not to have Gmail read, and the tab would show Attest nothing. The page opened is `https://mail.google.com/mail/u/0/#sent` (`AUTO_CHECK_URL`, the first account signed in), which the Gmail reader reads as it reads any Gmail tab. Attest never acts on it (Hard Rule 3).
- 2026-10-09 [4.9] The flag lives where src/config.ts already said: `chrome.storage.local` `attest.flags`, now a record of the flags the person set, each `true` or `false` (src/background/flags.ts). A stored value that is not such a record (another key, another value, a list) counts as every flag off, and is logged: a choice that cannot be read is never consent. Pages change it only through a new extension-page-only bus command, `flag.set` (`{ flag, on }`), answered with `SettingResult`. Its `flag` is one of `SETTABLE_FLAGS`, today `ATTEST_AUTO_CHECK_GMAIL` alone, so no page can turn on telemetry or the model through it. The toggle sits in the options page's Adapters, under the Gmail reader, and its text says what opens, when, how often, that it is the only page Attest opens, and that Attest only reads it. The Privacy section and docs/privacy-architecture.md §3 say it too. The popup says why the tab opened, for how many emails and whose, from the open until its 30 minutes end. Delete all data clears the flag and the record.
- 2026-10-09 [4.9] Test harness: every vendor end-to-end session now runs behind the dead proxy the mandatory network tests use (tests/e2e/support/session.ts). A tab the extension creates itself sends its first request before Playwright's route guard is attached to it. In the first runs of the auto-check test, that request reached the real mail.google.com from the empty test profile and was redirected to Google's sign-in page. No personal data was in that profile. With the proxy, such a request fails on the machine; the test then loads the tab again under the guard, where the demo's Sent page answers and the Gmail reader reads it. Requests to the local fixture server bypass the proxy, and every other test's requests were already answered or refused by the guard.

A merchant gives its domain only when it is written as one (`merchantDomain`, "Amazon.com"): a merchant is a business, never a person. A subject hint keeps its words, each address in it written as its domain and any other `@` word left out (`withoutAddresses`). - 2026-10-10 [4.close] The readers a person turned off and the flags they set are kept in ledger meta (`readerOff.<reader>`, `flag.<flag>`; src/ledger/consent.ts), no longer in `chrome.storage.local`, which a content script can write on every Chrome Attest supports: no script on a vendor page can turn a reader or Check Gmail for me back on. Only `SETTABLE_FLAGS` are read; any other flag reads as its default, whatever is stored. A value that cannot be read reads as the choice that reads and opens less (that reader off, that flag off), logged by key. The wipe also clears `chrome.storage.session` and takes down Attest's notifications (a failure logged, `ledger.wipe_notifications_failed`). Security review SEC-6; Hard Rules 2, 11.

- 2026-10-10 [5.0] The model's score enters a grade in one place (src/core/model-raise.ts; docs/grading.md §5.4, contract v1.19): after the best candidate is chosen and assigned on its deterministic floor score S, a model score m for that candidate raises its score to max(S, (1 − λ)·S + λ·m), and only when S is already at or above `thresholds.partial`. Assignment between claims never sees the model: a raise there could take evidence from another claim and lower that claim's grade, which the sprint's monotonicity rule forbids. Hard Rules 4, 5.
- 2026-10-10 [5.0] The model's weight λ is `features.modelScore`, 0.15 as the sprint sets it, and the loader caps it so the model alone never makes a candidate at the partial floor verified: λ·(1 − partial) < verified − partial (src/core/policy-checks.ts `modelIssues`; 0.7 under the shipped thresholds). The cap comes from the policy's own thresholds, so src/core holds no new number. With λ = 0.15 a candidate reaches VERIFIED through the model only from a floor of about 0.8235 with a model score near 1. Hard Rules 4, 5.
- 2026-10-10 [5.0] `modelScore` is never a pair feature: `features()` never applies it, the loader refuses it in `featuresByType` and `identityFeatures`, and a type's zero-sum check leaves it out. Without that, a type listing only `modelScore` would pass the check now that the model has a weight, and its claims would divide by a weight sum of 0 when graded. Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-10 [5.0] A claim's model scores travel in its grading context (`GradeContext.modelScores`: integer basis points, at most 500, one per observation, in id order so equal sets hash alike). The context is hashed into `inputsHash` and stored with each receipt's replay record, so replay applies exactly the scores the grading had; a receipt's `signals` show `modelScore` whenever the context holds one for its best candidate. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-10 [5.0] The policy stays at version 2: no key changed, only `features.modelScore`'s value. Its hash changes, so receipts graded after this update name the new policy, and earlier receipts replay under the policy each stored. With no model score in a context, which is every grading until 5.4 wires the model in, no grade changes.
- 2026-10-10 [5.0] src/core/schema.ts had reached its 400-line cap again with the context field: its length caps and branded primitives moved to src/core/schema-primitives.ts, which imports nothing from schema.ts and which schema.ts re-exports, so no caller changed (schema.ts: 363 lines). The no-magic-number exemption for `SCHEMA_LIMITS` moved with it to the new file, the same exemption for the same block. Tech review TECH-13 of Sprint 4.
- 2026-10-10 [5.0] The monotonicity property (tests/property/monotonicity.spec.ts) grades 5,000 random inputs, from the generator the determinism property uses (tests/property/grade-tuples.ts, now shared), with no model score, with every candidate's score at 0 and at 1. It holds that a score of 0 grades as none, that a score of 1 changes no rule but PARTIAL to VERIFIED, that NOT_FOUND and UNVERIFIABLE never change, and that the observation taken never changes; and it fails unless the inputs reach PARTIAL and the model moved some of them. Hard Rule 4.
- 2026-10-10 [5.1] What the advisory model sees (src/intel/input.ts): for the claim its type, normalised counterparty, subject hint, amount in minor units and the instant it is about; for each candidate its id, type, normalised counterparty, normalised merchant, subject hint, amount and instant, the fields the sprint lists and no others. A calendar claim's instant is its event start (`targetAt`), since its candidates are events at their start; any other claim's is `claimedAt`. A counterparty is an address in ASCII lower case or a name's tokens, as core reads it; a merchant is its `name()`. The currency is left out because the sprint's list leaves it out; the floor's `amountExact` already requires equal currencies before the model can raise anything. Hard Rule 2.
- 2026-10-10 [5.1] No request reaches the model: src/intel takes a claim and its candidates and nothing else, a claim holds no free text beyond its 80-character subject hint, and the input passes a strict schema, so a stored row's index fields and any field no schema names are dropped. A test double records the input for 20 claims and compares it with an independently written projection; one claim's request says "ignore previous instructions", and the same words in a stray field of its stored row never reach the model. Hard Rules 2, 4.
- 2026-10-10 [5.1] The prompt is a constant system prompt and one data block (src/intel/prompt.ts), and the system prompt says the block is data, not instructions. A subject hint is text an agent or a mail sender chose, so every `<` in the block's JSON is written `\u003c` (the same JSON value), and no text can close the block. Agents/intel_engineer.md rule 3.
- 2026-10-10 [5.1] A model's answer counts only when it is exactly `{scores: [{candidateId, score in [0, 1], rationaleHint?}]}` for candidates it was given, each scored once (src/intel/schema.ts `scoresOf`); a score becomes integer basis points. The rationale hint is checked to be one line of at most 80 characters and then dropped: Attest never shows, stores or logs it, so nothing a model writes reaches the person or the ledger. Hard Rules 4, 10.
- 2026-10-10 [5.1] One model call (src/intel/index.ts `modelScores`) gives scores or nothing and never throws: a provider that throws, an answer later than `INTEL_TIMEOUT_MS` (2 s, src/config.ts; the provider's signal is aborted), and an answer that is not one each give no score, logged as `intel.failed`, `intel.timeout` or `intel.answer_invalid` with the provider's id alone (`builtin` or `byo`), never the input, the answer or an error's text. A claim with no candidate asks nothing. Hard Rules 6, 10.
- 2026-10-10 [5.2] The built-in provider uses Chrome's Prompt API as its specification and Chrome's documentation give it on 2026-10-10 (founder's Chrome: 154): the global `LanguageModel`, Chrome 138 and later, in extension pages and service workers, with no manifest permission (the origin-trial permission it once needed has expired). `LanguageModel.availability()` resolves to `unavailable`, `downloadable`, `downloading` or `available`, and takes the same languages `create()` does; `create({initialPrompts, expectedInputs, expectedOutputs, signal, monitor})` puts the system prompt first; `prompt(text, {signal, responseConstraint})` holds the answer to a JSON Schema and returns text. A context with no `LanguageModel` is `no_api`, and a check that throws reads as `unavailable` (src/intel/providers/builtin.ts). Hard Rule 6.
- 2026-10-10 [5.2] Each built-in call creates its own session with the constant system prompt and English as its languages, prompts it once with the input's data block, holds the answer to the output's JSON Schema, and destroys the session: no session carries one claim's data into another's. The JSON Schema is written out by hand because the shipped build carries no zod JSON Schema code; a test holds it to `IntelOutput`. Text that is not JSON is passed on as text, which the call contract reads as no answer. Hard Rules 2, 4.
- 2026-10-10 [5.2] The options page's Model section turns Chrome's on-device model on or off (flag `ATTEST_MODEL`, now settable). The switch works only where Chrome says its model is `available`; otherwise it is disabled with one line saying why (no API, a computer that cannot run it, not downloaded yet, downloading). Turning it off always works. Where Chrome can download the model, a button does so; Chrome downloads only in answer to a click, so the download starts from the person's click alone, never from the service worker, with its progress shown. The section says the model runs on this computer, sees only a claim's kind, recipient, short subject, amount and time and the same of each possible match, and never decides a grade. Hard Rules 1, 4, 8.
- 2026-10-10 [5.2] A development page, entrypoints/model-check (in `DEV_ENTRYPOINTS`, so no release build holds it), scores ten invented check claims through the grading's own provider and call contract, with its 2-second limit, and lists each claim's parsed scores. It is how a person checks the real model on a Chrome that has it (MANUAL.md, Sprint 5); the tests run the same claims over a stand-in Prompt API. The options page's Model placeholder and its "Available in a later version" components are gone, since nothing uses them now.
- 2026-10-10 [5.3] The person's own key may be for Anthropic or OpenAI, each at its official endpoint and nowhere else (src/config.ts `BYO_VENDORS`): Anthropic's Messages API (`https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages`, model `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`, `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`, and `anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access: true`, which Anthropic requires of a call made from a browser) or OpenAI's Chat Completions API (`https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions`, model `gpt-4o-mini`, a JSON-object answer). Small, fast models, because each call has 2 seconds. The request is the constant system prompt and the input's data block, with at most 1,024 tokens of answer; no cookie, no referrer, no cache. A refusal is an `IntelError` named by its status alone (`unauthorized`, `rate_limited`, `server`, `refused`), and a reply not in its vendor's shape is a `bad_reply`. Hard Rules 1, 4, 10.
- 2026-10-10 [5.3] The call needs no new permission: it is a plain cross-origin request, which both vendors allow from a browser, so the manifest is unchanged (Hard Rule 8). A vendor's host is contacted only while the person's own key is on with that vendor; it is never in `NETWORK_ALLOWLIST`, which holds what may be contacted whatever the person chose, so the mandatory network session, run with the key off, still allows no host. Hard Rules 1, 8.
- 2026-10-10 [5.3] The key lives in `chrome.storage.local`, as the sprint says, but closed to content scripts: the service worker calls `setAccessLevel({accessLevel: 'TRUSTED_CONTEXTS'})` (Chrome 102 and later) before it saves a key, and at every install, update and browser start, so a script on a Gmail or Calendar page can read nothing in that area. No content script used it. The key and its vendor are stored apart (`attest.byoKey`, `attest.byoVendor`), and the options page reads only the vendor, written only with a key and removed with it. The key is never logged, never in the ledger and so never in an export; `byo.forget` and Delete all data delete it. Hard Rules 2, 10.
- 2026-10-10 [5.3] Two page commands carry it (src/bus/page-commands.ts): `byo.save`, with the vendor and the key, refused by its schema unless the key is letters, digits, `-` and `_`, 20 to 300 characters (a refusal is logged by message type alone); and `byo.forget`, which deletes both and turns the person's own key off. The switch is the settable flag `ATTEST_MODEL_BYO`, which the options page enables only once a key is saved. Hard Rule 10.
- 2026-10-10 [5.3] When both the on-device model and the person's own key are on, the on-device model is used whenever Chrome has it ready, and nothing is sent: the choice that keeps data on the computer wins, and the options page says so. The grading wires this in (5.4). Hard Rule 1.
- 2026-10-10 [5.3] The mandatory secret-seed test now saves an invented key through the options page and turns the person's own key on. The key must sit in exactly its own slot of `chrome.storage.local`, and every channel is searched for it with that one slot taken out; the negative control plants it under another storage key and in the console, and both are caught, so the slot taken out is that slot alone. Hard Rules 2, 10.
- 2026-10-10 [5.3] The provider's network behaviour is tested with `fetch` stubbed: one POST to exactly its vendor's endpoint, the key only in that vendor's auth header, and a body that is exactly the vendor's request around the allowlisted data block. Seen at the network level, through the session's route guard, it comes with 5.4, when grading first calls it. Until then nothing in the service worker imports the provider, and the built-bundle check's allowance for its one `fetch` is added with that wiring.
